    3.1  What is Argo CD? — The Heart of GitOps
         - "Argo CD watches your Git repo and syncs it to your cluster"
         - Core concepts: Application, AppProject, Repository, Cluster
         📖 KEY TERM — Application, per Argo CD's official Core Concepts
            docs: "a group of Kubernetes resources as defined by a
            manifest... a Custom Resource Definition (CRD)." Of these four
            concepts, only Application and AppProject are real CRDs you
            can `kubectl get -n argocd`. Repository and Cluster are NOT
            CRDs — both are plain Kubernetes Secrets labeled
            `argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type: repository` (or `cluster`) in
            the argocd namespace. Confirm it yourself once Ch. 4 connects
            a repo: `kubectl get secrets -n argocd -l
            argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type=repository`.
         🧠 MENTAL MODEL — AppProject is a locked filing-cabinet drawer:
            it whitelists which Git repos, destination clusters/
            namespaces, and resource Kinds are even eligible to be
            referenced. An Application is one labeled folder inside that
            drawer — one specific repo+path pointed at one specific
            destination. Every Application belongs to exactly one
            AppProject; the built-in `default` project ships wide open
            (`*` for source, destination, and Kinds), which is why 3.4's
            easypj-dev Application doesn't question `project: default` —
            you lock this drawer down for real in Chapter 5.
         - Argo CD architecture: API Server, Repository Server, Application Controller
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — this trio is not the full install. Argo CD's own
            architecture docs single out exactly these three as the core
            "brain" of the system; the other four pods you'll meet in 3.3
            (redis, dex-server, applicationset-controller,
            notifications-controller) are supporting processes, not core
            architecture. An exam question asking you to "name Argo CD's
            core architecture" wants these 3 — answering with all 7 pods
            is technically true but not what's being tested.
         [ARCHITECTURE DIAGRAM: Argo CD components]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD ARCHITECTURE                                      │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────┐ │
         │  │  Git Repo    │    │ Argo CD API  │    │  Kubernetes  │ │
         │  │ (easypj-k8s) │◀──▶│   Server     │───▶│   Cluster    │ │
         │  └──────────────┘    └──────┬───────┘    └──────────────┘ │
         │                             │                              │
         │                    ┌────────┴─────────┐                   │
         │                    │  Application     │                   │
         │                    │  Controller      │                   │
         │                    │  (reconcile loop)│                   │
         │                    └──────────────────┘                   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.2  Installing Argo CD on Your Cluster
         - Step-by-step: kubectl create namespace → apply install manifest
         - Verifying the installation: kubectl get pods -n argocd
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — why plain `kubectl apply` chokes on this
            install: client-side apply stashes the ENTIRE previous
            manifest as JSON inside the `kubectl.kubernetes.io/
            last-applied-configuration` annotation to compute 3-way
            diffs. Kubernetes caps the combined size of all of an
            object's annotations at 262,144 bytes — and the
            ApplicationSet CRD's OpenAPI schema alone is large enough to
            blow that budget. `--server-side` never writes that
            annotation; it tracks per-field ownership via `managedFields`
            instead, so the cap doesn't apply. `--force-conflicts` is a
            separate, second flag — it tells kubectl to take ownership of
            fields even if another field-manager claims them, which
            matters the moment you re-run install or switch tooling.
            One real consequence: re-running the install command (an
            upgrade, say) with `--force-conflicts` will silently
            overwrite any manifest-defined field you hand-edited after
            the first install — `affinity`, `env`, `probes`, and
            similar. Fields the manifest never sets at all (resource
            requests/limits you added yourself, `tolerations`) survive
            untouched, since ownership only applies to fields the
            manifest actually declares.
         - Accessing Argo CD UI (port-forward vs. LoadBalancer vs. Ingress)
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — port-forward (this chapter's method) sidesteps this
            entirely, but the moment you swap in a real Ingress for
            easypj's staging/prod Argo CD, TLS termination matters. If
            your Ingress controller terminates TLS (the common case —
            Contour, Traefik, an AWS ALB, or nginx without
            ssl-passthrough) and argocd-server's own TLS stays enabled,
            you get a redirect loop or HTTP 307s: the Ingress sends plain
            HTTP to the pod, argocd-server tries to bounce it to HTTPS,
            forever. Fix: add `--insecure` to the argocd-server
            Deployment's args, or set `server.insecure: "true"` in the
            argocd-cmd-params-cm ConfigMap. This is a different
            `--insecure` from the CLI login flag in [I] Gotcha 1 — that
            one lives on the client, this one on the server.
         - Argo CD CLI installation and login
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — the `admin` superuser from Steps 4-6 below is
            meant to be temporary. Once real users authenticate via SSO
            (argocd-dex-server, met in 3.3), teams set
            `admin.enabled: "false"` in the argocd-cm ConfigMap to
            disable local admin login entirely — a locked-out account
            can't be phished or brute-forced. Confirm SSO + RBAC actually
            cover every operation you need before flipping this switch;
            there's no undo without cluster access to flip it back.
         📖 KEY TERM — `argocd login` writes a local CLI context (server
            address + auth token) to `~/.config/argocd/config`, the same
            way `kubectl config` tracks clusters. Logging into a second
            Argo CD instance adds a second context; `argocd context`
            lists them and lets you switch without re-authenticating.
         [CODE BLOCKS: Full install commands with expected outputs]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD — COMPLETE INSTALLATION                           │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Step 1: Create namespace                                │
         │  kubectl create namespace argocd                           │
         │                                                            │
         │# Step 2: Install Argo CD (non-HA version for lab)          │
         │kubectl apply -n argocd --server-side --force-conflicts -f \│
         │  https://raw.githubusercontent.com/argoproj/argo-cd/stable/│
         │  manifests/install.yaml                                    │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Step 3: Wait for all pods to be ready                   │
         │  kubectl wait --for=condition=Ready pods --all \           │
         │    -n argocd --timeout=300s                                │
         │  kubectl get pods -n argocd                                │
         │  # Expected:                                               │
         │  # NAME                                  READY  STATUS     │
         │  # argocd-application-controller-0       1/1    Running    │
         │  # argocd-applicationset-controller-...  1/1    Running    │
         │  # argocd-dex-server-...                 1/1    Running    │
         │  # argocd-notifications-controller-...   1/1    Running    │
         │  # argocd-redis-...                      1/1    Running    │
         │  # argocd-repo-server-...                1/1    Running    │
         │  # argocd-server-...                     1/1    Running    │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Step 4: Get initial admin password                      │
         │  kubectl -n argocd get secret argocd-initial-admin-secret \│
         │    -o jsonpath="{.data.password}" | base64 -d; echo        │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Step 5: Access UI (port-forward — simplest for lab)     │
         │  kubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443 │
         │  # Open https://localhost:8080 (accept self-signed cert)   │
         │  # Username: admin  Password: (from step 4)                │
         │                                                            │
         │# Step 6: Install CLI and login                             │
         │argocd login localhost:8080 --username admin \              │
         │  --password $(kubectl -n argocd get secret \               │
         │  argocd-initial-admin-secret \                             │
         │  -o jsonpath="{.data.password}" | base64 -d) \             │
         │  --insecure                                                │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Step 7: Change admin password (SECURITY!)               │
         │  argocd account update-password                            │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.3  Argo CD Core Components Walkthrough
         - argocd-server: API + UI
         - argocd-repo-server: Clones Git repos, generates manifests
         - argocd-application-controller: The reconciliation engine
         - argocd-applicationset-controller: Generates Applications from templates
         - argocd-redis: Cache layer
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — "cache layer" undersells it. Redis holds (1)
            git-refs (`git ls-remote` results, ~3min default TTL, so
            repo-server doesn't re-resolve `main` on every poll), (2)
            rendered manifests keyed by repo+commit-SHA+path+params — e.g.
            easypj's `overlays/dev` output at a given commit — with a much
            longer ~24h TTL, and (3) each Application's last-computed
            state. Kill argocd-redis and Argo CD still works; it just
            re-clones and re-renders on every reconcile instead of
            reusing a cached result for an unchanged commit.
         🔑 DAILY-WORK — the default install's argocd-redis is not
            wide open: it requires password auth out of the box. The
            password lives in the `argocd-redis` Secret's `auth` key
            in the argocd namespace — you'll need it if you ever
            `redis-cli` into the pod directly to debug a caching issue
            (rare, but it happens when an easypj Application shows
            stale rendered manifests after a Git force-push).
         - argocd-dex-server: OIDC/SSO authentication
         📖 KEY TERM — Dex is optional, not a required hop. Argo CD
            supports SSO two ways: point `oidc.config` in argocd-cm
            directly at a modern OIDC provider (Okta, Auth0, Keycloak —
            skips argocd-dex-server entirely), or route through Dex
            (`dex.config`) when you need a connector Dex bridges for
            you — SAML, LDAP, or GitHub/GitLab org-and-team-to-
            group-claim mapping — protocols plain OIDC doesn't speak. A
            default lab install still runs the argocd-dex-server pod even
            with SSO unconfigured; it just sits idle until dex.config is
            set.
         - argocd-notifications-controller: Slack/Email/Webhook alerts
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — subscriptions are per-Application and
            self-service, not a global switch. A team owning easypj adds
            one annotation to opt their own Application in:
            `notifications.argoproj.io/subscribe.on-sync-succeeded.slack:
            <channel>`. No cluster-admin access to argocd-notifications-cm
            needed — any user who can edit their own Application's
            annotations can wire up their own Slack alerts.
         [COMPONENT DIAGRAM: How they interact]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD — COMPONENT INTERACTION                           │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────────┐     ┌──────────────┐  │
         │  │  USER    │────▶│ argocd-server│────▶│    REDIS     │  │
         │  │(UI/CLI)  │     │  (API + UI)  │     │   (Cache)    │  │
         │  └──────────┘     └──────┬───────┘     └──────┬───────┘  │
         │                         │                     │           │
         │                         ▼                     │           │
         │                ┌────────────────┐            │           │
         │                │ argocd-repo-   │◀───────────┘           │
         │                │    server      │    (cache Git clones)   │
         │                │                │                         │
         │                │ 1. Clones Git  │                         │
         │                │ 2. Runs Helm/  │                         │
         │                │    Kustomize   │                         │
         │                │ 3. Returns     │                         │
         │                │    YAML        │                         │
         │                └───────┬────────┘                         │
         │                        │                                  │
         │                        ▼                                  │
         │                ┌────────────────────┐                     │
         │                │ argocd-application-│                     │
         │                │    controller      │                     │
         │                │                    │                     │
         │                │ RECONCILE LOOP:    │                     │
         │                │ Every 3 minutes    │                     │
         │                │ (by default):      │                     │
         │                │ 1. Get desired     │                     │
         │                │    from repo-server│                     │
         │                │ 2. Get actual      │                     │
         │                │    from K8s API    │                     │
         │                │ 3. If diff → sync  │                     │
         │                └────────┬───────────┘                     │
         │                         │                                  │
         │                         ▼                                  │
         │                ┌────────────────────┐                     │
         │                │ argocd-applicationset-│                  │
         │                │    controller       │                     │
         │                │                    │                     │
         │                │ Generates multiple  │                     │
         │                │ Applications from   │                     │
         │                │ templates (List,    │                     │
         │                │ Git, Cluster gen.)  │                     │
         │                └────────────────────┘                     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         📖 KEY TERM — the diagram's "every 3 minutes" is the exam-friendly
            rounded number. The precise mechanism (argocd-cm ConfigMap):
            `timeout.reconciliation: 120s` (base interval, also exposed as
            the `--app-resync` flag, default 120) + `timeout.reconciliation.
            jitter: 60s` (randomization so many Applications don't all poll
            in the same instant) — worst case ~180s, hence "~3 minutes."
            Real-time Kubernetes watch events and Git webhooks can trigger
            a reconcile sooner; this timer is only the fallback ceiling.

    3.4  First Argo CD Application — Deploy easypj
         - Creating your first Application manifest
         📖 KEY TERM — `targetRevision` accepts a branch name, tag, or
            exact commit SHA. Leaving it unset (or set to `HEAD`) makes
            Argo CD track the repo's default branch symbolically —
            re-resolving HEAD -> latest commit on every reconcile, and
            surviving a default-branch rename. The walkthrough below pins
            `targetRevision: main` explicitly, the safer habit for a real
            environment: HEAD-tracking is convenient for a scratch dev
            Application, but pinning a branch/tag is what you want once
            "dev" needs to mean something specific and reviewable.
         - Connecting Argo CD to your Git repository
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the walkthrough points `repoURL` straight at a
            GitHub URL with zero prior setup, and that's fine because a
            PUBLIC repo needs no credentials — repo-server just clones it
            anonymously. The moment easypj-k8s goes private, that same
            manifest fails with a clone/auth error until you first
            register credentials — `argocd repo add <url> --username <u>
            --password <token>` or an SSH deploy key — which is exactly
            the Repository Secret from 3.1's KEY TERM. "Connecting to
            your Git repository" is implicit (anonymous clone) for public
            repos and explicit (a Secret) for private ones.
         - Syncing: Manual sync vs. Auto-sync
         - Watching the magic: easypj appears in your cluster!
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Step 1 (`kubectl apply -f
            easypj-dev-app.yaml`) is a completely ordinary CRD create —
            nothing argocd-specific about the verb. The Application
            object now lives as a resource IN the cluster, in the argocd
            namespace, which is exactly why Argo CD can (and later
            chapters will) manage Applications the same GitOps way it
            manages easypj itself: commit the Application YAML to Git
            too, and let a parent Application sync it — the "app of
            apps" pattern. You're one small step from that here; this
            chapter just applies it by hand once.
         [HANDS-ON: Complete walkthrough with YAML and screenshots]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  FIRST ARGO CD APPLICATION — easypj                        │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Create the Application YAML:                            │
         │  cat > easypj-dev-app.yaml <<EOF                           │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
         │  kind: Application                                         │
         │  metadata:                                                 │
         │    name: easypj-dev                                        │
         │    namespace: argocd                                       │
         │  spec:                                                     │
         │    project: default                                        │
         │    source:                                                 │
         │      repoURL: https://github.com/YOUR-USER/easypj-k8s     │
         │      targetRevision: main                                  │
         │      path: overlays/dev                                    │
         │    destination:                                            │
         │      server: https://kubernetes.default.svc               │
         │      namespace: easypj-dev                                 │
         │    syncPolicy:                                             │
         │      automated:                                            │
         │        prune: true                                         │
         │        selfHeal: true                                      │
         │      syncOptions:                                          │
         │        - CreateNamespace=true                              │
         │  EOF                                                       │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Apply it:                                               │
         │  kubectl apply -f easypj-dev-app.yaml                      │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Watch the sync:                                         │
         │  argocd app get easypj-dev                                 │
         │  # STATUS: OutOfSync → Syncing → Synced → Healthy          │
         │                                                            │
         │  # What happens:                                           │
         │  # 1. Argo CD detects new Application                     │
         │  # 2. Repo-server clones https://github.com/.../easypj-k8s│
         │  # 3. Reads overlays/dev/ path                             │
         │  # 4. Runs kustomize build                                 │
         │  # 5. Creates namespace easypj-dev                         │
         │  # 6. Applies deployment, service, configmap               │
         │  # 7. Health check: deployment is healthy                  │
         │  # 8. App status: Synced ✓  Healthy ✓                      │
         │                                                            │
         │  # Port-forward to access easypj:                          │
         │  kubectl port-forward -n easypj-dev svc/easypj 8000:80    │
         │  # Open http://localhost:8000 — you should see jobpost!    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.5  Argo CD Sync Strategies and Policies
         - Automatic sync vs Manual sync
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — automated ≠ automatically retried. Per Argo CD's own
            docs: "Automatic sync will not reattempt a sync if the
            previous sync attempt against the same commit-SHA and
            parameters had failed." If easypj-dev's automated sync fails
            (say, a bad manifest), Argo CD will NOT keep hammering it
            every reconcile — it waits for either a new commit or a
            manual `argocd app sync` before retrying that same state.
            Configure `retry` (limit + backoff) under `syncPolicy` if you
            want automatic retries on transient failures.
         - Prune resources (delete things not in Git)
         - Self-heal (auto-correct drift)
         📖 KEY TERM — self-heal is not the same clock as the ~3-min
            reconcile safety-net in 3.3's KEY TERM. When `selfHeal: true`
            is set, an out-of-band change (a `kubectl edit` on a resource
            easypj-dev owns) is caught by the live watch-based cache
            almost immediately, then Argo CD waits a short, separate
            `--self-heal-timeout-seconds` window (5s by default) before
            re-syncing — enough of a debounce to avoid fighting another
            controller's own legitimate writes, not so long a human
            watching the dashboard would call it slow. That 5s applies
            to the first self-heal attempt; if easypj-dev keeps
            drifting (e.g. a controller repeatedly reverting your fix),
            Argo CD backs off exponentially on each subsequent retry —
            `--self-heal-backoff-timeout-seconds` (2s initial) ×
            `--self-heal-backoff-factor` (3) up to a
            `--self-heal-backoff-cap-seconds` ceiling (300s) — so a
            flapping resource doesn't hammer the API server every 5
            seconds forever.
         - Sync waves and hooks
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — waves run in ascending order (negative
            numbers allowed, unannotated resources default to wave `0`),
            and Argo CD won't start wave N+1 until every resource in wave
            N is both Synced AND Healthy (plus a small fixed delay
            between waves so controllers have time to react). Hooks
            (PreSync/Sync/Skip/PostSync/SyncFail/PreDelete/PostDelete,
            via `argocd.argoproj.io/hook: <Type>`) give coarse phase
            ordering; `argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "<int>"` gives fine
            ordering inside a phase. Full precedence: phase, then wave
            (lowest first), then resource kind, then name. `Skip` tells
            Argo CD not to apply that manifest at all (useful for a
            template-only resource); `PreDelete` runs before an
            Application's resources are deleted, `PostDelete` after —
            the delete-side mirror of PreSync/PostSync. Pair any hook
            with `argocd.argoproj.io/hook-delete-policy:
            HookSucceeded|HookFailed|BeforeHookCreation` to control
            whether the hook resource (e.g. easypj's migration Job)
            gets cleaned up automatically after it runs.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — a wave that never turns Healthy blocks every wave
            after it, forever — there's no timeout that skips a stuck
            wave. easypj's DB-migration Job (`python manage.py migrate`,
            run as a PreSync hook) hanging permanently stalls the
            Deployment sitting at wave 1, with no automatic escalation —
            you have to intervene by hand (fix or delete/terminate the
            Job) to unblock the rest of the sync.
         [DECISION TABLE: When to use which sync strategy]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SYNC STRATEGY DECISION TABLE                              │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌────────────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────────┐  │
         │  │ SCENARIO       │AUTO-SYNC?│ PRUNE?   │ SELF-HEAL?   │  │
         │  ├────────────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┤  │
         │  │ DEV environment │   ✅ YES │   ✅ YES │    ✅ YES     │  │
         │  │ (fast feedback) │          │          │              │  │
         │  ├────────────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┤  │
         │  │ STAGING         │   ✅ YES │   ✅ YES │    ✅ YES     │  │
         │  │ (auto-deploy    │          │          │              │  │
         │  │  after tests)   │          │          │              │  │
         │  ├────────────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┤  │
         │  │ PRODUCTION      │   ✅ YES │ ⚠️ CAREFUL│   ✅ YES    │  │
         │  │ (manual approve │   BUT    │ Or manual │              │  │
         │  │  before sync)   │  MANUAL  │ prune     │              │  │
         │  ├────────────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┤  │
         │  │ DATABASE MIGR.  │   ❌ NO  │   ❌ NO   │    ❌ NO     │  │
         │  │ (needs PreSync  │ MANUAL   │ MANUAL   │  MANUAL      │  │
         │  │  hook + backup) │          │          │              │  │
         │  ├────────────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┤  │
         │  │ CRITICAL INFRA  │   ❌ NO  │   ❌ NO   │   ⚠️ ALERT   │  │
         │  │ (RBAC, netpol)  │ MANUAL   │ MANUAL   │  ONLY         │  │
         │  └────────────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────────┘  │
         │                                                            │
         │  PRO TIP: Start with manual sync, observe, then enable    │
         │  automated. Progressive trust-building!                   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    3.6  Sync Options Beyond Prune and CreateNamespace
         - The full syncOptions catalog easypj's real environments will need
         📖 KEY TERM — 3.4 and 3.5 already cover CreateNamespace=true and
            Prune=false; Argo CD's official sync-options list has five more
            worth knowing before you hit them in production:
            - `Replace=true` — swaps `kubectl apply` for `kubectl replace`
              (or create) per-resource, for objects apply can't handle
              cleanly (docs: "In some cases `kubectl apply` is not
              suitable").
            - `ServerSideApply=true` — opts a specific Application (or one
              resource, via the `argocd.argoproj.io/sync-options`
              annotation) into Kubernetes server-side apply for the sync
              itself — independent from the `--server-side` flag you used
              to *install* Argo CD in 3.2.
            - `ApplyOutOfSyncOnly=true` — syncs only resources Argo CD has
              actually flagged OutOfSync, skipping a full re-apply of
              everything else in the Application; useful once easypj's
              manifest set gets large.
            - `Validate=false` — the sync-time equivalent of `kubectl
              apply --validate=false`, for objects that fail schema
              validation for reasons unrelated to correctness.
            - `PruneLast=true` — official docs: "Resource pruning happens
              as a final, implicit wave of a sync operation, after other
              resources have been deployed and become healthy" — set this
              explicitly if you need pruning to always be the last thing
              that happens, not just the default wave-ordering behavior.
            - `FailOnSharedResource=true` — fails the sync outright if a
              resource is already owned by a different Application,
              instead of silently taking it over. Worth setting once more
              than one team can create Applications against the same
              cluster.
            - `RespectIgnoreDifferences=true` — makes `spec.ignoreDifferences`
              apply during the sync itself, not just status diffing.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — easypj's DB-migration Job from 3.5's GOTCHA is a
            good candidate for `ApplyOutOfSyncOnly=true` at the
            Application level once you have more than a handful of
            manifests: it keeps routine syncs from re-touching resources
            that haven't changed.

    3.7  Installation Profiles — Namespace-Scoped, Core, and High Availability
         - 3.2 installed the standard, single-cluster profile — Argo CD
           ships three more, each a different manifest URL
         📖 KEY TERM — the same `stable` branch that gave you 3.2's
            `install.yaml` also serves:
            - `.../manifests/namespace-install.yaml` — same components,
              but "requires only namespace level privileges (does not
              need cluster roles)." No ClusterRoleBinding — install this
              when you don't have cluster-admin.
            - `.../manifests/core-install.yaml` — "primarily used to
              deploy Argo CD in headless mode": no API server, no UI,
              no SSO, no multi-cluster support. Use `argocd login --core`
              to drive it — the CLI talks straight to the Kubernetes API,
              never to an argocd-server pod, because there isn't one.
            - `.../manifests/ha/install.yaml` and
              `.../manifests/ha/namespace-install.yaml` — the
              production-scale profile, "tuned for high availability and
              resiliency."
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — HA is not a flag, it's a different manifest, and it
            has a hard prerequisite: "The HA installation will require at
            least three different nodes due to pod anti-affinity rule in
            the specs." Fewer than 3 schedulable nodes and HA pods will
            sit Pending forever. HA also "runs Redis in HA mode" (Redis
            + Sentinel, pre-configured for exactly 3 redis
            servers/sentinels — not tunable to 5 without editing the
            manifest), scales `argocd-server` to 3+ replicas, and scales
            `argocd-application-controller`'s StatefulSet replica count
            for sharded reconciliation. The repo-server isn't
            multi-replica by default under HA — its parallelism is
            tuned via `--parallelismlimit` instead.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for easypj's lab/dev cluster, 3.2's plain
            `install.yaml` is correct. The day easypj-in-prod's Argo CD
            becomes a shared, org-wide control plane serving multiple
            teams, that's the trigger to re-install against
            `ha/install.yaml` on a 3+ node cluster — not before, since HA
            components consume meaningfully more cluster resources for
            no benefit on a single-tenant lab.
         - Kustomize-based installs (for teams patching the upstream
           manifest rather than editing it in place) are also officially
           supported: reference the manifest URL as a remote Kustomize
           resource and layer patches on top, rather than hand-editing
           downloaded YAML.

    3.8  Configuring TLS Certificates for argocd-server
         - Moving past the self-signed cert 3.2's `--insecure` flag
           sidesteps
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — "By default, and without further
            configuration, these endpoints will be set up to use an
            automatically generated, self-signed certificate." To swap
            in a real one, create a Secret named exactly
            `argocd-server-tls` holding PEM data under the `tls.crt` and
            `tls.key` keys:
            ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
            │ kubectl create -n argocd secret tls argocd-server-tls    │
            │   --cert=/path/to/cert.pem                                │
            │   --key=/path/to/key.pem                                  │
            └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            Argo CD checks three sources in priority order: (1) the
            `argocd-server-tls` secret — the recommended, current
            method; (2) the `argocd-secret` secret — deprecated, still
            honored for back-compat; (3) the auto-generated self-signed
            fallback if neither exists. Unlike most Argo CD config
            changes, this one is hot-reloaded: "Argo CD will pick up
            changes to the `argocd-server-tls` secret automatically and
            will not require restarting" the pod.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for easypj's staging/prod Ingress (the exact
            scenario 3.2's Ingress GOTCHA already covers), the choice is
            binary: either terminate TLS at the Ingress and set
            `server.insecure: "true"` (argocd-server serves plaintext
            behind the Ingress), or keep argocd-server's own TLS on and
            configure `ssl-passthrough` at the Ingress so it terminates
            at the pod using this real certificate — never both, and
            never neither (that's the redirect-loop GOTCHA in 3.2).

    3.9  Upgrading Argo CD Safely
         - Same server-side-apply command from 3.2, pointed at a version
           tag instead of `stable`
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Argo CD follows semver-ish upgrade rules:
            patch releases (e.g. v3.2.0 → v3.2.3) introduce no breaking
            changes; minor releases (v3.2.x → v3.4.x) "might introduce
            minor changes with a workaround" — check the upgrade guide
            for every intermediate minor version, not just the jump;
            major releases "introduce backward incompatible behavior
            changes" and warrant a disaster-recovery backup first. The
            upgrade command is identical to 3.2's install, just with a
            pinned tag replacing `stable`:
            ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
            │ kubectl apply -n argocd --server-side --force-conflicts │
            │   -f https://raw.githubusercontent.com/argoproj/         │
            │      argo-cd/<version>/manifests/install.yaml            │
            └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
            (swap `manifests/install.yaml` for `manifests/ha/install.yaml`
            if you're running 3.7's HA profile). Docs explicitly warn:
            "Even though some releases require only image change it is
            still recommended to apply whole manifests set" — never
            hand-patch just the image tag and skip the rest of the
            manifest, since CRDs/RBAC can change between versions too.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — never skip straight from an old minor version to a
            far-future one without reading every intermediate upgrade
            guide (the docs publish a dedicated guide per version pair,
            v1.0→v1.1 through the current release) — a config key or CRD
            field renamed two minor versions ago can silently break
            easypj's Applications if you leapfrog past its removal
            notice.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — this is why 3.2's install command pins nothing
            (`stable`) for the lab, but production teams pin an exact
            tag (e.g. `v3.2.0`, per the docs' own recommendation) and
            upgrade deliberately, version by version, reading each
            guide — not by silently riding `stable` forward.

    3.10  argocd-cm and argocd-cmd-params-cm — The Two Configuration ConfigMaps
         - Consolidating what 3.2/3.3 already touched piecemeal
           (admin.enabled, server.insecure, oidc.config, dex.config)
           into one reference
         📖 KEY TERM — two ConfigMaps, two different jobs, easy to
            confuse:
            `argocd-cm` — application-level behavior. Beyond
            `admin.enabled` (3.2) and `oidc.config`/`dex.config` (3.3):
            - `users.session.duration` — CLI/UI session token lifetime,
              default `24h`.
            - `timeout.reconciliation` / `timeout.reconciliation.jitter`
              — already covered in 3.3's KEY TERM (120s/60s defaults);
              set `timeout.reconciliation: "0"` to disable the periodic
              timer entirely and rely only on watch events + webhooks.
            - `resource.exclusions` / `resource.inclusions` — glob-based
              filters on which Group/Kinds Argo CD even watches;
              `events.k8s.io` and `metrics.k8s.io` are excluded by
              default for performance. Use `resource.exclusions` to keep
              easypj's Application from churning on high-frequency
              resources it doesn't manage (Events, Leases).
            - `application.instanceLabelKey` — overrides the tracking
              label Argo CD stamps on every resource it owns, default
              `app.kubernetes.io/instance`.
            - `url` — the externally-facing base URL for the Argo CD
              instance; required once you configure SSO (oidc.config
              redirect URIs need it).

            `argocd-cmd-params-cm` — component *startup flags*, expressed
            as ConfigMap keys instead of container args (so you don't
            have to `kubectl edit` a Deployment for common tuning):
            - `server.insecure` — the ConfigMap form of `--insecure`
              from 3.2's Ingress GOTCHA, default `"false"`.
            - `server.disable.auth` — disables client auth entirely,
              default `"false"`; never set true outside a fully
              air-gapped debug session.
            - `application.namespaces` — the key that lets Argo CD
              manage Applications created in namespaces *other* than
              argocd (comma-separated list) — off by default, meaning a
              stray Application CRD outside argocd's namespace is
              silently ignored until you add it here.
            - `controller.status.processors` (default 20) /
              `controller.operation.processors` (default 10) — worker
              pool sizes for the application-controller; raise these
              before scaling replicas if a large easypj-style multi-app
              install starts lagging on reconciles.
            - `server.basehref` — set this when argocd-server sits behind
              a reverse proxy at a sub-path instead of `/`.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — editing either ConfigMap does not restart the pod
            that reads it for every key. argocd-server picks up most
            argocd-cm/argocd-cmd-params-cm changes without a restart, but
            some application-controller flags (the `controller.*` keys
            above) are only read at process start — check the specific
            key's docs before assuming a live edit takes effect
            immediately.

    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How the reconcile loop actually detects drift
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    The application-controller does NOT call the Kubernetes API fresh on
    every tick to answer "what's live?" — that wouldn't scale past a
    handful of Applications. On startup, it builds a watch-based,
    in-memory cluster cache (via the gitops-engine library Argo CD is
    built on) that subscribes to the Kubernetes watch API for every
    resource type/namespace it manages, so the cache stays live in real
    time as events arrive. "Get actual state" is a cache read, not a
    network round trip.

    "Get desired state" comes from argocd-repo-server: clone/fetch the
    Git repo at the target revision, render it (kustomize build / helm
    template / plain YAML pass-through), and hand back raw manifests —
    cached in Redis so repeated diffs against an unchanged commit don't
    re-render from scratch.

    The diff itself is NOT a text diff. It's gitops-engine's structured
    diff engine doing a normalized, field-by-field comparison per
    resource: both sides are normalized first (K8s-injected defaults
    stripped, the status subresource ignored) so real intent-level
    differences survive and noise doesn't. You can further suppress
    known-benign diffs with ignoreDifferences.

    The result sets SyncStatus (Synced/OutOfSync/Unknown) — a completely
    separate axis from HealthStatus (computed per-resource-kind). This
    whole reconcile — cache read, diff, status update — happens
    unconditionally, triggered by watch events, a Git webhook, or the
    periodic safety-net timer (~120s + up to 60s jitter). SYNCING —
    actually applying desired manifests — only happens automatically on
    top of that if spec.syncPolicy.automated is set; otherwise the
    controller stops at "I see a diff, marking OutOfSync" and waits for
    a human to run `argocd app sync`.

      GIT COMMIT              REPO-SERVER            APPLICATION-CONTROLLER
      +----------+  webhook/  +--------------+ desired +---------------------+
      | easypj-  |  poll/wave | clone + build|-------->| DIFF ENGINE          |
      | k8s repo |----------->| (kustomize/  |         | (normalize + compare)|
      +----------+            |  helm)       |         |        |             |
                               +--------------+         |        v             |
                                                         |  SyncStatus updated  |
      K8S WATCH API  ------------------------actual---->|  (Synced/OutOfSync)  |
      (live cluster cache, real-time)                   |        |            |
                                                          |  automated=true?    |
                                                          |   YES -> APPLY      |
                                                          |   NO  -> wait for   |
                                                          |        `app sync`   |
                                                          +---------------------+

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - Argo CD is 3 "brain" components (API server, repo-server,
      application-controller) + 4 support pods (redis, dex-server,
      applicationset-controller, notifications-controller) — 7 total in
      a non-HA install.
    - Install with `kubectl apply -n argocd --server-side --force-conflicts
      -f .../install.yaml` — plain client-side apply can fail on the
      ApplicationSet CRD's oversized annotation.
    - Application/AppProject are real CRDs; Repository/Cluster
      connections are plain Secrets labeled
      argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type.
    - The controller reconciles continuously but only syncs
      automatically when syncPolicy.automated is set — reconcile does
      not equal auto-fix.
    - `argocd admin initial-password -n argocd` -> log in -> `argocd
      account update-password` -> delete argocd-initial-admin-secret.
    🎯 3.1-3.3 = "Understand Argo CD Fundamentals"; 3.4-3.5 =
       "Synchronize Applications Using Argo CD" — both official CAPA
       competencies, both part of Argo CD's 34% exam domain.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Forgetting `--insecure` on `argocd login` — a fresh install
       serves a self-signed cert; without it the CLI refuses to connect.
    2. Missing `CreateNamespace=true` — Argo CD never creates the
       destination namespace on its own; first sync errors with
       "namespaces easypj-dev not found."
    3. Plain `kubectl apply` on install.yaml — without --server-side
       --force-conflicts, the ApplicationSet CRD can blow the
       262,144-byte annotation limit.
    4. Assuming OutOfSync means "Argo CD is fixing it" — without
       syncPolicy.automated, drift just changes the status badge.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    1. `argocd admin initial-password -n argocd` — no jsonpath/base64
       pipeline needed for the first login.
    2. `kubectl config set-context --current --namespace=argocd` right
       after install — drop -n argocd from later commands.
    3. `argocd app create <name> --repo <url> --path <path> --dest-server
       https://kubernetes.default.svc --dest-namespace <ns> && argocd app
       sync <name>` beats hand-writing a YAML file for throwaway labs.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ──────────────────────────────
    ┌─ INSTALL ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ kubectl create namespace argocd                                       │
    │ kubectl apply -n argocd --server-side --force-conflicts -f \          │
    │   https://raw.githubusercontent.com/argoproj/argo-cd/stable/          │
    │   manifests/install.yaml                                              │
    │ kubectl get pods -n argocd                    # expect 7 Running      │
    ├─ ACCESS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ kubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443             │
    │ argocd admin initial-password -n argocd        # first login          │
    │ argocd login localhost:8080 --username admin --insecure               │
    │ argocd account update-password                # then rotate it        │
    │ kubectl -n argocd delete secret argocd-initial-admin-secret # cleanup │
    │ # Argo CD recreates this secret on demand if the admin password       │
    │ # ever needs regenerating — deleting it is safe.                      │
    ├─ FIRST APPLICATION (easypj) ───────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ kubectl apply -f easypj-dev-app.yaml                                  │
    │ argocd app get easypj-dev                      # watch status         │
    │ argocd app sync easypj-dev                     # manual sync          │
    │ kubectl port-forward -n easypj-dev svc/easypj 8000:80                 │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~15 min, do-it-now)
    ────────────────────────────────────────────
    1. Install Argo CD with --server-side --force-conflicts; confirm 7
       pods reach Running.
    2. Identify which one is a StatefulSet and explain why.
    3. Retrieve the admin password, log in with --insecure, rotate the
       password, delete the initial-admin secret.
    4. Write easypj-dev-app.yaml pointing at a public repo, apply it,
       drive it to Synced/Healthy.
    5. Delete one resource easypj owns by hand and time how long Argo CD
       takes to notice (and restore it, if selfHeal is on).

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. You get "metadata.annotations: Too long: must have at most
        262144 bytes" installing Argo CD. Fix, and why does it work?
    A1. Re-run with --server-side --force-conflicts.
    Explanation: server-side apply tracks ownership via managedFields
    instead of the last-applied-configuration annotation, so the size
    limit no longer applies.

    Q2. List the 7 pods a default install creates, and identify the one
        StatefulSet.
    A2. argocd-server, argocd-repo-server, argocd-application-controller
        (StatefulSet), argocd-applicationset-controller, argocd-redis,
        argocd-dex-server, argocd-notifications-controller.
    Explanation: the application-controller is the only StatefulSet —
    that's why its pod carries an ordinal suffix (-0) while others carry
    a random hash.

    Q3. After `argocd login localhost:8080 --username admin --insecure`,
        what does --insecure actually do, and when should you drop it?
    A3. It skips TLS certificate verification. Drop it for any
        non-lab, production-facing endpoint.
    Explanation: a fresh install serves a self-signed cert the CLI
    won't trust by default.

    Q4. Your Application errors with "namespaces easypj-dev not found."
        What's missing, and where?
    A4. Add syncOptions: [CreateNamespace=true] under spec.syncPolicy.
    Explanation: destination.namespace only tells Argo CD WHERE to put
    resources — it never creates the namespace on its own.

    Q5. Which component actually runs kustomize build/helm template
        against your Git repo?
    A5. argocd-repo-server.
    Explanation: it's stateless with respect to the cluster — its only
    job is Git -> rendered YAML; it never talks to the target API.

    Q6. True/False: Repository and Cluster connections are CRDs, just
        like Application.
    A6. False.
    Explanation: Application/AppProject are real CRDs; Repository/
    Cluster connections are Secrets labeled
    argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type: repository or cluster.

    Q7. What does timeout.reconciliation (120s) + timeout.reconciliation.
        jitter (60s) control, and what's the worst-case interval?
    A7. The periodic full-resync safety-net timer; worst case ~180s.
    Explanation: real-time watch events and webhooks can trigger a
    reconcile sooner — this timer is only the fallback ceiling.

    Q8. No syncPolicy.automated is set. Someone kubectl edits the
        Deployment directly. What does Argo CD do?
    A8. Detects the diff, flips SyncStatus to OutOfSync — but does NOT
        revert the change, since no automated policy is configured.
    Explanation: reconcile (diff) is unconditional; sync (apply) only
    happens automatically when automated is set.

    Q9. You need a PVC to survive even when automated.prune: true is
        set. What single annotation, on the PVC, achieves this?
    A9. argocd.argoproj.io/sync-options: Prune=false
    Explanation: resource-level sync-options always override the
    Application-wide prune policy for that one object.

    Q10. You need a DB-migration Job to finish before the Deployment
         rolls out, ordered ahead of a ConfigMap update in the same
         sync. What two mechanisms, and their annotation keys?
    A10. A resource hook (argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync) plus a sync
         wave (argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "<int>") for fine ordering
         within that phase.
    Explanation: hooks give coarse phase ordering; sync waves give
    fine-grained ordering within a phase (lower first, default 0).

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 3 VISUAL SUMMARY — Argo CD Installed & easypj Live             │
    │                                                                          │
    │   Git (easypj-k8s) --> argocd-repo-server --> argocd-application-      │
    │        ^                  (render YAML)          controller            │
    │        |                                          (diff + reconcile)   │
    │   git push                                              |              │
    │        |                                                 v              │
    │   You -+                                     SyncStatus: Synced        │
    │                                               HealthStatus: Healthy    │
    │                                                          |              │
    │                                                          v              │
    │                                          K8s cluster: easypj-dev ns     │
    │                                          (Deployment/Service/ConfigMap) │
    │                                                                          │
    │   Supporting cast: argocd-server (UI/API/CLI) - argocd-redis (cache) - │
    │   argocd-dex-server (SSO) - argocd-notifications-controller (alerts) - │
    │   argocd-applicationset-controller (multi-app generation)              │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: You installed Argo CD's 7-pod control plane with a
    server-side apply, logged in, and pointed it at easypj via a single
    Application manifest. From `git push` onward, the loop is always the
    same three-hop relay: repo-server renders what Git says, the
    application-controller diffs that against what it's watching live in
    the cluster, and the result becomes a SyncStatus/HealthStatus badge,
    acted on automatically only if you told it to.


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 2: ARGO CD DEEP DIVE — THE GITOPS ENGINE                         🟢🟡
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
    🎯 EASYPJ FOCUS: Deploy the easypj/jobpost Django app to dev,
       staging, and production using Argo CD GitOps with Kustomize.

Chapter 4: Argo CD Applications — The Core Object
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~3.5 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo CD 34% (highest-density chapter) · 🏢 Daily-Work: high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    Every single thing Argo CD does for easypj — every sync, every
    rollback, every self-healed drift — funnels through ONE Kubernetes
    object: the Application. Get its fields wrong and easypj either
    never deploys, deploys to the wrong namespace, or silently
    diverges from Git and nobody notices until it breaks. Get them
    right and `kubectl apply -f easypj-app.yaml` is the LAST manual
    deploy command you ever run against this app — Git becomes the
    only thing that changes production from here on.

    ┌─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE (Chapter 3, manual) │  AFTER (this chapter, declared)  │
    ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
    │ You hand-craft one          │ You describe WHERE (source),     │
    │ Application YAML by copying │ WHERE-TO (destination), HOW      │
    │ the Ch.3 example and hoping │ (syncPolicy), and Argo CD figures│
    │ the fields are right.       │ out the rest.                    │
    └─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES — 🎯 What you'll be able to DO
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Write a complete easypj Application manifest from memory —
         source, destination, project, syncPolicy — with zero fields
         missing or misplaced.
      2. Choose the correct source type (Directory / Helm / Kustomize
         / Plugin) for a given easypj scenario and defend the choice.
      3. Explain spec.source vs spec.sources (2.6+), and wire a
         values-only repo into a Helm chart repo using `ref:`.
      4. Sequence a multi-resource easypj rollout with sync-wave
         annotations and predict creation order.
      5. Pick the right combination of sync options (Replace,
         ServerSideApply, PruneLast, RespectIgnoreDifferences,
         ApplyOutOfSyncOnly, CreateNamespace) to solve a real sync
         failure.
      6. Read an Application's Sync Status + Health Status combination
         cold and state the correct next troubleshooting step.

    [D] CAPA RELEVANCE  (Argo CD domain — 34% of the exam)
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
    │ CAPA Competency                     │ Covered in this chapter   │
    ├────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
    │ Use Argo CD Application             │ 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, 4.6 (core) │
    │ Configure Argo CD with Helm and     │ 4.2 (source types), 4.3   │
    │ Kustomize                           │ (multi-source Helm)       │
    │ Synchronize Applications Using      │ 4.4 (sync waves), 4.5     │
    │ Argo CD                             │ (sync options)            │
    │ Identify Common Reconciliation      │ 4.6 (health/sync status)  │
    │ Patterns                            │                            │
    └────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘
    This is the single most exam-dense chapter in the guide's Argo CD
    section — nearly every "Use Argo CD Application" question draws
    directly from 4.1-4.6.

    [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM  (you are here)

    4.1  The Application CRD — Deep Dive
         - Every field explained: source, destination, project, syncPolicy
         - Source types: Helm, Kustomize, Directory, Plugin (NOT Ksonnet
           — removed from Argo CD years ago; upstream Ksonnet was
           deprecated in 2019, treat it as an exam distractor)
         - Destination: Which cluster + namespace?
         📖 KEY TERM — Three top-level spec fields sit outside the anatomy
            diagram below but are real, examinable Application fields:
            `ignoreDifferences` (per-resource diff exceptions — see 4.5's
            RespectIgnoreDifferences), `info` (a list of {name, value}
            pairs shown only in the Argo CD UI — e.g. "- name: Owner" /
            "value: easypj-team" — zero functional effect), and
            `revisionHistoryLimit` (how many old synced revisions Argo CD
            keeps in status.history for `argocd app rollback`; default
            10 — lowering it never touches Git history, only how far back
            rollback can reach).
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `destination.server` and `destination.name` are
            ALTERNATIVES for the same purpose (which cluster) — set
            exactly one, never both. `destination.server` is the literal
            API server URL; `destination.name` is a cluster alias — for
            REMOTE clusters it's created by `argocd cluster add` (stored
            as a Secret in the argocd namespace), but the LOCAL/default
            cluster already ships with the built-in alias "in-cluster"
            and has no such Secret at all.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `project: default` is not a neutral placeholder.
            The `default` AppProject is auto-created on install with
            wildcard everything: `sourceRepos: ['*']`, `destinations:
            ['*']`, `clusterResourceWhitelist: ['*']` — any repo, any
            cluster, any namespace, any resource kind. Leaving
            easypj-prod on `project: default` means any user who can
            create Applications can point any repo at any cluster
            through it. Chapter 5 replaces this with a scoped
            AppProject — read `default` as "no restrictions configured
            yet," not a safe choice.
         [YAML ANATOMY: Application manifest with every field annotated]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ANATOMY OF AN ARGO CD APPLICATION                         │
         │                                                            │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
         │  kind: Application                                         │
         │  metadata:                                                 │
         │    name: easypj-prod        ← Unique name in namespace     │
         │    namespace: argocd        ← Argo CD namespace            │
         │  spec:                                                     │
         │    project: default         ← AppProject this belongs to   │
         │    source:                  ← WHERE is the YAML?           │
         │      repoURL: https://github.com/...  ← Git repo URL      │
         │      targetRevision: main   ← Branch/tag/commit           │
         │      path: k8s/overlays/prod ← Folder in repo             │
         │    destination:             ← WHERE to deploy to           │
         │      server: https://kubernetes.default.svc ← Cluster     │
         │      namespace: easypj-prod ← Target namespace            │
         │    syncPolicy:              ← HOW to sync                  │
         │      automated:             ← Auto-sync enabled           │
         │        prune: true          ← Delete resources not in Git │
         │        selfHeal: true       ← Auto-fix drift              │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.2  Source Types — Choosing the Right One
         - Directory: Plain YAML files in a folder
         - Helm: Helm charts with values files
         - Kustomize: Overlay-based configuration
         - Plugin: Custom config management
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — if spec.source.helm/kustomize/directory/
            plugin isn't explicitly set, the repo-server auto-detects
            the tool by file presence, in this FIXED priority order:
            (1) Helm, if Chart.yaml exists in the path; (2) Kustomize,
            if kustomization.yaml/kustomization.yml/Kustomization
            exists; (3) otherwise, plain Directory.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — a stray Chart.yaml left in an easypj Kustomize
            overlay folder (e.g. copy-pasted from another project)
            silently flips that Application to Helm rendering —
            Kustomize detection never even runs, because Helm is
            checked first.
         📖 KEY TERM — Config Management Plugin (CMP): a sidecar
            container attached to the repo-server pod, running the
            argocd-cmp-server binary, that the repo-server delegates
            manifest generation to for tools with no native support
            (cdk8s, a custom shell script, etc.). spec.source.plugin
            can name the plugin explicitly or rely on the CMP's own
            discovery rules.
         📖 KEY TERM — Jsonnet is real and still supported — but it's
            a capability of the Directory source (spec.source.
            directory.jsonnet, with extVars/tlas), not a 5th source
            type. Don't confuse it with Ksonnet (the removed, unrelated
            tool already flagged as a distractor elsewhere in this
            guide) — similar name, opposite exam answer.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — easypj's own overlays/dev, overlays/staging,
            overlays/prod layout is exactly the "multiple environments,
            one internal app" branch of the decision tree above — which
            is why this guide defaults easypj to Kustomize, not Helm.
         [DECISION FLOWCHART: Which source type for your project?]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SOURCE TYPE DECISION TREE                                 │
         │                                                            │
         │  START: Do you need to share this app with others?         │
         │         │                                                  │
         │    ┌────┴────┐                                             │
         │    │ YES     │  → HELM                                     │
         │    │ (share) │    Package as chart, publish to Artifact Hub│
         │    │         │    Best for: public apps, complex templates │
         │    └─────────┘                                             │
         │         │                                                  │
         │    ┌────┴────┐                                             │
         │    │ NO      │  → Do you have multiple environments?       │
         │    │ (internal)│                                           │
         │    └─────────┘     │                                       │
         │               ┌────┴────┐                                  │
         │               │ YES     │  → KUSTOMIZE                     │
         │               │ (dev,   │    Base + overlay patching       │
         │               │ staging,│    Simple, built into kubectl    │
         │               │ prod)   │    Best for: easypj, internal apps│
         │               └─────────┘                                  │
         │               ┌────┴────┐                                  │
         │               │ NO      │  → DIRECTORY                     │
         │               │ (single │    Plain YAML files              │
         │               │ env)    │    Simplest, no tooling needed   │
         │               └─────────┘                                  │
         │                                                            │
         │  PLUGIN: Use when none of the above fits (legacy tools,    │
         │  custom config management like Jsonnet, CUE, etc.)         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.3  Multiple Sources (Argo CD 2.6+)
         - Why multiple sources? — Separate app code from config
         - Example: easypj Helm chart + environment values from separate repos
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — a source with `ref:` set can also set `path:` (to
            contribute rendered manifests too, not just values), but it
            CANNOT also set `chart:` — ref and chart are mutually
            exclusive on the same source entry.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — spec.source (singular) and spec.sources (plural)
            are NOT merged if both are present — Argo CD silently
            ignores spec.source entirely and uses only spec.sources.
            There's no validation error for setting both. Don't
            half-migrate an Application to multi-source and expect the
            old source: block to still contribute anything.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — wiring a values-only repo into a Helm chart
            repo using ref: is a common exam pattern — the diagram's
            `ref: env-values` plus a companion `helm.valueFiles:
            ['$env-values/prod-values.yaml']` line is the complete
            pattern; the $-prefix on the ref name is the #1 syntax slip.
         [DIAGRAM: Multiple sources combining into one Application]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  MULTIPLE SOURCES — One Application, Multiple Repos       │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐             │
         │  │ REPO A (app code) │    │ REPO B (config)  │             │
         │  │ github.com/team/  │    │ github.com/ops/  │             │
         │  │ easypj-helm-chart │    │ easypj-env-values│             │
         │  │                    │    │                  │             │
         │  │  Chart.yaml        │    │  dev-values.yaml │             │
         │  │  templates/        │    │  staging-values. │             │
         │  │    deployment.yaml │    │    yaml          │             │
         │  │    service.yaml    │    │  prod-values.yaml│             │
         │  │  values.yaml (base)│    │                  │             │
         │  └────────┬─────────┘    └────────┬─────────┘             │
         │           │                       │                        │
         │           └───────────┬───────────┘                        │
         │                       │                                    │
         │                       ▼                                    │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐           │
         │  │         ARGO CD APPLICATION                 │           │
         │  │                                            │           │
         │  │  spec:                                     │           │
         │  │    sources:                                │           │
         │  │      - repoURL: .../easypj-helm-chart     │           │
         │  │        path: .                            │           │
         │  │        targetRevision: main               │           │
         │  │      - repoURL: .../easypj-env-values     │           │
         │  │        path: .                            │           │
         │  │        targetRevision: main               │           │
         │  │        ref: env-values  ← reference name  │           │
         │  │                                            │           │
         │  │  RESULT: Helm chart + env values merged    │           │
         │  │  → rendered as single YAML set             │           │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────┘           │
         │                                                            │
         │  BENEFITS:                                                 │
         │  • Dev team owns app chart, Ops team owns env values       │
         │  • Change DB password? Only update config repo             │
         │  • App upgrade? Only update chart repo                     │
         │  • Separation of concerns!                                 │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.4  Sync Waves and Resource Ordering
         - Any integer wave (negative or positive), default 0 if unannotated
           — this guide's example spans -1 to 10 for illustration only
         - Example: Namespace (wave -1) → Secrets (wave 0) → Deployments (wave 1) → Services (wave 2)
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — sync-wave is the SECOND sort key, not the
            first. Argo CD orders a sync as: (1) hook PHASE first — all
            PreSync hooks run, then Sync-phase resources, then PostSync
            hooks; (2) WITHIN a phase, sync-wave (lowest first); (3)
            then resource kind, then name. A PreSync Job (e.g. easypj's
            Django migrate hook) always runs before ANY Sync-phase
            resource regardless of its sync-wave — wave only breaks
            ties inside one phase.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — "Across apps: use Application sync order" means
            something specific and limited: put sync-wave annotations
            on the CHILD Application resources themselves in an
            app-of-apps parent. This only orders the initial creation/
            sync triggered BY the parent syncing. Once child apps exist
            with their own independent automated syncPolicy, later
            syncs are NOT re-ordered by the parent's waves — they sync
            in whatever order each detects OutOfSync.
         [TIMELINE DIAGRAM: Sync waves executing in order]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SYNC WAVES — Ordered Resource Deployment                      │
         │                                                            │
         │  TIME ──────────────────────────────────────────────▶      │
         │                                                            │
         │  WAVE -1  │ Pre-deploy setup (create namespaces, CRDs)    │
         │  ─────────┤                                              │
         │  WAVE 0   │ Secrets, ConfigMaps, RBAC (no dependencies)  │
         │  ─────────┤                                              │
         │  WAVE 1   │ PVCs, Deployments, StatefulSets (apps)       │
         │  ─────────┤                                              │
         │  WAVE 2   │ Services (need Deployment ready first)       │
         │  ─────────┤                                              │
         │  WAVE 3   │ Ingress, NetworkPolicies (need Services up)  │
         │  ─────────┤                                              │
         │  WAVE 10  │ Post-deploy (smoke tests, notifications)     │
         │                                                            │
         │  HOW TO SET:                                               │
         │  metadata:                                                 │
         │    annotations:                                            │
         │      argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "2"                    │
         │                                                            │
         │  WAVES WITHIN SAME APP: All wave N resources sync before  │
         │  wave N+1 starts. Across apps: use Application sync order.│
         │                                                            │
         │  easypj EXAMPLE:                                           │
         │  Wave -1: Namespace "easypj-prod"                         │
         │  Wave 0:  DB password Secret, Django settings ConfigMap    │
         │  Wave 1:  PostgreSQL PVC, Django Deployment                │
         │  Wave 2:  Django Service (ClusterIP)                       │
         │  Wave 3:  Ingress (expose to internet)                     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    4.5  Sync Options — Fine-Tuning Behavior
         - Replace vs Apply (replace for immutable fields)
         - Server-Side Apply
         - FailOnSharedResource
         - RespectIgnoreDifferences
         [TABLE: Sync options and when to use them]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SYNC OPTIONS REFERENCE                                     │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌───────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │ OPTION               │ WHEN TO USE                    ││
         │  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Replace=true         │ Immutable fields (Job specs)   ││
         │  │                      │ Recreate resources that can't  ││
         │  │                      │ be patched in-place            ││
         │  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ ServerSideApply=true │ When you use kubectl apply     ││
         │  │                      │ --server-side in your workflow ││
         │  │                      │ (K8s 1.22+, handles conflicts) ││
         │  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Validate=true        │ Default: validates YAML before ││
         │  │                      │ applying. Set false for CRDs   ││
         │  │                      │ that K8s doesn't recognize yet ││
         │  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ PruneLast=true       │ Delete old resources AFTER     ││
         │  │                      │ creating new ones (zero-downtime)│
         │  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ RespectIgnoreDiff... │ Ignore fields that drift       ││
         │  │ =true                │ intentionally (HPA replicas,   ││
         │  │                      │ pod lifecycle fields)          ││
         │  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ FailOnSharedResource │ Prevent accidental deletion    ││
         │  │ =true                │ of resources shared across apps││
         │  └───────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         📖 KEY TERM — two of the rows above are more precise than they
            look: RespectIgnoreDifferences=true makes the SYNC/APPLY step
            itself (not just the diff view) honor spec.ignoreDifferences —
            without it, `ignoreDifferences` only changes what counts as
            OutOfSync, while sync still re-patches the "ignored" field,
            fighting a controller like an HPA on every sync.
            FailOnSharedResource=true actually fails the whole sync
            operation (refuses to apply) when a resource is already
            owned by a DIFFERENT Application — it stops silent cross-app
            overwrites in general, not just deletions specifically.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the Validate=true row under-states WHY you'd flip
            it off. Validate=false is for resources using RawExtension
            fields that don't fit standard OpenAPI schema validation
            (documented example: ServiceCatalog resources) — it's about
            client-side schema shape, not whether a CRD is registered
            yet. "CRD not yet in the cluster" is a DIFFERENT sync
            option entirely: SkipDryRunOnMissingResource=true, which
            skips the dry-run diff for a resource whose CRD isn't
            discoverable yet (created in the same sync, or by an
            out-of-band controller like Gatekeeper).
         📖 KEY TERM — the table is also missing a real, distinct
            option: Force=true. Where Replace=true swaps `kubectl
            apply` for `kubectl replace`/`kubectl create` (for
            oversized specs that don't fit the last-applied-
            configuration annotation), Force=true actually DELETES and
            recreates the resource — e.g. a Job that must re-run on
            every sync. The two are independent and commonly combined:
            "Force=true,Replace=true".

    4.6  Health Checks and Status
         - How Argo CD determines health (progressing, healthy, degraded, suspended)
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — that's only 4 of the 6 real HealthStatusCode
            values. The full enum is Healthy, Progressing, Degraded,
            Suspended, Missing, and Unknown — both real, returnable
            statuses. Missing means the resource is defined in Git but
            absent from the live cluster. This chapter's own [H] Key
            Takeaways and Lab Questions correctly use all 6 — this
            bullet is just an abbreviated list, not a smaller enum.
         - Custom health checks for CRDs
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — a custom Lua health check (registered
            under resource.customizations.health.<group>_<kind> in
            argocd-cm) must return a table with a `status` field (and
            optional `message`) — and it may ONLY return Healthy,
            Progressing, Degraded, or Suspended. It can never return
            Missing or Unknown — those are computed by Argo CD itself
            (Missing = expected but absent; Unknown = the check errored
            or no rule matched), never by user Lua code.
         - App status (Sync Status, exactly 3 values): Synced, OutOfSync,
           Unknown — NOT "Syncing" (that's the operation PHASE, a separate
           field: status.operationState.phase, active only mid-sync)
         [STATUS FLOW DIAGRAM: Health check lifecycle]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD APPLICATION HEALTH & SYNC STATUS                   │
