         │                                                            │
         │  SYNC STATUS (Source vs Cluster):                          │
         │  ┌──────────┐     ┌──────────┐     ┌───────────┐          │
         │  │ Unknown  │────▶│OutOfSync │────▶│ Syncing   │          │
         │  │(initial) │     │(diff!)   │     │(applying) │          │
         │  └──────────┘     └──────────┘     └─────┬─────┘          │
         │                                          │                │
         │                   ┌──────────────────────┘                │
         │                   ▼                                       │
         │              ┌──────────┐                                 │
         │              │ Synced   │ ←  Git = Cluster ✓              │
         │              └──────────┘                                 │
         │                                                            │
         │  HEALTH STATUS (Resource-level):                           │
         │  ┌───────────┐   ┌──────────┐   ┌──────────┐             │
         │  │Unknown    │──▶│Progressing│──▶│ Healthy  │  ← ✅ Good! │
         │  └───────────┘   └──────────┘   └──────────┘             │
         │                        │                                  │
         │                        ▼                                  │
         │                   ┌──────────┐   ┌──────────┐             │
         │                   │ Degraded │   │Suspended │             │
         │                   │ (failed) │   │(paused)  │             │
         │                   └──────────┘   └──────────┘             │
         │                                                            │
         │  APP STATUS (Combined):                                    │
         │  Synced + Healthy    = 🟢 Everything is good               │
         │  Synced + Degraded   = 🔴 YAML applied but pods crashing   │
         │  OutOfSync + Healthy = 🟡 Drift detected, need to sync     │
         │  Synced + Progressing= 🟡 Waiting for pods to be ready     │
         │                                                            │
         │  CUSTOM HEALTH CHECK for Django:                           │
         │  Built-in check: updatedReplicas/available vs spec.replicas│
         │  + observedGeneration (NOT readyReplicas==replicas)        │
         │  You can add custom checks via Lua scripts in argocd-cm   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — this section's own bullet states Sync
            Status is "exactly 3 values... NOT Syncing" — yet the
            diagram above draws a 4th box literally labeled "Syncing."
            Both are true because they describe different layers: the
            API field status.sync.status (SyncStatusCode) really is
            only ever Unknown/Synced/OutOfSync. The "Syncing" the UI/
            CLI shows is a DERIVED label displayed whenever
            status.operationState.phase == "Running" (the literal enum
            value is Running, not Syncing; the full set is Running/
            Succeeded/Failed/Error/Terminating). Read the diagram's
            "Syncing" box as "OutOfSync + an operation currently
            Running," not as a 4th Sync Status.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the resource-level HEALTH STATUS flow above never
            draws a Missing box. Missing is real and outranks Degraded
            in the health rollup: Healthy > Suspended > Progressing >
            Missing > Degraded > Unknown. A likely easypj cause:
            someone `kubectl delete`s the Django Service manually while
            syncPolicy.automated.selfHeal is off — Sync Status flips
            OutOfSync, Health Status flips to Missing for that
            resource specifically.

    4.7  Parameter Overrides and the Build Environment
         - Override a value WITHOUT a Git commit: `argocd app set
           <app> -p (COMPONENT=)PARAM=VALUE` — e.g. `argocd app set
           easypj-prod -p image.tag=v2.4.1` (Helm) or
           `--kustomize-image easypj-django=easypj-django:v2.4.1`
           (Kustomize).
         - Where the override actually lives: NOT only in-memory —
           Argo CD writes it to a generated file back in the source
           repo, so the next `git pull` shows it.
         📖 KEY TERM — the override file naming scheme: a bare
            `.argocd-source.yaml` in the source path applies to every
            Application sourced from that path; `.argocd-source-
            <appname>.yaml` (or, namespaced installs,
            `.argocd-source-<namespace>_<appname>.yaml`) applies to one
            named Application only. If both exist, the generic file's
            parameters merge FIRST, then the app-specific file's
            parameters merge on top — an app-specific override always
            wins a conflict with the generic file.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — for multi-source Applications (4.3), plain
            `-p` targets source #1 by default; use `--source-position
            <index>` (1-based) to target a specific source, e.g.
            `argocd app set easypj-prod --source-position 2 -p
            replicaCount=2`.
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — build env vars are injected automatically
            on every render, for Helm, Kustomize, Jsonnet, and Custom
            (Plugin) tools alike: `ARGOCD_APP_NAME`, `ARGOCD_APP_
            NAMESPACE`, `ARGOCD_APP_PROJECT_NAME`, `ARGOCD_APP_REVISION`
            (full commit SHA), `ARGOCD_APP_REVISION_SHORT` (7 chars),
            `ARGOCD_APP_REVISION_SHORT_8` (8 chars), `ARGOCD_APP_SOURCE_
            PATH`, `ARGOCD_APP_SOURCE_REPO_URL`, `ARGOCD_APP_SOURCE_
            TARGET_REVISION`, `KUBE_VERSION`, and `KUBE_API_VERSIONS`.
            Reference them with a single `$` prefix (e.g.
            `$ARGOCD_APP_REVISION` inside a Plugin's `command:`/`args:`
            block); to emit a literal `$` instead of triggering
            interpolation, escape it as `$$`.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — these are BUILD environment variables (available
            while the repo-server renders YAML), not the same thing as
            the `argocd` CLI's own environment variables (`ARGOCD_
            SERVER`, `ARGOCD_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ARGOCD_OPTS`, etc., used to
            configure how the CLI binary connects) — same `ARGOCD_`
            prefix, two unrelated purposes.

    4.8  Compare Options — Diff Customization
         - A resource-level ANNOTATION (not a sync option) that changes
           what counts toward Sync Status, not how a sync behaves.
         📖 KEY TERM — `argocd.argoproj.io/compare-options:
            IgnoreExtraneous` on a resource excludes THAT resource from
            the Application's Sync Status calculation — e.g. a
            ConfigMap some other controller mutates in ways that would
            otherwise permanently show easypj-prod as OutOfSync.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — IgnoreExtraneous only touches Sync Status. If that
            same resource is unhealthy, it still drives the
            Application's Health Status to Degraded via the worst-child
            rollup (4.6) — "ignored for diff purposes" is not "ignored
            for health purposes." Pair it with the `Prune=false` sync
            option (4.5) if you also don't want it deleted.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — easypj's Kustomize overlays can stamp this
            automatically via `generatorOptions.annotations` on a
            `configMapGenerator`, so every ConfigMap that generator
            produces gets `compare-options: IgnoreExtraneous` without
            hand-annotating each one.

    4.9  Resource Tracking — How Argo CD Matches Live Resources to
         Applications
         - The mechanism that answers "does THIS Deployment in the
           cluster belong to easypj-prod, or to some other Application
           (or nothing)?" — set via `application.resourceTrackingMethod`
           in the `argocd-cm` ConfigMap.
         📖 KEY TERM — three valid values: `annotation` (the
            `argocd.argoproj.io/tracking-id` annotation, formatted
            `<app-name>:<group>/<kind>:<namespace>/<name>` — e.g.
            `easypj-prod:apps/Deployment:easypj-prod/django`);
            `label` (the older `app.kubernetes.io/instance` label, set
            to the Application name); and `annotation+label` (writes
            BOTH — the annotation is authoritative for ownership, the
            label stays so external tooling that reads app.kubernetes.
            io/instance still sees something sensible).
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — per current stable docs, `annotation` is the
            documented DEFAULT if the key is unset — a newer default
            than older Argo CD deployments may assume; verify with
            `kubectl get cm argocd-cm -n argocd -o yaml | grep
            resourceTrackingMethod` before assuming which one is
            active on easypj's cluster.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — why annotation tracking exists, two
            concrete reasons: (1) Kubernetes labels truncate at 63
            characters — a long easypj Application name plus prefix
            can exceed that; (2) label COLLISIONS — some Helm charts
            and operators write their own app.kubernetes.io/instance
            label onto generated resources, confusing label-based
            tracking about which Application really owns a resource.
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — changing resourceTrackingMethod doesn't
            retroactively relabel/reannotate live resources — Argo CD
            only writes the new tracking metadata the NEXT time each
            Application syncs. `installationID` (a separate argocd-cm
            key) solves a related but different problem: multiple
            Argo CD instances sharing one cluster, each needing to
            tell its own managed resources apart via an `argocd.
            argoproj.io/installation-id` annotation.


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How the repo-server actually renders your manifests
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    When you write path: overlays/dev in an Application, nothing "runs"
    until the repo-server is asked. Here's the real pipeline:

    1. TRIGGER — the application-controller decides to refresh
       easypj-prod: on a poll timer (~180s max), on a Git webhook push,
       on `argocd app get --refresh`, or when a watched child resource
       changes in the live cluster.
    2. CACHE CHECK — the controller asks the repo-server for manifests
       at the target revision. The repo-server first checks its Redis
       manifest cache, keyed on {repo, revision, path, source config}.
       Cache hit -> cached YAML returned immediately, steps 3-4 skipped.
    3. CLONE/FETCH — on a cache miss, the repo-server keeps ONE local
       working clone per repo on disk and updates it via `fetch` (not a
       fresh clone every time), then checks out the target revision.
    4. TEMPLATE — the repo-server runs the tool matching your source
       type IN that checkout: Helm -> `helm template` (Argo CD's own
       embedded engine), Kustomize -> `kustomize build`, Directory ->
       reads files directly, Plugin -> invokes a sidecar Config
       Management Plugin. Output: plain rendered YAML, cached in Redis.
    5. DIFF — the application-controller compares this DESIRED state
       against LIVE state (held in a watch-based in-memory cache, not
       re-queried fresh). Result -> Sync Status.
    6. HEALTH — per resource, Argo CD runs its built-in or custom Lua
       health check -> Health Status. Worst-child rollup -> Application
       Health Status.
    7. ACT — if syncPolicy.automated is set and there's drift, a new
       Sync operation triggers, re-invoking the repo-server and
       applying wave-by-wave, honoring sync options.

    PROSE RECAP: the repo-server is a pure "Git+config -> YAML" renderer
    that leans hard on caching so it doesn't re-clone or re-template on
    every 3-minute poll; the application-controller is the brain that
    diffs, health-checks, and decides whether to act.

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - The Application CRD has 4 top-level concerns: project, source(s),
      destination, syncPolicy.
    - Source type is one of 4: Directory, Helm, Kustomize, Plugin (NOT
      Ksonnet — removed years ago).
    - spec.sources (plural, 2.6+) lets you split app code from config
      across repos, linked with ref:.
    - Sync waves (any integer, default 0) control ORDER; sync options
      control BEHAVIOR.
    - Sync Status (Synced/OutOfSync/Unknown) and Health Status
      (Healthy/Progressing/Degraded/Suspended/Missing/Unknown) are two
      INDEPENDENT axes — read both before you act.
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: this chapter maps to 4 of the 5 Argo CD domain
       competencies — the highest-density chapter in Part 2.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Sync Status is not Health Status. "Synced + Degraded" is real
       and common — Git applied fine, pods are crashing.
    2. Forgetting CreateNamespace=true when the destination namespace
       doesn't exist yet -> first sync fails outright.
    3. ignoreDifferences alone does NOT stop sync from re-applying an
       ignored field — you also need RespectIgnoreDifferences=true.
    4. Ksonnet and "Syncing-as-a-Sync-Status" are both outdated/wrong
       facts — Ksonnet was removed; Sync Status is only ever
       Unknown/Synced/OutOfSync.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - Skip the YAML for a first draft:
      argocd app create easypj-dev --repo <url> --path overlays/dev
      --dest-server https://kubernetes.default.svc --dest-namespace
      easypj-dev --sync-policy automated --auto-prune --self-heal
      --sync-option CreateNamespace=true
    - One-off prune without touching syncPolicy: argocd app sync
      easypj-prod --prune.
    - Add a sync option without re-editing YAML: argocd app set
      easypj-prod --sync-option ApplyOutOfSyncOnly=true.
    - Fast status peek: kubectl get application easypj-prod -n argocd
      -o jsonpath='{.status.sync.status} / {.status.health.status}'
    - Always argocd app diff easypj-prod before a manual sync.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ──────────────────────────────
      CREATE / INSPECT
        argocd app create <name> --repo <url> --path <path> \
          --dest-server <url> --dest-namespace <ns>
        argocd app get <name>
        argocd app diff <name>
      SYNC CONTROL
        argocd app sync <name>
        argocd app sync <name> --prune
        argocd app set <name> --sync-policy automated \
          --auto-prune --self-heal
        argocd app set <name> --sync-option <Option>=<value>
      TROUBLESHOOTING
        argocd app rollback <name> <revision-id>
        kubectl get application <name> -n argocd -o yaml
        kubectl -n argocd logs deploy/argocd-repo-server

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪  (10 minutes, no copy-paste)
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. From memory, write a complete Application manifest for a
         Kustomize source at overlays/dev, namespace easypj-dev, with
         automated prune+selfHeal and CreateNamespace=true.
      2. Add sync-wave annotations to 3 hypothetical resources
         (Namespace, Secret, Deployment).
      3. Without looking: what are the 3 valid Sync Status values, and
         the 6 valid Health Status values?
      4. State the difference between ignoreDifferences and
         RespectIgnoreDifferences=true.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪  (10, exam-style)
    ──────────────────────────────────────
      Q1. sync-wave: "-1" on the Namespace only, everything else
          unannotated. What wave do the rest sync in, relative to when?
      A1. Wave 0 (default), AFTER wave -1's Namespace is applied and
          healthy.
      Explanation: waves execute lowest-to-highest; absence of the
      annotation = wave 0, not "no wave."

      Q2. True or false: "Syncing" is one of the three valid Sync
          Status values.
      A2. False.
      Explanation: SyncStatusCode is Unknown/Synced/OutOfSync.
      "Syncing" is the operation PHASE, a different field entirely.

      Q3. Write spec.destination targeting the cluster registered as
          "in-cluster" (not by server URL), namespace easypj-prod.
      A3. destination: {name: in-cluster, namespace: easypj-prod}
      Explanation: destination.name is a valid alternative to
      destination.server — set one or the other, not both.

      Q4. ignoreDifferences is set on spec.replicas (an HPA manages
          it), but every sync still resets replicas. What syncOption
          fixes this?
      A4. RespectIgnoreDifferences=true.
      Explanation: ignoreDifferences alone only changes the diff view;
      RespectIgnoreDifferences makes sync itself honor the ignore rule.

      Q5. List every source type Argo CD's Application CRD supports
          today.
      A5. Directory, Helm, Kustomize, Plugin.
      Explanation: Ksonnet is NOT one of them — removed years ago.

      Q6. In a 2-source Application where source #2 sets ref:
          env-values, what line in source #1 pulls in its values file?
      A6. helm.valueFiles: [$env-values/prod-values.yaml]
      Explanation: the ref name becomes a $-prefixed path variable. A
      source with ref: set cannot also set chart:.

      Q7. Sync Status = OutOfSync, Health Status = Healthy. What does
          this mean, and next step?
      A7. Live differs from Git, but currently-running Pods are fine.
          Next: argocd app diff to see exactly what changed.
      Explanation: the two axes are independent — this is the most
      common "someone changed something, hasn't synced yet" state.

      Q8. PruneLast=true is set. What specifically changes about
          deletion?
      A8. Resources no longer in Git are still deleted, but only as
          the FINAL step, after every other resource applies.
      Explanation: PruneLast reorders WHEN prunes happen, for safer
      sequencing.

      Q9. Custom Lua health check for a Certificate CRD — which
          ConfigMap/key, and what 4 statuses may it return?
      A9. argocd-cm, key resource.customizations.health.<group>_<kind>;
          may return Healthy/Progressing/Degraded/Suspended only.
      Explanation: Missing and Unknown are computed by Argo CD itself.

      Q10. Rank Health Statuses most-to-least healthy for rollup:
           Degraded, Healthy, Missing, Progressing, Suspended, Unknown.
      A10. Healthy > Suspended > Progressing > Missing > Degraded >
           Unknown.
      Explanation: Missing outranks Degraded — a known exam trap.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │        ONE Application OBJECT, EVERY DECISION IT MAKES       │
    │                                                              │
    │  project --> which AppProject's rules apply (Ch.5)          │
    │  source(s) -> WHERE + WHICH TOOL (4.1-4.3)                  │
    │  destination -> WHICH cluster + namespace                   │
    │  syncPolicy -> automated? prune? selfHeal? + waves (4.4) +  │
    │               sync options (4.5)                             │
    │                        │                                     │
    │                        v                                     │
    │              repo-server renders manifests (see [G])         │
    │                        │                                     │
    │                        v                                     │
    │         controller diffs + health-checks (4.6)               │
    │                        │                                     │
    │            +-----------+-----------+                        │
    │            v                       v                        │
    │      Sync Status               Health Status                 │
    │  (Synced/OutOfSync/Unknown) (Healthy/Progressing/Degraded/   │
    │                              Suspended/Missing/Unknown)       │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: every field in the Application spec answers one of
    four questions — WHERE do manifests live, WHERE do they go, WHOSE
    rules apply, and HOW should applying happen. Sync Status and Health
    Status, read together, tell you the complete truth about
    easypj-prod without ever running kubectl by hand.


Chapter 5: AppProjects — Organizing and Securing Applications
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~2.5 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo CD 34% (indirect, RBAC/security) · 🏢 Daily-Work: very high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    Right now, every easypj Application you built in Chapter 4 quietly
    lives under the "default" AppProject — Argo CD's own wide-open,
    catch-all bucket. That's fine for a solo learning cluster. It is
    NOT fine the moment a second team, a second environment, or a
    single junior engineer with kubectl access joins the picture: with
    no AppProject boundary, ANY Application can source from ANY Git
    repo, deploy to ANY namespace on ANY registered cluster, and create
    ANY Kubernetes resource kind — including ClusterRoleBinding, which
    is a straight line to full cluster admin. AppProjects are how Argo
    CD turns "one big shared deployment tool" into "many safely-fenced
    teams and environments on the same instance." This chapter builds
    the fence around easypj-dev, easypj-staging, and — the one that
    actually matters — easypj-prod.

         BEFORE (no AppProject discipline)  │  AFTER (Ch 5 applied)
         ──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────
         easypj-web -> project: default     │ easypj-web -> project:
              sourceRepos: '*'              │      easypj-prod
              destinations: any ns/cluster  │   sourceRepos: 1 repo only
              clusterResourceWhitelist: '*' │   destinations: 1 ns only
              roles: (none)                 │   clusterResourceWhitelist: []
                                             │   roles: prod-admin (rare),
                                             │     prod-deployer, prod-readonly
         One bad merge = cluster admin      │ Blast radius = one project

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES
    ────────────────────────
      - EXPLAIN what an AppProject is, why every Application belongs to
        one (including the auto-created "default"), and why that
        default is a security risk if left untouched.
      - WRITE an AppProject manifest that restricts source repos,
        destinations, and resource kinds for a specific easypj
        environment.
      - DISTINGUISH allow-list (cluster-scoped) from deny-list
        (namespace-scoped) resource-restriction semantics.
      - WRITE AppProject RBAC policy lines using the `p,
        proj:<project>:<role>, <resource>, <action>, <object>, allow`
        syntax, and bind them to an SSO group or a CI token correctly.
      - DIAGNOSE common AppProject misconfigurations using `argocd proj
        get` and `argocd account can-i`.
      - APPLY AppProject best practices to harden easypj-prod.

    [D] CAPA RELEVANCE
    ───────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────┬────────────────┐
    │ Official Competency (Argo CD, 34%)      │ Graded? │ How Ch.5 helps │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────┼────────────────┤
    │ Use Argo CD Application (4.1-4.6, 7.3)  │ 🎯 YES  │ every spec.    │
    │                                          │         │ project field  │
    │ Understand Argo CD Fundamentals         │ 🎯 YES  │ AppProject is  │
    │  (3.1-3.3)                              │         │ core CD arch   │
    │ Identify Common Reconciliation          │ 🎯 YES  │ project-level  │
    │  Patterns (8.3, 21.1)                   │         │ restrictions   │
    │                                          │         │ shape sync     │
    └────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────┴────────────────┘
    NOTE: Chapter 5 isn't a standalone row in the official competency
    table — its content is tested THROUGH Application-focused questions
    that assume AppProject literacy.

    [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM
    ────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Ch 4: Application  --spec.project-->  Ch 5: AppProject         │
    │  (the WHAT to deploy)                  (the FENCE around it)    │
    │                                                                 │
    │        5.1 What IS an AppProject?  (concept + the "default"     │
    │            project trap)                                       │
    │                    │                                            │
    │                    v                                            │
    │        5.2 Creating AppProjects for easypj  (dev/staging/prod   │
    │            YAML)                                                │
    │                    │                                            │
    │                    v                                            │
    │        5.3 RBAC & Security  (roles -> policies -> WHO can        │
    │            sync/get/delete)                                    │
    │                    │                                            │
    │                    v                                            │
    │        5.4 Best Practices  (allow-lists, audits, hardening)     │
    │                    │                                            │
    │                    v                                            │
    │  Ch 6: ApplicationSets — generates MANY Applications, each      │
    │  still bound by the AppProject rules built here.                │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.1  What is an AppProject?
         - Logical grouping of Applications
         📖 KEY TERM — AppProject: a namespace-scoped Kubernetes custom
            resource (apiVersion argoproj.io/v1alpha1, kind AppProject)
            that normally lives in the argocd namespace next to the
            Applications it governs. It's a real object — `kubectl get
            appproject -n argocd` lists it like any other CRD, not an
            abstract policy concept layered on Applications.
         - Access control boundary
         🧠 ANALOGY — An AppProject is a building's floor-access badge,
            not a lock on one door. The Application (Ch 4) is the
            employee; the AppProject is which floors (namespaces), which
            entrances (source repos), and which rooms (resource kinds)
            that badge opens. easypj-dev and easypj-prod Applications
            can run in the SAME cluster, report to the SAME security
            desk (argocd-server), and still never touch each other's
            floor.
         - Restrict source repos, destination clusters, namespaces
         - Restrict resource types (what can be deployed)
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — Omitting spec.project on an Application does NOT
            mean "no project applies." Argo CD auto-creates a project
            literally named "default" the moment the argocd namespace
            exists, ships it with sourceRepos: ['*'], wildcard
            destinations, and zero resource restrictions — and it
            cannot be deleted (only edited). An unclaimed Application
            silently inherits full access through it. See 5.4 for
            locking "default" down.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — "why is the default AppProject dangerous"
            is a repeated CAPA question stem for this chapter. Know
            it's real, auto-created, un-deletable, and wide-open — not
            merely "no project."
         [CONCEPT DIAGRAM: AppProject as a boundary container]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  APPPROJECT — THE BOUNDARY CONTAINER                        │
         │                                                            │
         │   AppProject: easypj-prod                                   │
         │   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │   │ sourceRepos:      [github.com/team-easypj/         │    │
         │   │                    easypj-k8s]                     │    │
         │   │ destinations:     [easypj-prod namespace only]     │    │
         │   │ clusterResource   [] (nothing cluster-scoped)      │    │
         │   │  Whitelist:                                         │    │
         │   │                                                     │    │
         │   │   ┌───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────┐   │    │
         │   │   │ Application:       │   │ Application:       │   │    │
         │   │   │ easypj-prod        │   │ (any other app     │   │    │
         │   │   │  ✅ ALLOWED         │   │  someone tries to  │   │    │
         │   │   │  (repo+dest match) │   │  add here)          │   │    │
         │   │   └───────────────────┘   │  ❌ REJECTED if repo│   │    │
         │   │                            │  or destination      │   │    │
         │   │                            │  doesn't match       │   │    │
         │   │                            └───────────────────┘   │    │
         │   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                            │
         │  Meanwhile, AppProject easypj-dev is a SEPARATE, isolated   │
         │  boundary — different sourceRepos/destinations/whitelist —  │
         │  even though both projects live in the SAME argocd install. │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         PROSE RECAP: the AppProject doesn't touch Applications directly
         — it defines a WALL (allowed repos, destinations, resource
         kinds), and Argo CD's admission logic rejects any Application
         that tries to reference a project while stepping outside that
         project's own walls.

    5.2  Creating AppProjects for easypj
         - easypj-dev project: dev cluster, dev namespace
         📖 KEY TERM — sourceRepos glob/negation: entries support "*"
            globbing and a leading "!" to negate. A repo is permitted
            only if some allow-entry matches AND no deny-entry matches.
            "!*" alone is invalid — Argo CD rejects "disallow
            everything" as nonsensical. Real use: if team-easypj forks
            easypj-k8s into an internal mirror, add the mirror as a
            SECOND sourceRepos entry rather than widening the existing
            one.
         - easypj-staging project: staging cluster, staging namespace
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — clusterResourceWhitelist: [] wasn't always
            deny-all. Argo CD had a real, confirmed bug (~v1.7.3,
            GitHub issue #5540) where an explicitly empty whitelist was
            treated as "unconfigured" and fail-OPEN instead of
            deny-all. Modern Argo CD (what this guide teaches) fixed
            it: an empty or unset clusterResourceWhitelist now
            correctly denies every cluster-scoped kind by default.
            Setting it explicitly to [] (as dev/staging/prod all do
            here) is still good practice — it documents intent even
            though omitting it now behaves identically.
         - easypj-prod project: prod cluster, restricted resources
         📖 KEY TERM — There are actually FOUR resource-restriction
            fields, not two: clusterResourceWhitelist /
            clusterResourceBlacklist (both evaluate ONLY cluster-scoped
            kinds) and namespaceResourceWhitelist /
            namespaceResourceBlacklist (both evaluate ONLY
            namespace-scoped kinds). This chapter only shows
            clusterResourceWhitelist + namespaceResourceBlacklist — the
            common "allow-list what's dangerous, deny-list what's
            routine" pattern — but for a maximally locked-down
            easypj-prod you could instead set namespaceResourceWhitelist
            to just Deployment, StatefulSet, Service, ConfigMap, Secret
            — denying every OTHER namespace-scoped kind, a stricter
            posture than namespaceResourceBlacklist's "block a few,
            allow the rest."
         [YAML EXAMPLES: Three AppProject definitions]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  APPPROJECTS FOR easypj — Dev / Staging / Prod            │
         │                                                            │
         │  # DEV: Broad permissions, fast iteration                  │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
         │  kind: AppProject                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                 │
         │    name: easypj-dev                                        │
         │    namespace: argocd                                       │
         │  spec:                                                     │
         │    description: easypj Development Environment             │
         │    sourceRepos:                                            │
         │      - https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s          │
         │    destinations:                                           │
         │      - namespace: easypj-dev                              │
         │        server: https://kubernetes.default.svc             │
         │    clusterResourceWhitelist: []  # No cluster-scoped      │
         │                                                            │
         │  # STAGING: Same structure, different namespace            │
         │  ---                                                       │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
         │  kind: AppProject                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                 │
         │    name: easypj-staging                                    │
         │    namespace: argocd                                       │
         │  spec:                                                     │
         │    sourceRepos:                                            │
         │      - https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s          │
         │    destinations:                                           │
         │      - namespace: easypj-staging                          │
         │        server: https://kubernetes.default.svc             │
         │                                                            │
         │  # PROD: RESTRICTED — only certain repos and resources     │
         │  ---                                                       │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
         │  kind: AppProject                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                 │
         │    name: easypj-prod                                       │
         │    namespace: argocd                                       │
         │  spec:                                                     │
         │    description: easypj Production (RESTRICTED)             │
         │    sourceRepos:      # ONLY these repos can deploy to prod │
         │      - https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s          │
         │    destinations:                                            │
         │      - namespace: easypj-prod                             │
         │        server: https://kubernetes.default.svc             │
         │    clusterResourceWhitelist: []  # deny ALL cluster-scoped │
         │      # kinds explicitly (ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding    │
         │      # blocked HERE, not in namespaceResourceBlacklist —   │
         │      # they're cluster-scoped, that list only evaluates    │
         │      # namespace-scoped kinds; see 5.2 gotcha below)       │
         │    namespaceResourceBlacklist:  # NEVER allow these in prod│
         │      - group: ''                                           │
         │        kind: ResourceQuota                                 │
         │    roles:  # Who can do what                                │
         │      - name: prod-admin                                    │
         │        policies:                                           │
         │          - p, proj:easypj-prod:prod-admin, *, *, *, allow │
         │      - name: prod-readonly                                 │
         │        policies:                                           │
         │          - p, proj:easypj-prod:prod-readonly, *, get, *, allow│
         │    orphanedResources:                                       │
         │      warn: true  # Warn but don't auto-delete in prod     │
         📖 KEY TERM — Orphaned Resource: a top-level namespaced
            resource that does not belong to any Argo CD Application —
            e.g., someone kubectl applies a ConfigMap straight into
            easypj-prod outside of Git. orphanedResources.warn: true
            surfaces these on the Application's UI page; it does NOT
            delete or adopt them. Argo CD never flags the default
            ServiceAccount/its token, the kube-root-ca.crt ConfigMap, or
            the in-namespace kubernetes Service as orphaned — and you
            can add your own exceptions with orphanedResources.ignore:
            [{kind: ConfigMap, name: 'legacy-*'}].
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.3  RBAC & Security — AppProject Roles
         - AppProject `roles` define WHO can do WHAT within that project
         - Policy syntax: `p, proj:<project>:<role>, <resource>, <action>, <object>, allow`
         📖 KEY TERM — Argo CD ships exactly TWO built-in GLOBAL roles —
            role:readonly (get-only across every resource type) and
            role:admin (unrestricted; internally defined as `g,
            role:admin, role:readonly` PLUS full access, so admin
            literally inherits readonly). Both live outside any
            project. AppProject roles (prod-admin, prod-readonly, etc.)
            are a SEPARATE, project-scoped mechanism you define
            yourself — they never inherit from role:readonly/role:admin
            and role:readonly/role:admin never inherit from them. A
            user can hold a global role AND one or more project roles
            simultaneously; Casbin evaluates the union of every policy
            that matches, with any `deny` line winning regardless of
            order.
         📖 KEY TERM — SIX resource types are meaningful inside a
            proj:<project>:<role> policy line: applications,
            applicationsets, repositories, clusters, logs, exec. Their
            <object> field uses the compound <project>/<name> form
            (e.g. easypj-prod/*). BUT repositories/clusters only bite
            if they're PROJECT-SCOPED (their Secret carries a
            `project: easypj-prod` field — see 5.8) — a proj: role
            grants nothing toward the global/unscoped repo or cluster
            list. accounts, certificates, gpgkeys, and projects are
            NEVER project-scoped at all — those always need a plain
            (non-proj:) subject in the global argocd-rbac-cm policy.csv.
         - `prod-admin` above gets `*` (all resources, all actions) — use sparingly
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `*` for <resource> here is WIDER than it might look.
            prod-admin's "*, *, *, allow" DOES reach any repository or
            cluster Secret that's project-scoped to easypj-prod
            (project: easypj-prod in the Secret) — it can create/
            update/delete those credentials, not just sync
            Applications. It still grants NOTHING toward account
            management, certificates, or gpgkeys — those stay gated by
            argocd-rbac-cm regardless of how wide this project role
            looks, because those resource types are never
            project-scoped at all.
         - `prod-readonly` above gets `get` only — safe for on-call/auditors
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — To hand prod-readonly to a person or CI system,
            mint a scoped credential: `argocd proj role create-token
            easypj-prod prod-readonly --expires-in 12h -t`. The signed
            JWT is shown exactly ONCE — since JWT tokens aren't stored
            in Argo CD, they can only be retrieved when they are
            created. The AppProject object only ever persists metadata
            (an id + issued-at timestamp) for revocation via `argocd
            proj role delete-token`, never the token itself. Losing it
            means minting a new one.
         - Map AppProject roles to Argo CD RBAC groups (argocd-rbac-cm) for SSO
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — Don't let the "(argocd-rbac-cm)" parenthetical
            above mislead you: the SSO group binding for a PROJECT role
            is NOT written into argocd-rbac-cm at all. It's a field on
            the AppProject itself — roles[].groups: [team-easypj:sre] —
            applied the same GitOps way as the rest of the AppProject
            manifest. argocd-rbac-cm only holds the GLOBAL policy.csv /
            policy.default that applies outside any project context.
         [DIAGRAM: AppProject role → policy → Argo CD action mapping]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  easypj-prod AppProject — roles YAML (what prod-admin/       │
         │  prod-readonly above actually look like)                    │
         │                                                            │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
         │  kind: AppProject                                           │
         │  metadata: {name: easypj-prod, namespace: argocd}           │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    roles:                                                    │
         │    - name: prod-admin                                       │
         │      policies:                                              │
         │      - p, proj:easypj-prod:prod-admin, applications, *,    │
         │        easypj-prod/*, allow                                 │
         │      groups: [team-easypj:sre]                              │
         │    - name: prod-readonly                                    │
         │      policies:                                              │
         │      - p, proj:easypj-prod:prod-readonly, applications,     │
         │        get, easypj-prod/*, allow                             │
         │      groups: [team-easypj:on-call]                          │
         │                                                            │
         │  REQUEST → POLICY → ACTION, traced:                         │
         │  someone in team-easypj:sre runs `argocd app sync            │
         │  easypj-prod`                                                │
         │        │                                                    │
         │        ▼                                                    │
         │  Argo CD resolves their SSO group → matches                 │
         │  roles[].groups: [team-easypj:sre] → binds them to           │
         │  proj:easypj-prod:prod-admin                                 │
         │        │                                                    │
         │        ▼                                                    │
         │  policy line: applications, *, easypj-prod/*, allow          │
         │  → "sync" falls under the applications resource + *          │
         │  action → ALLOWED                                            │
         │                                                            │
         │  Someone in team-easypj:on-call runs the SAME command:       │
         │  bound to prod-readonly instead → policy only allows         │
         │  `get` → sync is DENIED, they can still `argocd app get`     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.4  AppProject Best Practices
         - One project per team/environment
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — "one project per team/environment" (vs. one
            shared project for everything) is the tested default
            pattern. A scenario question describing multiple teams
            sharing one loosely-configured AppProject is almost always
            modeling an anti-pattern the correct answer fixes by
            splitting projects, not by adding more roles to the one.
         - Allow-list destinations (never use "*")
         - Regular project audits
         [BEST PRACTICE CARDS: 6 project management tips]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  APPPROJECT BEST PRACTICES                                  │
         │                                                            │
         │  1. 🎯 NAMING CONVENTION                                   │
         │     <team>-<env>  e.g., easypj-dev, easypj-prod            │
         │                                                            │
         │  2. 🔒 NEVER USE WILDCARD DESTINATIONS                     │
         │     ❌ destinations.namespace: "*"                          │
         │     ✅ destinations.namespace: "easypj-prod"               │
         │                                                            │
         │  3. 🚫 BLACKLIST DANGEROUS RESOURCES IN PROD               │
         │     ClusterRole, ClusterRoleBinding, ResourceQuota,        │
         │     LimitRange, NetworkPolicy (if managed centrally)       │
         │                                                            │
         │  4. 📋 SEPARATE PROJECTS PER ENVIRONMENT                    │
         │     Dev can be loose, Prod must be tight                   │
         │                                                            │
         │  5. 👥 ASSIGN ROLES EXPLICITLY                              │
         │     prod-admin: full access (rare)                         │
         │     prod-deployer: sync + get applications only            │
         │     prod-readonly: get, list, log only                     │
         │                                                            │
         │  6. 🔍 AUDIT REGULARLY                                      │
         │     argocd proj list                                       │
         │     argocd proj get easypj-prod                            │
         │     # Check for unexpected source repos or destinations    │
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — a "blacklist dangerous resources in prod" mindset
            can list ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding next to
            ResourceQuota/LimitRange/NetworkPolicy as if one mechanism
            blocks all five. It doesn't. ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding
            are cluster-scoped — keep them OUT of
            clusterResourceWhitelist (or add them to
            clusterResourceBlacklist); putting them in
            namespaceResourceBlacklist is the exact silent no-op bug
            5.2's YAML comment warns about. ResourceQuota/LimitRange/
            NetworkPolicy ARE namespace-scoped, so
            namespaceResourceBlacklist is the correct field for those
            three only.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — "Audit regularly" in a real GitOps shop rarely
            means running `argocd proj get` ad hoc. easypj-prod's
            AppProject manifest should itself be committed to Git and
            applied the same way as any other manifest (app-of-apps or
            a bootstrap Application) — so "audit" becomes `git log -p --
            appprojects/easypj-prod.yaml`, giving you WHO changed a
            sourceRepo or role and WHEN for free, instead of relying on
            live-cluster inspection after the fact.
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    5.5  Applications in Any Namespace
         - By default every Application manifest must live IN the
           argocd namespace itself — even though spec.project and
           spec.destination can point anywhere.
         📖 KEY TERM — sourceNamespaces: an AppProject field (list of
            namespace names, shell-glob capable — e.g. 'easypj-*')
            declaring which OUTSIDE-argocd namespaces are allowed to
            host an Application that references THIS project. Requires
            a cluster-scoped Argo CD install (won't work namespace-
            scoped) plus the --application-namespaces flag (or
            argocd-cmd-params-cm's application.namespaces key) set
            GLOBALLY on argocd-server AND argocd-application-controller
            — both gates must open for a non-argocd-namespace
            Application to be recognized at all.
         🧠 ANALOGY — think of it as two locks on the same door: the
            controller-wide --application-namespaces flag is "which
            namespaces Argo CD is even willing to look at," and a
            project's sourceNamespaces is "which of those namespaces
            THIS project accepts Applications from." An Application
            sitting in an allowed namespace but naming a project that
            didn't list that namespace is still rejected.
         [YAML EXAMPLE: easypj-dev Application living outside argocd]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # argocd-application-controller / argocd-server flag:      │
         │  --application-namespaces=easypj-dev,easypj-staging         │
         │                                                              │
         │  # AppProject opts the namespace in:                        │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                            │
         │  kind: AppProject                                            │
         │  metadata: {name: easypj-dev, namespace: argocd}             │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    sourceNamespaces:                                        │
         │      - easypj-dev                                            │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Application can now live IN easypj-dev, not argocd:       │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                            │
         │  kind: Application                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: easypj-web                                          │
         │    namespace: easypj-dev      # <- was always "argocd" before│
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    project: easypj-dev                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — this is a privilege-escalation control, not a
            convenience feature. Docs explicitly warn: never add
            user-controlled namespaces to a privileged project's (e.g.
            "default") sourceNamespaces, and NEVER grant sourceNamespaces
            access to the argocd namespace itself — an Application still
            physically living in argocd can reference ANY AppProject
            regardless of that project's sourceNamespaces list, so
            widening sourceNamespaces on a permissive project is
            effectively widening who can self-service-create Applications
            with that project's blast radius.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — CLI/API calls now need the namespace qualifier:
            `argocd app get easypj-dev/easypj-web` (CLI form
            <namespace>/<name>; omit the namespace only for Applications
            still in argocd). RBAC object strings also change shape once
            this is enabled: <project>/<application> becomes
            <project>/<namespace>/<application>.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — the two-gate design (controller flag +
            project sourceNamespaces) is the testable point: an app
            team asking "why can't my Application in our own namespace
            sync" is almost always missing ONE of the two gates, not
            both.

    5.6  Sync Windows — Project-Level Deployment Scheduling
         - syncWindows restrict WHEN, not WHAT/WHERE — a fourth kind of
           AppProject fence alongside repos/destinations/resource kinds.
         📖 KEY TERM — syncWindows: an AppProject-level array, each
            entry has kind (allow|deny), schedule (standard cron
            string), duration (e.g. 1h), and one or more of
            applications/namespaces/clusters selectors (wildcard-
            capable) to scope which Applications the window applies to;
            optional manualSync (bool) and timeZone (IANA name, e.g.
            "Europe/Amsterdam"). This is a DIFFERENT mechanism from
            Chapter 6's Sync Windows subsection covers the same feature
            — that one focuses on the mechanics/precedence table, this
            one focuses on the AppProject placement and RBAC angle.
         - kind: allow → syncs are BLOCKED except during an active
           allow window.
         - kind: deny → syncs are BLOCKED only during the active
           window(s); allowed otherwise.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — if BOTH an allow and a deny window are active at
            the same instant for the same Application, deny wins —
            deny windows always override allow windows.
         - manualSync: true lets a human force a sync during an
           otherwise-blocked window; it does not affect automated sync.
         [YAML EXAMPLE: freeze easypj-prod outside a nightly deploy slot]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    syncWindows:                                              │
         │    - kind: allow                                             │
         │      schedule: '0 22 * * 1-5'   # 22:00 UTC, weekdays        │
         │      duration: 2h                                            │
         │      applications: ['easypj-*']                              │
         │      namespaces: [easypj-prod]                                │
         │      manualSync: true            # on-call can still force it│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — manage declaratively (commit to Git, same as
            the rest of the AppProject) or imperatively:
            `argocd proj windows add easypj-prod --kind allow --schedule
            "0 22 * * 1-5" --duration 2h --applications "easypj-*"` /
            `argocd proj windows list easypj-prod` /
            `argocd proj windows enable-manual-sync easypj-prod <ID>`.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — "why did an automated sync silently not
            fire even though the Application is Healthy and OutOfSync"
            is a syncWindows question stem as often as it's an
            automated-sync-policy question stem — check the PROJECT
            first, not just the Application.

    5.7  GPG Commit Signature Verification
         - Cryptographically prove every commit an Application deploys
           was made by an authorized person — not just that it came
           from an allowed repo.
         📖 KEY TERM — signatureKeys: an AppProject-level array of
            SignatureKey objects, each with exactly one field, keyID
            (the GPG key's 16-character hex ID). The MOMENT
            signatureKeys is non-empty on a project, EVERY Application
            in that project requires every deployed commit to carry a
            valid signature from one of those keys — there's no
            per-Application opt-out.
         [YAML EXAMPLE: require GitHub's signing key for easypj-prod]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                            │
         │  kind: AppProject                                            │
         │  metadata: {name: easypj-prod, namespace: argocd}            │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    signatureKeys:                                            │
         │      - keyID: 4AEE18F83AFDEB23                               │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — the key itself must be imported separately:
            `argocd gpg add --from <path-to-pubkey>` (binary or
            ASCII-armored). Argo CD stores it in the argocd-gpg-keys-cm
            ConfigMap; the argocd-repo-server pods mount it into a
            live GPG keyring. Adding signatureKeys to the AppProject
            without first running `argocd gpg add` means every sync in
            that project starts failing — key import and project
            enforcement are two separate steps, both required.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — an unsigned OR wrong-key-signed commit doesn't
            just get excluded like a resource-kind violation — the
            controller emits a ResourceComparison error and refuses to
            sync to that revision AT ALL. Also: this only works for Git
            repos — Helm chart repos can't be signature-verified this
            way.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — know this feature is DEPRECATED-adjacent in
            current Argo CD (flagged for eventual removal in docs) —
            expect it tested as "what it does today," not as a
            forward-looking best practice to recommend unprompted.

    5.8  Project-Scoped Repositories and Clusters
         - Solves a real daily-work bottleneck: without this, every new
           Git repo or cluster a team needs added to easypj-dev requires
           an Argo CD admin to edit the AppProject by hand.
         📖 KEY TERM — a repository or cluster Secret becomes
            project-scoped by adding a `project: <name>` field to its
            stringData (same Secret shape as any other repo/cluster
            credential, argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type: repository or
            cluster). Once scoped, it's usable ONLY by Applications/
            ApplicationSets whose spec.project matches — and it's what
            makes the `repositories`/`clusters` resource types in a
            proj:<project>:<role> policy line actually mean something
            (see 5.3's KEY TERM above).
         [YAML EXAMPLE: self-service repo credential scoped to easypj-dev]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: v1                                              │
         │  kind: Secret                                                │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: easypj-extra-repo                                   │
         │    labels: {argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type: repository}      │
         │  stringData:                                                 │
         │    project: easypj-dev        # <- scopes it to this project │
         │    name: easypj-extra                                       │
         │    url: https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-extra.git     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - To let a team self-service this, an admin grants it via RBAC
           (NOT via the AppProject's sourceRepos list):
           `p, proj:easypj-dev:admin, repositories, create,
            easypj-dev/*, allow` (+ update/delete as needed) — or,
           imperatively: `argocd repo add <URL> --project easypj-dev`.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — a project-scoped cluster ALSO implicitly widens
            that project's destinations: Argo CD dynamically adds an
            implicit destinations entry for it during evaluation
            (wildcard namespace by default, or the cluster secret's own
            `namespaces` field if set) — but this implicit entry is
            NEVER visible in the AppProject's own declarative spec or
            the UI, which can make `argocd proj get` look incomplete
            compared to actual permitted destinations.
         - permitOnlyProjectScopedClusters: true on the AppProject
           closes a related gap — by default an Application can name
           ANY registered cluster's server URL in spec.destination even
           if that cluster was never scoped to the project; this flag
           forces destinations to resolve only to clusters actually
           scoped to that same project.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — project-scoped repos/clusters is the
            mechanism CAPA questions use to test "how do teams add
            their own Git sources without admin involvement, safely" —
            the answer is never "widen sourceRepos to '*'."


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How Argo CD actually enforces an AppProject
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    AppProject restrictions are enforced at TWO separate points, and
    conflating them is the #1 source of confusion in this chapter:

    GATE 1 — API-LAYER VALIDATION (argocd-server, on every request)
      When ANY client (UI, CLI, CI token) calls an API action, argocd-
      server's embedded Casbin enforcer evaluates (subject, resource,
      action, object) against the FULL compiled policy set: built-in
      policy.csv + argocd-rbac-cm's policy.csv + every AppProject's
      roles[].policies. This answers "is THIS CALLER allowed to make
      THIS API call." It has nothing to do with sourceRepos/
      destinations/resource whitelists.

    GATE 2 — RECONCILE-LAYER VALIDATION (application-controller, does
      NOT depend on WHO asked)
      Separately, when an Application is created/updated or the
      controller reconciles it, its declared spec is checked against
      its OWN project's rules, regardless of caller identity: does
      spec.project reference an AppProject that exists? Does
      source.repoURL match a sourceRepos entry? Does
      destination.{server,namespace} match a destinations entry? For
      every rendered resource: cluster-scoped -> checked against
      clusterResourceWhitelist/Blacklist; namespace-scoped -> checked
      against namespaceResourceBlacklist/Whitelist. A violating
      resource is excluded from the sync, not silently applied.

    WHY THIS MATTERS: Gate 1 (RBAC) governs WHO may act. Gate 2
    (project spec) governs WHAT an Application belonging to that
    project may even declare — enforced even for an admin.

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - Every Application belongs to exactly one AppProject via
      spec.project — omit it and you silently get the wide-open
      "default" project.
    - Cluster-scoped resources = ALLOW-list. Namespace-scoped resources
      = DENY-list. Using the wrong list for a kind's scope is a silent
      no-op, not an error.
    - RBAC policy syntax is always `p, proj:<project>:<role>,
      <resource>, <action>, <object>, allow`.
    - Bind SSO groups to a project role via roles[].groups in the
      AppProject manifest itself — no argocd-rbac-cm edit required.
    - AppProject enforcement happens at TWO independent gates: RBAC
      (who may call the API) and project-spec validation (what the
      Application may declare).
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: AppProject literacy underpins "Use Argo CD
       Application" and "Identify Common Reconciliation Patterns."

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Forgetting to restrict destinations = accidental cross-namespace
       (or cross-cluster) access — the single most common real-world
       AND exam trap.
    2. WRONG LIST FOR THE WRONG SCOPE: putting a cluster-scoped kind
       into namespaceResourceBlacklist does NOTHING.
    3. Assuming project roles inherit from role:admin/role:readonly.
       They don't — no implicit baseline, no inheritance.
    4. A typo'd subject fails silently — Casbin just never matches it,
       so the role grants nothing. Confirm with `argocd account can-i`.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - `argocd proj create easypj-staging --dest
      https://kubernetes.default.svc,easypj-staging --src
      https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s` — one imperative line.
    - `argocd proj allow-cluster-resource <proj> <group> <kind>` /
      `deny-namespace-resource` — patch resource lists imperatively.
    - `argocd account can-i sync applications easypj-prod/*` — fastest
      "will this even work" check.
    - `argocd proj role create-token <proj> <role> --expires-in 1h -t`
      — mint a short-lived scoped token fast.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ──────────────────────────────
    ┌── PROJECT LIFECYCLE ──────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ argocd proj create <NAME> [--dest SERVER,NAMESPACE]            │
    │                            [--src REPO_URL]                    │
    │ argocd proj get <NAME>  /  argocd proj list                    │
    │ argocd proj edit <NAME>          # opens $EDITOR on live spec  │
    │ argocd proj delete <NAME>                                      │
    ├── SOURCES / DESTINATIONS ───────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ argocd proj add-source <NAME> <REPO_URL>                       │
    │ argocd proj add-destination <NAME> <SERVER|NAME> <NAMESPACE>   │
    ├── RESOURCE KIND RESTRICTIONS ────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ argocd proj allow-cluster-resource <NAME> <GROUP> <KIND>       │
    │ argocd proj deny-namespace-resource <NAME> <GROUP> <KIND>      │
    ├── ROLES & TOKENS ────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ argocd proj role create <NAME> <ROLE-NAME>                     │
    │ argocd proj role add-policy <NAME> <ROLE-NAME>                 │
    │             -a <ACTION> -o <OBJECT> [-p allow|deny]            │
    │ argocd proj role create-token <NAME> <ROLE-NAME>                │
    │             [--expires-in 12h] [-t]                            │
    ├── AUDIT / DEBUG ─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ argocd account can-i <ACTION> <RESOURCE> <SUBRESOURCE>         │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 — 10 MINUTES, DO IT NOW
    ────────────────────────────────────────────
    1. Imperatively create easypj-staging with --dest and --src.
    2. Add a staging-deployer role.
    3. Grant it sync+get only via add-policy.
    4. Verify BEFORE binding anyone with `argocd account can-i`.
    5. Mint a token for the role and confirm it works.
    6. Try (and watch fail) deploying a ClusterRoleBinding into
       easypj-staging — confirm rejection.
    7. `argocd proj get easypj-staging` and confirm every field
       matches what you intended.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10)
    ───────────────────────────
    Q1. Application has spec.project: easypj-prod, but that AppProject
        doesn't exist yet. What happens?
    A1. Validation error; the Application won't sync until the
        AppProject is created.
    Explanation: AppProject existence is checked before any sync
    attempt.

    Q2. easypj-prod has clusterResourceWhitelist: []. A manifest
        includes a ClusterRole. What happens on sync?
    A2. The ClusterRole is excluded from the sync; other permitted
        resources still sync normally.
    Explanation: an empty whitelist is deny-all for cluster-scoped
    kinds, by design.

    Q3. A teammate adds ClusterRoleBinding to
        namespaceResourceBlacklist, believing it's blocked. True or
        false: this successfully blocks it?
    A3. False (though it happens to be blocked anyway via
        clusterResourceWhitelist).
    Explanation: only clusterResourceWhitelist/Blacklist ever evaluate
    cluster-scoped kinds.

    Q4. Write the RBAC policy line granting easypj-prod's "deployer"
        role permission to SYNC (not delete) Applications in that
        project only.
    A4. p, proj:easypj-prod:deployer, applications, sync,
        easypj-prod/*, allow
    Explanation: subject = proj:<project>:<role>; applications uses the
    compound <project>/<object> object form.

    Q5. How do you bind SSO group team-easypj:sre to prod-admin WITHOUT
        editing argocd-rbac-cm?
    A5. roles[].groups: [team-easypj:sre] in the AppProject manifest.
    Explanation: roles[].groups is the primary, GitOps-native binding
    mechanism.

    Q6. CI pipeline must run `argocd app sync` non-interactively,
        scoped ONLY to easypj-prod. What mechanism?
    A6. Mint a project role token via `argocd proj role create-token`.
    Explanation: roles[].jwtTokens is the standard scoped-automation
    credential — narrower blast radius than a global admin token.

    Q7. True or False: policy.default: role:readonly in argocd-rbac-cm
        also restricts which Git repos easypj-prod's Applications may
        use.
    A7. False.
    Explanation: RBAC governs WHO may call WHAT; it's orthogonal to an
    AppProject's sourceRepos/destinations restrictions.

    Q8. Express in destinations: allow ANY namespace on the dev cluster
        except kube-system.
    A8. destinations: [{namespace: '!kube-system', server: ...},
        {namespace: '*', server: ...}]
    Explanation: glob-matched with optional "!" negation; permitted
    only if it matches an allow entry AND no deny entry.

    Q9. Why is the auto-created "default" AppProject a security risk?
    A9. It ships maximally permissive; any Application omitting
        spec.project silently inherits full access.
    Explanation: lock "default" down to deny-everything so a missing
    spec.project fails loudly instead of silently granting access.

    Q10. User holds global role:readonly. They can get Applications but
         `argocd app sync` returns permission denied. Expected?
    A10. Yes.
    Explanation: role:readonly's built-in policy only grants get-class
    actions; sync needs an explicit grant.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Application (spec.project: easypj-prod)                        │
    │        │                                                        │
    │        v                                                        │
    │  AppProject: easypj-prod                                        │
    │   sourceRepos | destinations | resource restrictions            │
    │        │  GATE 2: reconcile-layer validation                    │
    │        v                                                        │
    │  resource permitted -> applied  /  blocked -> excluded          │
    │                                                                  │
    │  roles: [prod-admin, prod-deployer, prod-readonly]              │
    │        bound via roles[].groups (SSO) or roles[].jwtTokens (CI) │
    │        v                                                        │
    │  GATE 1: argocd-server Casbin enforcer (WHO may call WHAT API)  │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: Chapter 5 built two independent fences around
    easypj-prod. The project SPEC fences WHAT the Application may ever
    declare; the project ROLES fence WHO may call which API action.
    Getting either wrong is the single most common real-world AND
    CAPA-exam AppProject failure mode.


Chapter 6: Advanced Argo CD Features
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡🔴 INTERMEDIATE/ADVANCED · ⏱️ ~2.5 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo CD 34% · 🏢 Daily-Work: high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ─────────────────────
    You've got easypj syncing to ONE cluster via ONE Application (Ch
    4-5). Real GitOps shops don't stop there: easypj needs to run in
    dev, staging AND prod, notify #deployments when it ships, and run
    a DB migration safely before the new pods ever take traffic.
    Hand-writing 3 near-identical Application YAMLs and remembering to
    kubectl apply a migration Job before every deploy doesn't scale
    and doesn't survive a 2am on-call handoff. This chapter is where
    Argo CD stops being "one app, one cluster" and becomes a
    fleet-management and release-safety engine.

    ┌─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE (Ch 1-5)            │  AFTER (Ch 6)                    │
    ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
    │  3 hand-written Application │  1 ApplicationSet generates all  │
    │  YAMLs (dev/staging/prod)   │  3, always in sync with the list │
    │  Manual "did the deploy     │  Slack/PagerDuty notified auto-  │
    │  work?" Slack message       │  matically on sync/health events │
    │  kubectl apply -f           │  PreSync hook runs the migration │
    │  migration-job.yaml (hope   │  Job for you — sync ABORTS if it │
    │  you remembered!)           │  fails                            │
    └─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES — 🎯 What you'll be able to DO
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      - Write an ApplicationSet using List, Cluster, Git, Matrix,
        Merge, SCM Provider, Pull Request, Cluster Decision Resource,
        and Plugin generators, and explain when to reach for each.
      - Deploy easypj to dev/staging/prod from a SINGLE ApplicationSet.
      - Configure a RollingSync strategy to promote easypj through
        environments in labeled, health-gated steps.
      - Wire up Argo CD Notifications so #deployments and on-call are
        alerted automatically.
      - Write PreSync/Sync/PostSync/SyncFail resource hooks that gate a
        deploy on a passing DB migration and smoke test.
      - Predict and control the ORDER hooks and resources apply in,
        using hook phase + sync-wave together.

    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌───────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐

    ────────────────────────────────────
