    PROSE RECAP: 6.1 creates the Applications, 6.2 controls the ORDER
    they roll out in, 6.4 controls the SAFETY of each individual sync,
    and 6.3 makes sure a human finds out what happened.

    6.1  ApplicationSets — Templated Applications at Scale
         - What problem do ApplicationSets solve?
         📖 KEY TERM — An ApplicationSet is its own CRD (group
            argoproj.io) reconciled by a SEPARATE controller (the
            "applicationset-controller"), distinct from the ordinary
            application-controller. It never talks to target clusters
            directly — it only ever creates/updates/deletes Application
            objects; the normal application-controller still does the
            actual syncing of each one.
         🧠 MENTAL MODEL — the generator is the dough, the
            ApplicationSet is the cookie cutter: a generator produces a
            list of {{key: value}} parameter maps ("dev", "staging",
            "prod"), and the ApplicationSet Go-templates spec.template
            once per map to stamp out one concrete Application each.
         - Generators (9 total): List, Cluster, Git (files + directories),
           Matrix, Merge, SCM Provider, Pull Request, Cluster Decision
           Resource, Plugin — the last 2 are commonly missed on the exam
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — Matrix and Merge are NOT interchangeable. Matrix
            combines EXACTLY two generators as a Cartesian product (env
            x cluster). Merge combines TWO OR MORE generators' outputs
            by matching keys — typically used to overlay per-cluster
            override values onto a base List/Cluster generator, not to
            multiply environments. To get a 3-generator product, nest a
            Matrix generator as one "child" of an outer Matrix.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — that nesting is capped at exactly ONE level deep.
            Official docs are explicit: Matrix and Merge generators
            "may be nested within each other, but only to one level" —
            an outer Matrix can have a nested Matrix/Merge as one of its
            two children, but that nested generator cannot itself
            contain another nested Matrix/Merge. A 3-level nest (Matrix
            inside Matrix inside Matrix) is explicitly rejected by the
            ApplicationSet controller, not just discouraged.
         - Real example: Deploy easypj to dev, staging, prod using a single ApplicationSet
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — in a real easypj/jobpost setup, List generator
            elements usually carry more than an env name — each entry
            also sets valuesFile/dbHost/replicaCount, e.g. {cluster:
            dev, valuesFile: values-dev.yaml, dbHost:
            postgres-dev.internal}, so the templated Application points
            Helm/Kustomize at the RIGHT overlay per environment, not
            just a different namespace.
         [DIAGRAM: ApplicationSet generating multiple Applications]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ApplicationSet → Generates → Multiple Applications       │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────────────────┐                                  │
         │  │   ApplicationSet    │                                   │
         │  │   Generator: List    │                                  │
         │  │   elements:          │                                  │
         │  │    - dev             │──┐                               │
         │  │    - staging         │  │                               │
         │  │    - prod            │  │                               │
         │  └──────────────────────┘  │                               │
         │                            ▼                               │
         │  ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐                   │
         │  │App:dev   │ │App:stage │ │App:prod  │                   │
         │  │namespace:│ │namespace:│ │namespace:│                   │
         │  │easypj-dev│ │easypj-stg│ │easypj-prd│                   │
         │  └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘                   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [GENERATOR REFERENCE TABLE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  APPLICATIONSET GENERATORS                                 │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌─────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │ GENERATOR       │ USE CASE                            ││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ List            │ Fixed list of environments          ││
         │  │                 │ (dev, staging, prod)                ││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Cluster         │ Deploy to every cluster Argo CD     ││
         │  │                 │ manages (fleet management)          ││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Git             │ One app per folder/branch in a repo ││
         │  │                 │ (monorepo pattern)                  ││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Matrix          │ Combine TWO generators (Cartesian   ││
         │  │                 │ product) e.g., env × region         ││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Merge           │ Merge multiple generators into one  ││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ SCM Provider    │ Auto-create apps for each repo in   ││
         │  │                 │ GitHub/GitLab org (platform pattern)││
         │  ├─────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ Pull Request    │ Create ephemeral preview envs per PR││
         │  │                 │ (auto-cleanup on PR close)          ││
         │  └─────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┘│
         │                                                            │
         │  easypj EXAMPLE — Matrix Generator:                         │
         │  Environments: [dev, staging, prod] ×                      │
         │  Clusters: [us-east, eu-west] = 6 Applications generated!  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         📖 KEY TERM — the table above shows 7 of the 9 generators; the
            2 missing rows are also real and examinable:
            • Cluster Decision Resource — duck-types a CUSTOM CR's
              status.decisions list (e.g. Open Cluster Management
              Placement) to pick target clusters dynamically.
            • Plugin — calls an RPC/HTTP endpoint you write yourself to
              return arbitrary parameter maps.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — CAPA scenario questions give you a TARGET
            AXIS (repos? clusters? PRs? a custom placement CR?) and ask
            for the ONE correct generator — memorize the use-case
            column above verbatim, including these 2.

    6.2  Progressive Sync — Staged Rollouts
         - Why sync in stages? (dev → staging → prod)
         📖 KEY TERM — the mechanism CAPA calls "Progressive Sync" is a
            named ApplicationSet field: spec.strategy.type: RollingSync
            (Beta since Argo CD v3.3). "Progressive Sync" is the
            docs/marketing name for the feature; RollingSync is the
            literal YAML value you write and the term the exam expects
            you to recognize.
         - Using sync waves across applications
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — this bullet is easy to misread. Sync WAVES
            (argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave, an annotation on individual
            resources/hooks) only order resources WITHIN one
            Application's own sync — they do NOT stage dev -> staging
            -> prod. Promoting MULTIPLE Applications in order is
            RollingSync's job (spec.strategy.type: RollingSync +
            strategy.rollingSync.steps), which groups the generated
            Applications by LABEL and waits for each group to go
            Healthy before unlocking the next. This exact mix-up is
            called out in [I] Exam Gotcha #3 later in this chapter.
         - Automated promotion with ApplicationSets
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — RollingSync steps look like this:
              strategy:
                type: RollingSync
                rollingSync:
                  steps:
                    - matchExpressions:
                        - key: envLabel
                          operator: In
                          values: [dev]
                      maxUpdate: 100%
                    - matchExpressions:
                        - key: envLabel
                          operator: In
                          values: [staging]
                      maxUpdate: 50%
                    - matchExpressions:
                        - key: envLabel
                          operator: In
                          values: [prod]
                      maxUpdate: 1
            Enabling RollingSync forces autosync OFF on every
            Application it manages (the controller warns if it finds
            automated syncPolicy already set) — you can't hand-enable
            autosync AND expect RollingSync to gate promotion.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the YAML field alone is silently insufficient. As a
            Beta feature, RollingSync also requires the
            applicationset-controller itself to be started with
            progressive syncs turned on, via ANY ONE of: the
            `--enable-progressive-syncs` container flag, the
            `ARGOCD_APPLICATIONSET_CONTROLLER_ENABLE_PROGRESSIVE_SYNCS`
            environment variable, or the
            `applicationsetcontroller.enable.progressive.syncs` key in
            the `argocd-cmd-params-cm` ConfigMap. Writing
            `strategy.type: RollingSync` on a cluster where none of
            those is set doesn't error — it just falls back to syncing
            every generated Application at once, silently skipping the
            staged rollout you configured.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for easypj this means dev promotes at 100% of
            matching Applications immediately, staging throttles to 50%
            at a time, and prod rolls ONE Application at a time
            (maxUpdate: 1) — so a bad easypj-prod-us-east rollout can't
            take out easypj-prod-eu-west in the same step.
         [FLOW DIAGRAM: Progressive sync through environments]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ROLLINGSYNC — easypj DEV → STAGING → PROD                  │
         │                                                            │
         │  ApplicationSet generates 3 Applications, labeled by env:   │
         │  easypj-dev(envLabel=dev)  easypj-staging(envLabel=staging) │
         │  easypj-prod-us-east + easypj-prod-eu-west (envLabel=prod)  │
         │                                                            │
         │  STEP 1  envLabel=dev  maxUpdate=100%                       │
         │  ┌─────────────┐                                            │
         │  │ easypj-dev  │  ██████████ synced immediately, ALL match  │
         │  └──────┬──────┘                                            │
         │         │ wait for Healthy                                  │
         │         ▼                                                   │
         │  STEP 2  envLabel=staging  maxUpdate=50%                    │
         │  ┌─────────────────┐                                        │
         │  │ easypj-staging  │  █████░░░░░ half the matches at a time│
         │  └────────┬────────┘                                        │
         │           │ wait for Healthy                                 │
         │           ▼                                                  │
         │  STEP 3  envLabel=prod  maxUpdate=1                          │
         │  ┌────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────┐          │
         │  │ easypj-prod-us-east│───▶│ easypj-prod-eu-west │          │
         │  │ █░░░░░░░░░ (1st)   │    │ █░░░░░░░░░ (2nd,     │          │
         │  │                     │    │  only after 1st Healthy)│      │
         │  └────────────────────┘    └────────────────────┘          │
         │                                                            │
         │  ⚠️ An Application with NO envLabel matches NO step — it   │
         │  sits un-synced, silently, forever (this section's own      │
         │  gotcha and [M] lab question below).                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — expect a question giving a RollingSync
            steps YAML and asking "why did step 2 never advance?" The
            answer is almost always (a) an Application matches NO
            step's matchExpressions — it's silently excluded and must
            be synced manually, or (b) an earlier step's Applications
            never went Healthy.

    6.3  Notifications and Webhooks
         - Argo CD Notifications: Slack, Email, Webhook, Teams
         📖 KEY TERM — Notifications runs as a SEPARATE controller
            (notifications-controller) that watches Application status
            and evaluates trigger conditions (Go expr-lang) against
            fields like app.status.operationState.phase, firing a
            template through a configured service. ~20 native service
            integrations exist (Slack, Email, Webhook, Teams Workflows,
            PagerDuty, Opsgenie, Grafana, Telegram, Mattermost, Google
            Chat, Rocket.Chat, Pushover, AWS SQS, GCP Pub/Sub, NATS, New
            Relic, Webex, Alertmanager, GitHub commit-status) — Discord
            is NOT one of them (see GOTCHA below).
         - Trigger templates: on-sync-succeeded, on-sync-failed, on-health-degraded
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — a trigger's when: clause is a Go expr-lang
            boolean expression evaluated against the live Application
            object, e.g. app.status.operationState.phase in
            ['Succeeded'] or app.status.health.status == 'Degraded'.
            Since it reads the SAME status object `argocd app get`
            shows you, anything visible in `argocd app get easypj-prod`
            is fair game for a custom trigger condition.
         - Setting up Slack notifications for easypj deployments
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — the ConfigMap defines WHAT can fire and HOW to
            send it, but doesn't attach it to easypj by itself. You
            subscribe an Application via annotation:
              metadata:
                annotations:
                  notifications.argoproj.io/subscribe.on-sync-succeeded.slack: deployments
            Add that to easypj-prod's Application manifest (or via
            `argocd app edit`) and #deployments starts getting pinged —
            no separate "enable" step needed once the ConfigMap exists.
         [CONFIG EXAMPLE: Notification setup]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  ARGO CD NOTIFICATIONS — Alert on Sync Events              │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
          │  │  TRIGGER EVENTS:                                      │ │
          │  │  • on-sync-succeeded → "✅ easypj deployed to prod!"  │ │
          │  │  • on-sync-failed    → "❌ easypj sync FAILED!"       │ │
          │  │  • on-health-degraded→ "⚠️ easypj is unhealthy!"      │ │
          │  │  • on-sync-running   → "🔄 easypj sync in progress"   │ │
          │  │  • on-deleted        → "🗑️ easypj app was deleted!"   │ │
          │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
          │                                                            │
          │  CONFIGURATION:                                            │
          │  argocd-notifications-cm ConfigMap:                        │
          │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
          │  │  service.slack: |                                     │ │
          │  │    token: $slack-token                                │ │
          │  │  template.app-sync-status: |                          │ │
          │  │    message: |                                         │ │
          │  │      App {{.app.metadata.name}} sync is {{.app.status.│ │
          │  │      sync.status}}.                                   │ │
          │  │      {{.app.status.operationState.message}}           │ │
          │  │  trigger.on-sync-succeeded: |                         │ │
          │  │    - description: Notify on successful sync           │ │
          │  │      send: [app-sync-status]                          │ │
          │  │      when: app.status.operationState.phase in         │ │
          │  │        ['Succeeded']                                  │ │
          │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
          │                                                            │
          │  SUPPORTED SERVICES:                                       │
          │  Slack | Email | Webhook | Microsoft Teams | Telegram     │
          │  | Discord | Opsgenie | PagerDuty | Grafana | Pushover    │
          │                                                            │
          │  easypj EXAMPLE:                                           │
          │  • Prod sync succeeded → Slack #deployments channel        │
          │  • Prod sync failed → PagerDuty alert to on-call          │
          │  • Dev health degraded → Discord #dev-team channel        │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — "Discord" in the box above is NOT a native
            Notifications service. To alert Discord, point the generic
            service.webhook config at Discord's incoming-webhook URL —
            same pattern any non-native chat tool uses.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — "Microsoft Teams" is now two services: the legacy
            Office 365 Connectors integration (teams.md, retired
            2026-03-31) and its replacement, Teams Workflows
            (teams-workflows.md). A CAPA question naming the OLD
            connector as "current" would be testing outdated info; know
            Teams Workflows as the one that actually works today.
    6.4  Resource Hooks — PreSync, Sync, PostSync, SyncFail
         - Resource hooks run at specific points in the sync lifecycle
         📖 KEY TERM — a resource hook is any manifest (almost always a
            Job) carrying argocd.argoproj.io/hook: <phase>. Argo CD
            treats it as a LIFECYCLE step, not an ordinary synced
            resource — applied and WATCHED at that phase instead of
            being diffed/pruned normally. There are 5 valid phase
            values, not 4: PreSync, Sync, Skip, PostSync, SyncFail
            (plus PreDelete/PostDelete for Application deletion, added
            v2.10+).
         - PreSync: runs before resources are applied (backups, migrations)
         📖 KEY TERM — Sync and Skip, the 2 values this list doesn't
            name: Sync fires alongside/after normal manifest apply,
            once all PreSync hooks succeed (rarely used explicitly —
            most teams just let ordinary resources apply here
            instead). Skip tells Argo CD to NOT apply that particular
            manifest during sync at all — useful for a resource
            tracked in Git for documentation but actually managed by
            something else (e.g. a Secret provisioned by an
            external-secrets operator).
         - PostSync: runs after resources are applied and healthy (smoke tests)
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — CAPA likes stacking 3-4 hooks across
            phases AND waves and asking you to order them. Full
            precedence: phase first (PreSync -> Sync -> PostSync ->
            SyncFail), THEN sync-wave low-to-high WITHIN that phase,
            THEN kind, THEN name. A PreSync hook at sync-wave: "5"
            still runs before EVERY Sync-phase resource, even one at
            sync-wave: "-5" — phase always beats wave.
         - SyncFail: Cleanup on failure
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — SyncFail fires regardless of WHICH earlier phase
            caused the failure (a failed PreSync hook, a failed
            Sync-phase resource, a degraded health check — all trigger
            it), not just failures late in the pipeline. Also: hooks
            (all 5 types) do NOT run during a selective sync (`argocd
            app sync --resource ...`) — only a full Application sync
            executes them.
         [HOOK LIFECYCLE DIAGRAM: Sync phases with hooks]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  SYNC HOOK LIFECYCLE                                       │
          │                                                            │
          │  TIME ────────────────────────────────────────────────▶    │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌─────────────────┐                                       │
          │  │   PreSync       │ ← DB backup, migrations, schema      │
          │  │   (run FIRST)   │    (if hook fails → sync aborts!)    │
          │  └────────┬────────┘                                       │
          │           │                                                │
          │           ▼                                                │
          │  ┌─────────────────┐                                       │
          │  │   Sync          │ ← Apply all resources (main deploy)  │
          │  │   (normal sync) │                                       │
          │  └────────┬────────┘                                       │
          │           │                                                │
          │           ▼                                                │
          │  ┌─────────────────┐                                       │
          │  │   PostSync      │ ← Smoke tests, notify Slack,         │
          │  │   (run LAST)    │    cache warm-up                     │
          │  └─────────────────┘                                       │
          │                                                            │
          │  IF SYNC FAILS:                                            │
          │  ┌─────────────────┐                                       │
          │  │   SyncFail      │ ← Cleanup partial deployment,        │
          │  │   (on failure)  │    rollback, alert on-call           │
          │  └─────────────────┘                                       │
          │                                                            │
          │  HOW TO SET:                                               │
          │  metadata:                                                 │
          │    annotations:                                            │
          │      argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync                     │
          │      argocd.argoproj.io/hook-delete-policy: HookSucceeded │
          │                                                            │
          │  easypj EXAMPLE — DB Migration Hook:                       │
          │  PreSync Job: python manage.py migrate                     │
          │  → if migration fails, sync stops, no bad deploy!         │
          │  PostSync Job: python manage.py healthcheck               │
          │  → verify app responds before marking healthy            │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — hook-delete-policy: HookSucceeded shown above is
            only 1 of 3 real values:
            • HookSucceeded — delete after the hook completes
              successfully (failures are LEFT for you to debug).
            • HookFailed — delete only if it FAILED (successes are
              left, e.g. to inspect migration output via `kubectl
              logs`).
            • BeforeHookCreation — the DEFAULT if the annotation is
              omitted entirely: the OLD hook resource is deleted right
              before the NEXT sync creates a new one.
            Most real migration Jobs combine two, e.g.
            "BeforeHookCreation,HookSucceeded".
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for a Django+Postgres app like jobpost, set
            restartPolicy: Never and a low backoffLimit (1-2) on the
            PreSync migration Job. Argo CD's hook health check waits on
            the Job's own completion/backoff logic, so a Job that
            retries 6 times before failing means the ENTIRE sync hangs
            6x longer before reporting SyncFail.

    6.5  Sync Windows — Time-Gated Syncing
         - A project-level schedule that allows or blocks syncing during
           specific time windows, regardless of what syncPolicy says
         📖 KEY TERM — configured on the AppProject (5.6 introduced this
            field; this section covers it operationally). Each window
            has a `kind` (`allow` or `deny`), a `schedule` (cron
            syntax), a `duration` (e.g. `1h`), and an `applications` /
            `namespaces` / `clusters` selector (glob patterns) scoping
            which Applications the window applies to.
         [YAML EXAMPLE: a Friday-afternoon prod deploy freeze]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ spec:                                                       │
         │   syncWindows:                                              │
         │   - kind: deny                                              │
         │     schedule: '0 14 * * 5'      # Friday 14:00               │
         │     duration: 4h                                            │
         │     applications: ['easypj-prod-*']                         │
         │     manualSync: false                                       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — precedence when allow and deny windows overlap:
            "if there are any Deny windows active, or the current time
            is outside an Allow window, syncs will be denied" — deny
            ALWAYS wins over an active allow. An overlapping
            allow+deny at the same instant means syncing is blocked,
            not permitted.
         - Manual override: `manualSync: true` inside a deny window
           entry lets an operator still run `argocd app sync` by hand
           during the freeze — auto-sync stays blocked, manual doesn't.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — CLI equivalents for managing windows without
            hand-editing the AppProject YAML:
            `argocd proj windows add easypj --kind deny --schedule
            '0 14 * * 5' --duration 4h --applications 'easypj-prod-*'`,
            `argocd proj windows enable-manual-sync easypj --id 0`,
            `argocd proj windows list easypj`.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — this is a DIFFERENT mechanism from Ch3's
            `syncPolicy.automated` (which controls WHETHER an
            Application auto-syncs at all) — Sync Windows control WHEN,
            at the project level, across every Application it scopes,
            regardless of each Application's own automated setting.

    6.6  Selective Sync & Skipping Reconciliation
         - Three distinct mechanisms that all sound like "sync less than
           everything," each with different scope and side effects
         [TABLE: Selective-sync mechanisms compared]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  MECHANISM          │ SCOPE      │ RECORDED IN HISTORY?     │
         │  ────────────────── │ ────────── │ ──────────────────────  │
         │  --resource CLI flag│ one-time,  │ NO — can't `argocd app   │
         │  on `app sync`      │ manual     │ rollback` to a           │
         │                     │            │ selective-sync state     │
         │  ────────────────── │ ────────── │ ──────────────────────  │
         │  SelectiveSync=true │ persistent │ YES — a normal recorded  │
         │  sync option        │ policy     │ sync, just resource-     │
         │                     │            │ filtered by label        │
         │  ────────────────── │ ────────── │ ──────────────────────  │
         │  IgnoreExtraneous   │ per-       │ N/A — this is a compare- │
         │  compare-option     │ resource   │ option (4.8), not a sync │
         │  (4.8)              │            │ operation at all         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         📖 KEY TERM — `argocd app sync easypj-prod --resource
            apps:Deployment:django` targets one Kind/name for THIS sync
            invocation only. Official docs flag the consequence
            directly: because it's not a full sync, "this operation
            will not be recorded in the sync history" — there is no
            rollback target for it.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `SelectiveSync=true` (a real, persistent
            `syncPolicy.syncOptions` entry) is a completely different
            thing from the `--resource` flag above despite the similar
            name: it changes HOW a normal, fully-recorded sync applies
            resources (Kind/name-sorted, one at a time) rather than
            picking a subset — don't confuse "selective" the sync
            option with "selective" the CLI flag's colloquial
            description.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — reach for `--resource` only for an emergency,
            one-off "just this Deployment, right now" fix — for
            anything you'd want to `argocd app rollback` later, use a
            real sync (letting Argo CD apply everything, or scope via
            `ignoreDifferences`/`compare-options` at the manifest level
            instead).

    6.7  Git Webhook Configuration — Push-Triggered Sync
         - The `/api/webhook` endpoint on argocd-server itself — DO NOT
           confuse this with Argo Events' own separate GitHub
           EventSource (Ch18-19), which is a different component
           entirely with a different purpose (triggering Workflows, not
           Argo CD Application syncs)
         📖 KEY TERM — this webhook exists purely to shortcut the
            `timeout.reconciliation` polling interval (3.10) from ~180s
            worst-case down to near-instant: a push notifies
            argocd-server directly, which triggers an immediate refresh
            for any Application tracking that repo/revision — polling
            still runs as the fallback safety net either way.
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — provider-specific shared-secret keys, all
            set in the `argocd-secret` Secret: `webhook.github.secret`,
            `webhook.gitlab.secret`, `webhook.bitbucket.secret` (UUID,
            not HMAC, on Bitbucket Cloud), `webhook.bitbucketserver.
            secret`, `webhook.gogs.secret`. Each provider signs its
            payload differently — GitHub/GitLab use an HMAC header Argo
            CD verifies against the matching secret key before trusting
            the payload.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `webhook.maxPayloadSizeMB` defaults to 50MB. A
            monorepo webhook payload listing an unusually large number
            of changed files in one push can exceed this and be
            rejected outright — raise the ConfigMap key rather than
            assuming webhooks "just don't work sometimes" for large
            pushes.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — pointing easypj's GitHub repo webhook at
            `https://<argocd-server-host>/api/webhook` with the matching
            `webhook.github.secret` is what makes a `git push` to
            easypj-k8s show up as OutOfSync in the UI within seconds,
            instead of waiting out the ~120s+60s jitter reconciliation
            timer from 3.10.

    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How the ApplicationSet controller reconciles
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    The ApplicationSet controller runs a watch/reconcile loop separate
    from the core Argo CD application-controller:

    1. WATCH — it watches every ApplicationSet object, and watches each
       generator's data source (Git repo, cluster-Secret list, SCM API,
       etc.) on a poll interval or webhook push.
    2. GENERATE — each generator in spec.generators produces a list of
       parameter maps. If more than one generator is combined
       (Matrix/Merge), the controller computes the product/merge FIRST.
    3. RENDER — each parameter map Go-template-renders spec.template
       into a concrete Application manifest.
    4. DIFF & APPLY — the controller diffs the rendered Application set
       against what exists in the argocd namespace and creates/updates/
       deletes Application objects to match — a mini GitOps loop, ONE
       LEVEL UP from the Application-to-live-cluster loop (Ch.4).
    5. HANDOFF — once an Application object exists, the ORDINARY Argo
       CD application-controller takes over syncing IT, running any
       resource hooks (6.4). The ApplicationSet controller never syncs
       manifests itself.
    6. If RollingSync is enabled, step 4's updates are throttled: only
       Applications matching the CURRENT step's matchExpressions are
       updated, and the controller polls their health before unlocking
       the next step.

    MENTAL MODEL — the ApplicationSet controller is a "factory that
    stamps out Application objects"; the ordinary application-controller
    is the "worker that syncs each stamped Application to its cluster."

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - ApplicationSets have 9 generators, not 7 — don't forget Cluster
      Decision Resource and Plugin.
    - Git generator has 2 sub-modes (files vs directories).
    - RollingSync is a Beta feature that must be explicitly enabled and
      forces autosync off on every app it manages.
    - Resource hooks: PreSync/Sync/PostSync/SyncFail/Skip — 5 values.
      hook-delete-policy has 3 values, not 1.
    - Ordering = phase, then sync-wave (low first), then kind, then
      name.
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: expect a scenario question picking the correct
       generator, and one completing a hook annotation block.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Matrix combines exactly TWO generators — nest Matrices for 3+.
    2. A hook with no hook-delete-policy is NOT cleaned up after it
       runs — it lingers until the next sync replaces it.
    3. Sync waves order resources WITHIN one Application's sync;
       RollingSync orders MULTIPLE Applications. Don't answer a
       "stage dev->staging->prod" question with "sync waves."
    4. If a PreSync hook fails, the ENTIRE sync aborts. If a PostSync
       hook fails after resources are healthy, live resources are NOT
       rolled back automatically.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - `argocd appset generate <file>.yaml` — dry-run render an
      ApplicationSet's output Applications without applying anything.
    - One ApplicationSet with a List generator beats N hand-written
      Applications, almost always fewer keystrokes.
    - `kubectl logs job/<hook-name>` is faster than the UI for a
      one-off hook test.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ─────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ argocd appset create <file>.yaml                                │
    │ argocd appset list                                              │
    │ argocd appset get <name>                                        │
    │ argocd appset generate <file>.yaml   # dry-run                  │
    │ argocd appset delete <name>                                     │
    │ kubectl get jobs -n easypj-prod -l argocd.argoproj.io/hook-type │
    │ argocd app get easypj-prod --show-operation                     │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~15 min, do-it-now)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    1. Write a List-generator ApplicationSet deploying easypj to
       dev/staging/prod using 3 elements (5 min).
    2. Add a PreSync hook Job that runs `python manage.py migrate` for
       jobpost, with hook-delete-policy: HookSucceeded (5 min).
    3. Add a PostSync hook Job that curls easypj's /health/ endpoint,
       sync-wave: "1" (5 min).
    Success check: argocd app get easypj-dev --show-operation shows all
    3 hooks Succeeded, in order.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. easypj needs deployment to 3 registered clusters, new clusters
        auto-picked-up with zero YAML changes. Which generator?
    A1. Cluster generator.
    Explanation: it watches Argo CD's own cluster-Secret list; a new
    cluster auto-creates a new Application with no edit.

    Q2. Auto-create an Application for every repo tagged easypj-team in
        GitHub, no manual onboarding.
    A2. SCM Provider generator.
    Explanation: Cluster Decision Resource picks CLUSTERS, not repos —
    wrong axis for this scenario.

    Q3. Annotation block that runs a DB migration Job BEFORE any other
        resource, aborting the sync if it fails.
    A3. argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync +
        argocd.argoproj.io/hook-delete-policy: HookSucceeded
    Explanation: PreSync runs first; if it fails, Sync never starts.

    Q4. Two PreSync Jobs, one loads config, one migrates and depends on
        the config. How do you guarantee order?
    A4. sync-wave: "-1" on the config loader, "0" on the migration —
        both still hook: PreSync.
    Explanation: within the same phase, ordering falls back to
    sync-wave (lower first).

    Q5. 40 db-migrate-* Jobs still present after 40 syncs. What's
        misconfigured?
    A5. Missing hook-delete-policy annotation.
    Explanation: with no policy, Argo CD only deletes the OLD hook
    right before creating a new one — never immediately after it runs.

    Q6. True/False: RollingSync is enabled by default.
    A6. False.
    Explanation: it's a Beta feature requiring explicit enablement.

    Q7. A generated Application has no envLabel label at all, but the
        RollingSync steps match on envLabel. What happens?
    A7. It's skipped by the rollout entirely and must be synced
        manually.
    Explanation: RollingSync only advances Applications matching a
    step's matchExpressions.

    Q8. Which generator spins up a temporary preview environment when a
        PR opens, and tears it down when it closes?
    A8. Pull Request generator.
    Explanation: it polls the SCM's PR API; when the PR closes, normal
    ApplicationSet garbage-collection removes the generated Application.

    Q9. Which of PreSync/Sync/Wait/PostSync/SyncFail/Skip is NOT a
        valid hook value?
    A9. Wait.
    Explanation: valid set is PreSync, Sync, Skip, PostSync, SyncFail
    (plus newer PreDelete/PostDelete).

    Q10. A PostSync smoke-test hook fails after resources already went
         Healthy. Does Argo CD roll back the Deployment?
    A10. No, not automatically.
    Explanation: hook failure marks the operation Failed/Degraded but
    doesn't auto-revert already-applied live resources.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY — one picture, the whole chapter
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  easypj-appset.yaml (6.1: 9 generators available)                │
    │        │ generates                                               │
    │        v                                                         │
    │  [easypj-dev] [easypj-staging] [easypj-prod]   <- N Applications  │
    │        │              │              │                            │
    │        +--------------+--------------+                            │
    │              RollingSync (6.2, Beta, opt-in)                      │
    │        step1: dev(100%) -Healthy?-> step2: staging(50%)           │
    │                              -Healthy?-> step3: prod(1 at a time) │
    │        │                                                         │
    │        v   EACH Application's sync individually gated by:        │
    │  PreSync(migrate,wave -1) -> Sync(apply mfsts) -> PostSync(smoke) │
    │        │            if FAIL                                       │
    │        v                          v                                │
    │        │                    SyncFail(rollback/page)                │
    │        v                                                         │
    │  Notifications (6.3): Slack/Teams Workflows/PagerDuty fire        │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: One ApplicationSet stamps out an Application per
    environment. RollingSync controls the ORDER those Applications get
    promoted in. Inside each individual sync, resource hooks gate
    safety. Every state change is broadcast through Notifications.


Chapter 7: easypj GitOps Setup
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~3 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo CD 34% · 🏢 Daily-Work: very high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    Every chapter so far taught you Argo CD's individual LEGO bricks —
    Applications, AppProjects, sync waves, ApplicationSets, hooks. This
    chapter is where you finally build the actual house: the real
    easypj-k8s Git repository that will run easypj in dev, staging, AND
    prod, wired end-to-end so that `kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml`
    — one command — stands up your entire cluster, and kubeseal keeps
    your Django SECRET_KEY and Postgres password out of Git forever.

    ┌─────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE (manual, no GitOps) │  AFTER (this chapter's setup)│
    ├─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
    │  3 envs = 3 sets of hand-   │  3 envs = 1 base + 3 thin    │
    │  edited full YAML files,    │  overlays; changing a shared │
    │  drifting apart silently    │  field touches ONE file      │
    │  DB password sits in a      │  Only a SealedSecret          │
    │  .env file or a raw Secret  │  ciphertext (or an            │
    │  committed to Git            │  ExternalSecret reference)   │
    │                              │  ever touches Git             │
    └─────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES
    ─────────────────────────
      - Lay out a GitOps repo using the base+overlays convention, and
        explain WHY "base"/"overlays" are convention, not a Kustomize
        requirement.
      - Choose Kustomize vs Helm for a given easypj scenario — and know
        when they're not either/or (helmCharts:).
      - Write complete, syncPolicy-correct Argo CD Application manifests
        for easypj-dev, easypj-staging, and easypj-prod.
      - Design an App-of-Apps root Application that bootstraps all three
        environments from a single kubectl apply.
      - Seal a real Kubernetes Secret with kubeseal using the CORRECT
        flags (--format=yaml) and explain the "strict" scope gotcha.
      - Decide between Sealed Secrets, External Secrets Operator, and
        Vault for easypj's secrets.

    [D] CAPA RELEVANCE
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Official CAPA Competency          │  Covered in this chapter by     │
    ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
