    │  multi-environment patterns         │  to 6.1 ApplicationSets)        │
    │  Manage sensitive data securely     │  7.5 (Sealed Secrets, ESO,      │
    │                                      │  Vault)                          │
    └────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┘
    Domain weight: Argo CD = 34% of the CAPA exam — this chapter is the
    single most "graded" chapter in Part 2.

    [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM
    ────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 7 MAP — building easypj-k8s, end to end                    │
    │                                                                      │
    │   7.1 Repo Structure        7.2 Kustomize vs Helm                   │
    │   (base + overlays)   --->  (pick your tool per overlay)            │
    │         │                            │                              │
    │         v                            v                              │
    │   7.3 Application YAMLs  <-----------+                              │
    │   (dev / staging / prod, one per environment)                       │
    │         │                                                           │
    │         v                                                           │
    │   7.4 App-of-Apps  (bootstrap all 3 from ONE root Application)      │
    │         │                                                           │
    │         v                                                           │
    │   7.5 Secrets Management  (keep secrets out of every file the       │
    │         previous 4 sections just created)                           │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.1  GitOps Repo Structure
         - Base + Overlays pattern (dev/staging/prod)
         - Directory layout best practices
         📖 KEY TERM — Kustomize's own glossary: an "overlay" is just
            another kustomization that refers to a base and refers to
            patches applied to it; a "base" is whatever an overlay
            refers to; a "variant" is the actual OUTPUT — the flat
            resource list — produced once an overlay is applied to a
            base. dev/staging/prod below are three variants of the
            SAME base, not three copies of it.
         [DIRECTORY TREE: easypj-k8s repo structure]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  easypj-k8s/                   ← GitOps repository         │
         │  ├── base/                     ← Common configuration     │
         │  │   ├── deployment.yaml                                  │
         │  │   ├── service.yaml                                      │
         │  │   ├── configmap.yaml                                    │
         │  │   ├── secret.yaml                                       │
         │  │   └── kustomization.yaml                               │
         │  ├── overlays/                ← Environment-specific       │
         │  │   ├── dev/                                             │
         │  │   │   ├── kustomization.yaml                            │
         │  │   │   └── configmap-patch.yaml                         │
         │  │   ├── staging/                                         │
         │  │   │   ├── kustomization.yaml                            │
         │  │   │   ├── replica-count.yaml                            │
         │  │   │   └── configmap-patch.yaml                         │
         │  │   └── prod/                                            │
         │  │       ├── kustomization.yaml                            │
         │  │       ├── replica-count.yaml                            │
         │  │       ├── resource-limits.yaml                          │
         │  │       └── configmap-patch.yaml                         │
         │  ├── argo-apps/               ← Argo CD Application YAMLs │
         │  │   ├── easypj-dev.yaml                                   │
         │  │   ├── easypj-staging.yaml                               │
         │  │   └── easypj-prod.yaml                                 │
         │  └── app-of-apps.yaml         ← Bootstrap Application     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the tree names the file base/secret.yaml, but 7.5
            is explicit that a plain v1 Secret must never reach Git. In
            a real easypj-k8s repo this file's `kind:` is SealedSecret
            (ciphertext), not Secret — "secret.yaml" here is a base-
            relative FILENAME, not a claim about its contents. Some
            teams rename it sealed-secret.yaml for exactly this reason.
            Always confirm by `kind:`, never by filename.
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — overlays/*/kustomization.yaml resolves
            ../../base through Argo CD's OWN checked-out copy of the
            repo on repo-server, never your local filesystem. Since
            Argo CD 2.5, repo-server refuses to follow any symlink that
            resolves outside the checkout root — hardening added after
            a real CVE class where a malicious commit's symlink could
            leak out-of-bounds YAML/JSON from OTHER Applications'
            source repos. A real relative path like ../../base works;
            a symlink pointing at /etc or a sibling repo is blocked.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — easypj-k8s being its OWN repository (separate
            from wherever easypj's Django application source lives) is
            not just this guide's convention: Argo CD's own Best
            Practices page states "Using a separate Git repository to
            hold your Kubernetes manifests, keeping the config separate
            from your application source code, is highly recommended."
            Concretely for easypj: a Django code commit never
            accidentally triggers an Argo CD sync (no manifest
            changed), easypj-k8s's commit history stays 100% about
            WHAT'S DEPLOYED (useful for audits), and the app repo's CI
            never needs cluster credentials or push access to
            easypj-k8s.

    7.2  Kustomize vs Helm for easypj
         - When to use Kustomize (simpler, YAML-only, overlay model)
         - When to use Helm (more complex, packaged, templated)
         - Recommendation for easypj: Kustomize for simplicity
         🧠 ANALOGY — Kustomize's patches are sticky notes on an
            unmodified original: base/deployment.yaml never changes,
            and each overlay just adds Post-its (a replica count here,
            an image tag there) on top, removable without touching the
            original page. Helm's templating is closer to a form
            letter with blanks ({{ .Values.replicaCount }}) filled in
            fresh on every render — once rendered there's no "original"
            left to inspect, only the template plus the values that
            filled it.
         [COMPARISON TABLE: Kustomize vs Helm]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  KUSTOMIZE vs HELM — Which for easypj?                    │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────┐   │
         │  │ FEATURE      │ KUSTOMIZE        │ HELM             │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Learning     │ 🟢 Easy (just    │ 🟡 Moderate      │   │
         │  │ Curve        │ YAML patching)   │ (Go templates)   │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Templating   │ ❌ No templates  │ ✅ Go templates  │   │
         │  │              │ (patch-overlay)  │ ({{ .Values }}) │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Environment  │ ✅ Overlay files │ ✅ values.yaml   │   │
         │  │ Management   │ base + patches   │ per environment  │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Built-in K8s │ ✅ kubectl kustomize │ ❌ Need Helm │   │
         │  │ Support      │ (no extra tools) │ CLI installed    │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Packaging &  │ ❌ Not designed  │ ✅ Chart repos   │   │
         │  │ Distribution │ for sharing      │ (Artifact Hub)   │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Versioning   │ ✅ Git (branches │ ✅ Chart version │   │
         │  │              │ + tags)          │ + appVersion     │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ Best For     │ Internal apps,   │ Shared/packaged  │   │
         │  │              │ simple configs,  │ apps, complex    │   │
         │  │              │ small teams      │ templating needs │   │
         │  ├──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────┤   │
         │  │ easypj RECO  │ ✅ YES — Simple  │ Maybe later if   │   │
         │  │              │ Django app with  │ sharing with     │   │
         │  │              │ env variations   │ other teams      │   │
         │  └──────────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────┘   │
         │                                                            │
         │  HOW ARGO CD USES THEM:                                    │
         │  Kustomize: source.path → runs kustomize build            │
         │  Helm: source.path + helm.values → helm template          │
         │  Both produce plain YAML that Argo CD applies to cluster  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Argo CD's Kustomize detection is fully
            automatic: if source.path contains a kustomization.yaml,
            Argo CD renders it with kustomize — no spec.source.kustomize
            block is required just to use Kustomize (you only add that
            block to override something: pin a version, set images:,
            or pass extra buildOptions). That's why 7.3's three
            Application manifests below have no kustomize: stanza at
            all and still work.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `kubectl kustomize overlays/prod` on your laptop
            and what Argo CD's repo-server actually renders CAN differ:
            repo-server ships its own bundled kustomize binary, which
            may trail the CLI version installed locally. If a patch
            behaves differently in Argo CD than in your terminal, check
            versions before assuming a YAML bug. Pin one exactly:
            register it in argocd-cm as
            `kustomize.path.v5.x.x: /custom-tools/kustomize_5_x_x`,
            then set `spec.source.kustomize.version: v5.x.x` per
            Application (or `argocd app set easypj-prod
            --kustomize-version v5.x.x`).

    7.3  Creating Argo CD Applications for easypj
         - Application YAML for dev, staging, prod
         - Setting up auto-sync with pruning
         - Health check configuration for Django
         [COMPLETE YAML: Three Application manifests]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD APPLICATIONS — easypj-dev / staging / prod          │
         │                                                              │
         │  # DEV — fast feedback, safe to prune+selfHeal               │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                            │
         │  kind: Application                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: easypj-dev                                          │
         │    namespace: argocd                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    project: easypj-dev   # AppProject, not "default" (Ch 5)  │
         │    source:                                                   │
         │      repoURL: https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s      │
         │      targetRevision: main                                    │
         │      path: overlays/dev                                      │
         │    destination:                                              │
         │      server: https://kubernetes.default.svc                  │
         │      namespace: easypj-dev                                   │
         │    syncPolicy:                                               │
         │      automated:                                              │
         │        prune: true                                           │
         │        selfHeal: true                                        │
         │      syncOptions:                                            │
         │        - CreateNamespace=true                                │
         │  ---                                                         │
         │  # STAGING — same policy; CI is the gate, not a human        │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                            │
         │  kind: Application                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: easypj-staging                                      │
         │    namespace: argocd                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    project: easypj-staging                                   │
         │    source:                                                   │
         │      repoURL: https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s      │
         │      targetRevision: main                                    │
         │      path: overlays/staging                                  │
         │    destination:                                              │
         │      server: https://kubernetes.default.svc                  │
         │      namespace: easypj-staging                               │
         │    syncPolicy:                                               │
         │      automated:                                              │
         │        prune: true                                           │
         │        selfHeal: true                                        │
         │      syncOptions:                                            │
         │        - CreateNamespace=true                                │
         │  ---                                                         │
         │  # PROD — selfHeal, no auto-prune (see [H] takeaway)         │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                            │
         │  kind: Application                                           │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: easypj-prod                                         │
         │    namespace: argocd                                         │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    project: easypj-prod                                      │
         │    source:                                                   │
         │      repoURL: https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s      │
         │      targetRevision: main                                    │
         │      path: overlays/prod                                     │
         │    destination:                                              │
         │      server: https://kubernetes.default.svc                  │
         │      namespace: easypj-prod                                  │
         │    syncPolicy:                                               │
         │      automated:                                              │
         │        selfHeal: true      # NOT prune: true — see [H]       │
         │      syncOptions:                                            │
         │        - CreateNamespace=true                                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         📖 KEY TERM — "Health check configuration for Django" is NOT
            an Application-spec field. Argo CD's built-in Deployment
            health check is generation-based: Healthy once
            status.observedGeneration == metadata.generation AND
            status.updatedReplicas == spec.replicas. Django has no
            built-in health endpoint of its own — the real "health
            check configuration" lives in base/deployment.yaml's
            readinessProbe (e.g. httpGet: {path: /healthz/, port:
            8000}, wired to a view from django-health-check or a small
            custom endpoint). Argo CD just reads that probe's result;
            it configures nothing Django-specific itself.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — omit spec.project, or typo the name, and Argo CD
            does NOT error at apply time — the Application silently
            lands in the wide-open built-in "default" project (Ch 5).
            That's why all three manifests above explicitly set
            project: easypj-{dev,staging,prod} instead of copying the
            project: default used in this guide's earlier generic
            examples — easypj already has real, restrictive AppProjects
            from 5.2; using "default" here would silently throw away
            every guardrail that chapter built.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — why prod above sets only selfHeal, never
            prune: a bad rebase or a manifest Kustomize silently stops
            picking up auto-prunes in dev/staging within minutes —
            annoying but cheap to notice and re-apply. The identical
            mistake in prod, with prune: true, deletes a live
            Deployment or Service before anyone reviews a diff.
            selfHeal alone still fixes drift (a manual kubectl edit
            gets reverted) — it just never DELETES a resource
            automatically; that stays a deliberate human action
            (`argocd app sync easypj-prod --prune`).
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — all three manifests above pin targetRevision: main,
            including easypj-prod. Argo CD's own Best Practices page
            warns manifests at a moving Git ref are NOT truly
            immutable: "When using templating tools like helm or
            kustomize, it is possible for manifests to change their
            meaning from one day to the next" even with zero local
            diff, because base/ or a chart dependency changed
            underneath you. The officially recommended fix is a Git
            tag or commit SHA for targetRevision — e.g.
            targetRevision: v1.4.2 or a pinned SHA — for prod at
            minimum. main is shown above for teaching simplicity; a
            real easypj-prod Application should not track a branch
            HEAD indefinitely.

    7.4  The App-of-Apps Pattern
         - One Application that deploys all other Applications
         - Bootstrap your entire cluster from a single kubectl apply
         🧠 ANALOGY — App-of-Apps is a packing list that itself
            contains other packing lists, not a container holding the
            actual items. The root Application never touches a
            Deployment or Service directly — it only ever manages
            OTHER Application objects. Every real Kubernetes resource
            (easypj's Deployment, Service, ConfigMap) is owned by a
            CHILD Application, exactly as if you'd created that child
            by hand.
         [DIAGRAM: App-of-Apps bootstrapping entire cluster]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  APP-OF-APPS PATTERN — Bootstrap Everything                │
         │                                                            │
         │  STEP 1: Apply ONE root Application                        │
         │  kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml                         │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  app-of-apps.yaml (Root Application)                │   │
         │  │  source.path: argo-apps/                            │   │
         │  │  → Points to folder containing all app YAMLs        │   │
         │  └────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                       │                                    │
         │                       ▼                                    │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │        ARGO CD RECONCILES & GENERATES               │   │
         │  └────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                       │                                    │
         │     ┌─────────────────┼─────────────────┐                 │
         │     │                 │                 │                  │
         │     ▼                 ▼                 ▼                  │
         │  ┌────────┐    ┌────────────┐    ┌──────────┐            │
         │  │easypj- │    │easypj-     │    │easypj-   │            │
         │  │dev     │    │staging     │    │prod      │            │
         │  │App     │    │App         │    │App       │            │
         │  └───┬────┘    └─────┬──────┘    └────┬─────┘            │
         │      │               │                │                   │
         │      ▼               ▼                ▼                   │
         │  ┌────────┐    ┌────────────┐    ┌──────────┐            │
         │  │Deploys:│    │Deploys:    │    │Deploys:  │            │
         │  │easypj  │    │easypj      │    │easypj    │            │
         │  │to dev  │    │to staging  │    │to prod   │            │
         │  │ns      │    │ns          │    │ns        │            │
         │  └────────┘    └────────────┘    └──────────┘            │
         │                                                            │
         │  ONE COMMAND = ENTIRE CLUSTER BOOTSTRAPPED!                │
         │  • Disaster recovery: kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml   │
         │  • New cluster: same command, everything deployed          │
         │  • Git revert = full cluster rollback!                    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — "ONE COMMAND = ENTIRE CLUSTER BOOTSTRAPPED!" above
            quietly assumes app-of-apps.yaml's OWN spec.syncPolicy.
            automated is set. The root Application is reconciled by
            the exact same loop as any other — leave it on manual sync
            and `kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml` creates ONE
            OutOfSync Application and nothing cascades until a human
            runs `argocd app sync app-of-apps`. Give the root
            Application its own automated: block if the one-command
            promise needs to be literal, not aspirational.

    7.5  Secrets Management in GitOps
         - NEVER store plaintext secrets in Git!
         - Solutions: Sealed Secrets, External Secrets Operator, Vault
         - Setting up Sealed Secrets for easypj database password
         📖 KEY TERM — SealedSecret: a CustomResource whose ciphertext
            was produced with ASYMMETRIC encryption — kubeseal uses
            only the controller's PUBLIC certificate to encrypt, and
            the original author is not able to obtain the original
            Secret back from the SealedSecret once sealed. Only the
            sealed-secrets-controller holds the matching private key
            (itself just a normal k8s Secret in its own namespace) and
            can decrypt it back into a real v1 Secret in-cluster. This
            is WHY it's safe to commit: possessing the ciphertext, or
            even the public cert used to make it, grants zero ability
            to read it back.
         [WORKFLOW DIAGRAM: Sealed Secrets encryption flow]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SECRETS MANAGEMENT — GitOps-Safe Approach                 │
         │                                                            │
         │  OPTION A: SEALED SECRETS (simple, Git-safe)               │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  1. Dev creates plain Secret YAML locally:          │   │
         │  │     kubectl create secret generic db-password \     │   │
         │  │       --from-literal=password=supersecret \         │   │
         │  │       --dry-run=client -o yaml > secret.yaml       │   │
         │  │                                                     │   │
         │  │  2. Encrypt with kubeseal:                          │   │
         │  │     kubeseal --format=yaml < secret.yaml \         │   │
         │  │       > sealed-secret.yaml                          │   │
         │  │     # sealed-secret.yaml is SAFE to commit to Git! │   │
         │  │                                                     │   │
         │  │  3. Commit sealed-secret.yaml to Git repo          │   │
         │  │                                                     │   │
         │  │  4. Argo CD applies it to cluster                  │   │
         │  │     → Sealed Secrets controller decrypts           │   │
         │  │     → Creates actual K8s Secret in cluster         │   │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                            │
         │  OPTION B: EXTERNAL SECRETS OPERATOR (ESO)                 │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  ExternalSecret CRD → fetches from AWS/Azure/GCP/  │   │
         │  │  Vault → creates K8s Secret                         │   │
         │  │  Git stores ExternalSecret YAML (no actual secrets) │   │
         │  │  Best for: cloud-native environments               │   │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                            │
         │  OPTION C: HASHICORP VAULT (enterprise)                    │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
         │  │  Argo CD → Vault plugin → inject secrets at sync   │   │
         │  │  Most secure, but most complex                     │   │
         │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
         │                                                            │
         │  easypj RECOMMENDATION: Sealed Secrets for simplicity      │
         │  (encrypt db-password, django-secret-key, API keys)        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — kubeseal's --format defaults to json, not yaml —
            the corrected command above (--format=yaml) matters because
            the file EXTENSION alone (".yaml") never changes the actual
            content; without the flag you'd get a single-line JSON
            SealedSecret saved under a .yaml filename.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for easypj's Postgres password specifically,
            Sealed Secrets vs ESO is a real operational tradeoff, not
            just a feature checklist: Sealed Secrets needs zero
            external infra (fine for a learning project or one small
            cluster), but the decryption key lives IN that cluster —
            lose the sealed-secrets-controller's private key without a
            backup and every SealedSecret in Git becomes permanently
            unrecoverable ciphertext. ESO instead defers storage to
            AWS Secrets Manager/Vault/etc., so the cluster holds no
            long-term secret material at all — worth the switch once
            easypj moves from a learning cluster to a real incident
            response plan. A minimal easypj ExternalSecret (current
            stable API):
              apiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1
              kind: ExternalSecret
              metadata: {name: easypj-db-password, namespace: easypj-prod}
              spec:
                secretStoreRef: {name: aws-secrets-manager, kind: SecretStore}
                target: {name: easypj-db-password}
                data:
                  - secretKey: password
                    remoteRef: {key: prod/easypj/db-password}
         📖 KEY TERM — Option C above ("Argo CD → Vault plugin → inject
            secrets at sync") describes argocd-vault-plugin, a Config
            Management Plugin that renders secret VALUES into the
            manifest at generation time. Argo CD's own Secret
            Management page flags this whole family as the riskier
            one: "Argo CD stores generated manifests in plaintext in
            its Redis cache," so a CMP-rendered Secret sits in Redis
            unencrypted, at least briefly. The officially preferred
            Vault integration is instead the Vault Secrets Operator —
            a "destination cluster" approach like Sealed Secrets/ESO in
            Options A/B, where Argo CD never sees the secret value at
            all; a CRD in easypj-prod's own namespace does the
            fetching, independent of any Argo CD sync.

    7.6  Safely Deleting easypj Applications
         - Cascading deletion (default): the Application AND every
           resource it manages are removed
         - Non-cascading (orphan) deletion: only the Application object
           is removed; Deployments/Services/ConfigMaps keep running
         - Foreground vs background cascading, and the exact finalizer
           string that controls which one happens
         📖 KEY TERM — cascading delete is controlled by ONE finalizer,
            already named in [G].9 below: metadata.finalizers:
            [resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io]. Its PRESENCE (not
            a separate flag) is what tells the application-controller to
            delete easypj-dev's Deployment/Service/ConfigMap when the
            Application object itself is deleted. Remove that finalizer
            and deleting the Application ONLY deletes the Application —
            every resource it managed keeps running, now unmanaged, in
            easypj-dev's namespace.
         [TABLE: three ways to delete an easypj Application]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  DELETING easypj-staging — pick ONE propagation behavior    │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌───────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │ MODE           │ WHAT HAPPENS                       │    │
         │  ├───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤    │
         │  │ Foreground     │ finalizer: resources-finalizer.     │    │
         │  │ cascading      │ argocd.argoproj.io (DEFAULT) —      │    │
         │  │ (default)      │ Application object stays until      │    │
         │  │                │ every managed resource is actually  │    │
         │  │                │ gone                                │    │
         │  ├───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤    │
         │  │ Background     │ finalizer suffix /background —      │    │
         │  │ cascading      │ Application object disappears       │    │
         │  │                │ immediately; resources deleted      │    │
         │  │                │ afterward, out of view              │    │
         │  ├───────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤    │
         │  │ Non-cascading  │ no finalizer (or removed first) —   │    │
         │  │ (orphan)       │ ONLY the Application object is      │    │
         │  │                │ deleted; Deployment/Service/        │    │
         │  │                │ ConfigMap in easypj-staging are     │    │
         │  │                │ left running, now unmanaged         │    │
         │  └───────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                              │
         │  CLI:                                                       │
         │  argocd app delete easypj-staging        (cascades,        │
         │                                            foreground,       │
         │                                            default)          │
         │  argocd app delete easypj-staging --cascade=false           │
         │                                            (orphan)          │
         │                                                              │
         │  kubectl-native: patch/remove the finalizer on the           │
         │  Application object BEFORE `kubectl delete application` to  │
         │  get the same effect without the argocd CLI installed.      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — this is the OTHER side of the App-of-Apps GOTCHA in
            7.4/[G].9 below: deleting easypj-staging.yaml from
            argo-apps/ and letting the root Application prune it only
            cascades to easypj-staging's own Deployment/Service if THAT
            child manifest itself carries the finalizer. A child
            Application written without it (easy to forget when
            hand-writing three near-identical YAMLs, as in 7.3) silently
            becomes an orphan delete, not a cascading one — the
            Deployment keeps serving traffic in a namespace Argo CD no
            longer tracks.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for easypj-prod specifically, prefer BACKGROUND
            over foreground when you must delete: foreground blocks the
            `argocd app delete` call (and any CI pipeline step wrapping
            it) until every last resource confirms deleted, which can
            hang on one stuck-terminating Pod; background returns
            immediately and finishes deletion asynchronously. Before
            EITHER cascading mode against easypj-prod, list the
            Application's managed resources first (`argocd app
            resources easypj-prod`, or the Resource Tree in the UI) —
            Argo CD's own docs are blunt about this: always verify what
            resources are managed by the application before performing
            a cascading delete.
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — Argo CD 3.2+ unified this in the UI: the
            Applications List and the Resource Tree now present the
            SAME propagation-policy dialog on delete, instead of one
            view defaulting to one behavior and the other to another.


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — the App-of-Apps cascade, step by step
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    1. `kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml` creates exactly ONE
       Application in the argocd namespace. Nothing else exists yet.
    2. The application-controller's shared informer sees it and
       enqueues a reconcile — same code path as any Application.
    3. Reconcile calls argocd-repo-server to fetch source.path
       (argo-apps/) and generate manifests — since that path just
       contains plain Application YAML files, this is nearly a no-op.
    4. The controller diffs those generated manifests (N child
       Application objects) against live state -> OutOfSync initially.
    5. With automated sync, the controller applies the N child
       Application objects.
    6. The SAME informer picks THEM up too — no special "bootstrap
       mode." Each child's repo-server call now actually runs
       `kustomize build overlays/dev` etc., syncing real
       Deployments/Services/ConfigMaps into easypj-dev/-staging/-prod.
    7. Ordering: child Application sync-wave annotations are respected
       by the root app's own sync operation.
    8. Continuous drift detection: if a human deletes a child
       Application object, the root app's next reconcile detects it's
       missing and (with selfHeal) recreates it.
    9. Deletion cascade requires opting in: pruning the root app
       removes the child Application OBJECT. Whether that ALSO deletes
       the Deployment/Service depends on whether that child manifest
       carries metadata.finalizers:
       [resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io].
    10. This recurses arbitrarily — "child" and "parent" aren't special
        types; every Application is reconciled by the same loop.

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - base/overlays is a NAMING CONVENTION Kustomize doesn't enforce —
      only a resolvable kustomization.yaml is required.
    - Argo CD's repo-server runs its OWN bundled kustomize, never
      kubectl's.
    - An Application's syncPolicy should differ per environment — dev
      can self-heal AND prune; prod often wants selfHeal without prune.
    - App-of-Apps bootstraps a whole cluster from one Application, but
      is admin-only by RBAC design and needs an explicit finalizer for
      cascading delete.
    - ApplicationSets (Ch 6.1) are Argo CD's own current recommendation
      for cluster bootstrapping at scale; App-of-Apps remains ideal for
      a small, fixed set like easypj's 3 environments.
    - kubeseal defaults to JSON output and "strict" scope — both common
      sources of a broken pipeline if unset explicitly.
    🎯 Exam relevance: direct hands-on expression of "Configure Argo CD
       with Helm and Kustomize" inside Argo CD's 34%-weighted domain.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. SECRET SPRAWL — a raw v1 Secret committed anywhere defeats the
       entire point of Sealed Secrets/ESO. Check the `kind:` field, not
       just the filename.
    2. FORGETTING TO SEAL BEFORE COMMITTING — muscle memory from plain
       Kubernetes work skips kubeseal entirely.
    3. APP-OF-APPS RBAC — a non-admin user's bootstrap failing partway
       through is expected behavior, not a YAML typo.
    4. APP-OF-APPS vs APPLICATIONSET CONFUSION — know the decision
       criteria (small fixed set vs growing set), not just the
       mechanics of either.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - `kubectl kustomize overlays/prod | less` — preview rendered
      manifests locally before committing.
    - `argocd app create ... --kustomize-image repo/easypj=repo/easypj:v2`
      — set an image tag override without touching Git.
    - `kubeseal --fetch-cert > pub-cert.pem` once, then reseal offline
      afterward — no live cluster access needed for repeat sealing.
    - `kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml --dry-run=server` — validate
      before really bootstrapping.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ──────────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  KUSTOMIZE                                                          │
    │  kubectl kustomize <dir>              preview rendered manifests    │
    │  kustomize build --enable-helm <dir>  render incl. helmCharts:      │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  APPLICATION CREATION                                               │
    │  argocd app create easypj-dev -f easypj-dev.yaml                    │
    │  argocd app diff easypj-prod          preview before sync           │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  SEALED SECRETS                                                     │
    │  kubectl create secret generic db-password --from-literal=password= │
    │    supersecret --dry-run=client -o yaml > secret.yaml               │
    │  kubeseal --format=yaml < secret.yaml > sealed-secret.yaml          │
    │  kubeseal --fetch-cert > pub-cert.pem                                │
    ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │  APP-OF-APPS BOOTSTRAP                                               │
    │  kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml     one command, whole cluster   │
    │  argocd app list                        confirm all children appeared│
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~20 min, do-it-now)
    ─────────────────────────────────────────
    1. Create easypj-k8s/base/ with a minimal deployment.yaml,
       service.yaml, kustomization.yaml (5 min).
    2. Create overlays/dev/ and overlays/prod/ each referencing
       ../../base + a replica-count patch (5 min).
    3. kubectl kustomize overlays/prod — confirm YOUR prod replica
       count (2 min).
    4. Seal a fake DB password Secret with --format=yaml; confirm
       readable YAML, not JSON (5 min).
    5. Write ONE Application YAML pointing at overlays/dev and apply it
       (3 min).

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ────────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. overlays/prod needs base's Deployment/Service. What's strictly
        required, and is the folder name "base" one of them?
    A1. overlays/prod/kustomization.yaml must list resources: pointing
        to ../../base; base/ must have its own valid kustomization.yaml.
        The folder name is NOT required.
    Explanation: Kustomize only needs a resolvable kustomization.yaml.

    Q2. `kubeseal < secret.yaml > sealed-secret.yaml`, no other flags —
        a teammate sees one line of JSON. What happened, and the fix?
    A2. kubeseal's --format defaults to json. Fix: kubeseal
        --format=yaml < secret.yaml > sealed-secret.yaml.
    Explanation: verified against the kubeseal CLI source.

    Q3. kubeseal fails with "services sealed-secrets not found," but
        the controller is actually sealed-secrets-controller in
        kube-system. What does this tell you?
    A3. Someone passed --controller-name explicitly, overriding the
        real default (sealed-secrets-controller).
    Explanation: defaults are --controller-name=sealed-secrets-controller,
    --controller-namespace=kube-system.

    Q4. You rename easypj-db-password to easypj-postgres-password and
        reapply the same SealedSecret. It fails to unseal. Why, and the
        two fixes?
    A4. kubeseal's default --scope is strict, binding ciphertext to the
        exact namespace+name. Fix: reseal under the new name, or
        originally seal with --scope namespace-wide/cluster-wide.
    Explanation: strict scope uses name as authenticated-encryption
    additional data.

    Q5. A container arg must always match a configMapGenerator's
        auto-generated hash suffix, without hardcoding it. Which field,
        and what deprecated field did it replace?
    A5. replacements: (source+targets); replaces the deprecated vars:
        field.
    Explanation: structured field-copy, not string templating.

    Q6. Product wants a shareable Helm chart; Platform wants Kustomize
        overlays on top. Must they pick one tool?
    A6. No — helmCharts: in kustomization.yaml (kustomize build
        --enable-helm) inflates a real Helm chart, then Kustomize
        patches apply on top.
    Explanation: rebuts an either/or framing.

    Q7. Admin runs app-of-apps.yaml successfully. A teammate with only
        the easypj-dev AppProject role tries it on another cluster and
        it fails partway. Is this a bug?
    A7. No — expected. Creating Applications across arbitrary Projects
        is an admin-level capability.
    Explanation: verbatim from Argo CD's official docs.

    Q8. You delete easypj-staging.yaml from argo-apps/, expecting the
        Deployment to vanish too. Only the Application object
        disappears. What's missing?
    A8. metadata.finalizers: [resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io]
        on the child manifest.
    Explanation: without it, deployed resources are orphaned, not
    deleted.

    Q9. When should you use App-of-Apps vs an ApplicationSet for
        easypj's dev/staging/prod?
    A9. App-of-Apps: small, fixed, explicitly-named set. ApplicationSet:
        the set grows dynamically without hand-writing new YAML.
    Explanation: Argo CD's current docs recommend ApplicationSets as
    the preferred bootstrap approach for the growing case.

    Q10. Your ExternalSecret uses apiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1.
         A teammate calls this "outdated." Right or wrong, and does it
         break anything today?
    A10. Partially right: v1 is now current stable (promoted from
         v1beta1). v1beta1 remains supported — nothing breaks today.
    Explanation: new manifests should default to v1 going forward.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  easypj-k8s/ (Git)                                                  │
    │   ├── base/  (deployment, service, configmap, sealed-secret.yaml)  │
    │   ├── overlays/{dev,staging,prod}/ (patches only)                  │
    │   ├── argo-apps/ (3 Application YAMLs, one per env)  <- 7.3        │
    │   └── app-of-apps.yaml  -- kubectl apply -f (ONCE)  --+  <- 7.4    │
    │                                                         v           │
    │                                    argocd-application-controller    │
    │                                    reconciles the SAME way at       │
    │                                    every level (recursive)          │
    │                       +-----------------+-----------------+        │
    │                       v                 v                 v         │
    │                  easypj-dev       easypj-staging     easypj-prod    │
    │                       v                 v                 v         │
    │                  Deployment/Service/ConfigMap + unsealed Secret     │
    │  base/secret.yaml is a SEALED ciphertext, never plaintext — 7.5     │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: one kubectl apply -f app-of-apps.yaml creates a single
    root Application; the SAME reconcile loop that watches it also
    watches the three child Applications it creates, each running its
    own kustomize build against a shared base. Nothing in that chain is
    safe to commit unless every Secret went through kubeseal or an
    ExternalSecret reference first.


Chapter 8: Argo CD CLI and UI Mastery
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~3 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo CD 34% (hands-on-CLI heavy) · 🏢 Daily-Work: very high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    In Chapter 7 you wired easypj into GitOps — commit to Git, Argo CD
    reconciles. That's the "set it and forget it" 95%. Chapter 8 is the
    other 5%, and it's the 5% both your job AND the CAPA exam actually
    grade: the 2am page when easypj-prod flips to Degraded, the exam
    task that says "roll back the last deploy — 90 seconds, terminal
    only, no docs open." This chapter turns you from a GitOps spectator
    into the operator — the same argocd binary, wielded fast from a
    terminal, safely from a browser, or across an entire fleet of
    clusters instead of just one.

    ┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE CHAPTER 8               │  AFTER CHAPTER 8                │
    ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
    │ "I wrote the YAML and pushed.   │ "I can OPERATE what I wrote:    │
    │  If something's wrong, I edit   │  diff before I sync, sync only  │
    │  YAML, push again, and wait."   │  what I mean, roll back in one  │
    │                                  │  command, read the RIGHT logs." │
    └────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES
    ────────────────────────
      - Log into any Argo CD instance from the CLI — including behind a
        proxy that doesn't support HTTP/2 (--grpc-web).
      - Diagnose whether an OutOfSync Application is real drift or a
        false alarm using app diff, BEFORE ever running app sync.
      - Execute a scoped, safe sync and roll back a bad release by
        History ID.
      - Navigate the Web UI's four resource views (Tree/Network/List/
        Pods) to find a failing container fast.
      - Register a second cluster with Argo CD and explain EXACTLY what
        gets created on it — and scope that access down.
      - Walk the sync-failure debugging flowchart end-to-end, reading
        the CORRECT controller logs (StatefulSet, not Deployment).
