    8.1  Argo CD CLI — Essential Commands
         - argocd login, argocd app list, argocd app get, argocd app sync
         🧠 MENTAL MODEL — map each argocd verb to its kubectl cousin so
            the CLI stops feeling like a new language: `argocd login` ~
            `kubectl config use-context` (establishes WHO/WHERE), `argocd
            app get` ~ `kubectl describe` (one resource, full detail),
            `argocd app list` ~ `kubectl get applications` (Application
            IS a CRD — visible via kubectl too), `argocd app sync` ~
            `kubectl apply -f <rendered-manifests>` but sourced from Git
            instead of your local disk.
         📖 KEY TERM — Application: everything argocd operates on is a
            Kubernetes Custom Resource (applications.argoproj.io, group
            argoproj.io) in the argocd namespace. `argocd app get
            easypj-prod` is a friendlier read of the same object
            `kubectl get application easypj-prod -n argocd -o yaml`
            shows.
         - argocd app diff (see what would change before syncing!)
         📖 KEY TERM — Target State vs. Live State: `app diff` renders a
            unified diff between the TARGET state (manifests rendered
            from the Git revision Argo CD last fetched) and the LIVE
            state (what's actually on the cluster). Secrets are excluded
            from this comparison by default.
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — exit codes make `app diff` scriptable:
            0 = no diff, 1 = a diff was found, 2 = a general error.
            CI gates commonly test `$?` after `argocd app diff <name>`
            to decide "block merge, drift detected" vs "clean."
         - argocd app history and argocd app rollback
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — a "History ID" is an entry in
            .status.history[] on the Application object (Git revision
            + timestamp), not a Git SHA by itself. Only the last 10
            entries are kept by default (spec.revisionHistoryLimit) —
            `argocd app rollback easypj-prod 11` fails once ID 11 has
            aged out.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `app rollback` re-syncs the cluster to an OLD
            revision; it does NOT touch Git. The next trigger (webhook,
            the ~3-min poll, or a manual Sync click) reconciles straight
            back to HEAD and silently undoes the rollback — unless you
            also `git revert` the bad commit or pause auto-sync first.
         - argocd proj list, argocd repo list, argocd cluster list
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — for easypj, an AppProject named "easypj" scopes
            exactly which repos (app repo + GitOps config repo), which
            destination clusters/namespaces (easypj-dev/staging/prod),
            and which resource Kinds are allowed. `argocd proj get
            easypj` is the fastest way to check "could this Application
            even deploy a ClusterRole?" before digging further.
         [COMMAND REFERENCE TABLE: All essential CLI commands]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD CLI — ESSENTIAL COMMANDS                          │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐│
         │  │ COMMAND                   │ DESCRIPTION               ││
         │  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ LOGIN & CONTEXT            │                           ││
         │  │ argocd login <server>      │ Login to Argo CD server   ││
         │  │ argocd context             │ Show current context       ││
         │  │ argocd account update-     │ Change a user's password  ││
         │  │   password                 │ (own, or another w/ RBAC) ││
         │  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ APPLICATIONS               │                           ││
         │  │ argocd app list            │ List all applications     ││
         │  │ argocd app get <name>      │ Detailed app info + status││
         │  │ argocd app create <name>   │ Create app from CLI args  ││
         │  │ argocd app sync <name>     │ Trigger manual sync       ││
         │  │ argocd app diff <name>     │ Preview changes (DRY RUN) ││
         │  │ argocd app history <name>  │ Show deployment history   ││
         │  │ argocd app rollback <name> │ Rollback to previous ver  ││
         │  │ argocd app delete <name>   │ Delete application        ││
         │  │ argocd app logs <name>     │ Stream app logs           ││
         │  │ argocd app manifests <name>│ Show rendered manifests   ││
         │  │ argocd app resources <name>│ List all K8s resources    ││
         │  │ argocd app set <name> -p   │ Override a Helm param      ││
         │  │   <comp>=<param>=<val>     │ (e.g. -p replicaCount=3)  ││
         │  │ argocd app set <name>      │ Change the source repo     ││
         │  │   --repo <url>             │ (NOT -p — see gotcha below)││
         │  │ argocd app unset <name>    │ Remove parameter override ││
         │  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ PROJECTS                   │                           ││
         │  │ argocd proj list           │ List all AppProjects      ││
         │  │ argocd proj get <name>     │ Show project details      ││
         │  │ argocd proj create <name>   │ Create a project           ││
         │  │   [-f <file>]              │ (optionally from YAML)     ││
         │  │ argocd proj delete <name>   │ Delete project           ││
         │  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ REPOS & CLUSTERS           │                           ││
         │  │ argocd repo add <url>      │ Register Git repo         ││
         │  │ argocd repo list           │ List registered repos     ││
         │  │ argocd cluster add <ctx>   │ Add K8s cluster           ││
         │  │ argocd cluster list        │ List managed clusters     ││
         │  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤│
         │  │ DIAGNOSTICS                │                           ││
         │  │ argocd version             │ Show client+server version││
         │  │ argocd app wait <name>     │ Wait for sync+healthy     ││
         │  │ argocd admin notifications │ Render + send a template   ││
         │  │   template notify          │ to test it                 ││
         │  └────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘│
         │                                                            │
         │  EXAM TIP: Memorize app list, app get, app sync, app diff │
         │  These are the most frequently used in CAPA exam tasks.   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the table's `-p <comp>=<param>=<val>` pattern is
            the FULL flag grammar ([COMPONENT=]PARAM=VALUE), but
            COMPONENT= is a ksonnet-era leftover (ksonnet is removed).
            For a Helm app like easypj, drop it: `argocd app set
            easypj-prod -p replicaCount=3` or `-p image.tag=v1.2.0`
            (dot-path for nested values) — NOT
            `easypj=replicaCount=3`. The table's own example already
            shows the 2-segment form.

    8.2  Argo CD Web UI Walkthrough
         - Dashboard overview
         📖 KEY TERM — every Application card shows TWO independent
            status axes: SYNC STATUS (Synced / OutOfSync / Unknown —
            does live match Git?) and HEALTH STATUS (Healthy /
            Progressing / Degraded / Suspended / Missing / Unknown — is
            the workload actually working?). An app can be Synced AND
            Degraded simultaneously (easypj-prod's manifests match Git
            exactly, but the Deployment it created is CrashLoopBackOff)
            — a common exam trap, not a contradiction.
         - Application detail view (tree, network, logs)
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the Web UI actually names FOUR resource views:
            TREE (ownership hierarchy), NETWORK (Service->Endpoint->Pod
            connectivity graph), LIST (flat sortable table — best once
            an app has 50+ resources), and PODS (pods only, groupable
            by node/parentResource/topLevelResource). "Logs" is NOT one
            of the four views — it's a per-resource tab reached by
            clicking a specific Pod inside any of the four.
         📖 KEY TERM — which view loads FIRST is configurable per-
            Application: the `pref.argocd.argoproj.io/default-view`
            annotation (values: tree/pods/network/list) sets it, and
            `pref.argocd.argoproj.io/default-pod-sort` (values: node/
            parentResource/topLevelResource) sets the Pods view's
            default grouping. Annotating an easypj Application with
            default-view=pods, default-pod-sort=node saves a click
            every time someone opens it mid-incident.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — the Pods view can also surface NODE labels
            directly (e.g. which zone or Karpenter capacity-type a
            crashing easypj Pod landed on) once an operator adds them
            to `application.allowedNodeLabels` in argocd-cm — e.g.
            `topology.kubernetes.io/zone,karpenter.sh/capacity-type` —
            without that ConfigMap key the labels exist on the Node but
            the UI won't render them.
         - Sync and rollback from UI
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the UI's Sync button defaults to the SAME safe
            behavior as the CLI: it will NOT prune resources removed
            from Git unless "Prune" is explicitly checked in the sync
            dialog. Repeatedly clicking Sync on a "stuck" OutOfSync app
            without checking why (app diff first!) is how orphaned
            easypj resources accumulate silently.
         🧠 MENTAL MODEL — UI rollback is a visual `app history` + `app
            rollback`: the History tab lists the same
            .status.history[] entries the CLI reads; clicking
            "Rollback" on an older row is exactly `argocd app rollback
            easypj-prod <that row's ID>` — same 10-entry retention
            limit, just clicked instead of typed.
         - Resource events and logs
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — the UI's log stream doesn't hit the target
            cluster from your browser. Your browser talks to
            argocd-server over gRPC-Web; argocd-server then uses the
            SAME bearer token stored in that cluster's registration
            Secret to open the log stream against the target cluster's
            API server, and relays it back. If that cluster was
            registered with --namespace easypj-prod (scoped RBAC), log
            streaming for Pods OUTSIDE easypj-prod fails even though
            the UI still shows the button.
         [SCREENSHOT GUIDE: Navigating the UI]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD WEB UI — WIREFRAME (what you'll actually click)    │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │ DASHBOARD (app list)                                   │ │
         │  │ ┌────────────┐ ┌────────────┐ ┌────────────┐          │ │
         │  │ │ easypj-dev │ │easypj-stag │ │easypj-prod │          │ │
         │  │ │ 🟢 Synced   │ │ 🟡 OutOfSync│ │ 🟢 Synced   │          │ │
         │  │ │ 🟢 Healthy  │ │ 🟢 Healthy  │ │ 🔴 Degraded │          │ │
         │  │ └─────┬──────┘ └────────────┘ └─────┬──────┘          │ │
         │  └───────┼─────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┘ │
         │          │ (click a card)               │                   │
         │          ▼                               ▼                   │
         │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
         │  │ APPLICATION DETAIL — easypj-prod                       │ │
         │  │ [TREE] [NETWORK] [LIST] [PODS]   <- 4 real view tabs   │ │
         │  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │ │
         │  │  │  TREE view (default):                            │   │ │
         │  │  │  Application                                     │   │ │
         │  │  │   └── Deployment: easypj                          │   │ │
         │  │  │        └── ReplicaSet                             │   │ │
         │  │  │             └── Pod (🔴 click for its Logs tab)   │   │ │
         │  │  │   └── Service: easypj                             │   │ │
         │  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │ │
         │  │  [SYNC] [REFRESH] [HISTORY & ROLLBACK] [DELETE]         │ │
         │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
         │                                                            │
         │  "Logs" is reached by clicking a Pod inside any of the 4    │
         │  views — it is NOT a 5th top-level tab (this section's own  │
         │  gotcha above).                                             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.3  Debugging Sync Failures
         - Common errors: Invalid YAML, missing CRDs, wrong namespace
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — "missing CRDs" is rarely an ordering problem
            WITHIN one Application: Argo CD already sorts each sync by
            phase, then sync-wave, then KIND (namespaces/standard kinds
            before custom-resource instances), then name — so a CRD and
            a CR of it in the SAME Application are already ordered
            correctly by default. The real cause is almost always a
            SEPARATE Application/chart installing the CRD that hasn't
            synced yet — ordering across Applications isn't guaranteed.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — a large CRD (common with operator charts) can fail
            with "metadata.annotations: Too long: must have at most
            262144 bytes." Client-side apply stores the whole object in
            a last-applied-configuration annotation, which some CRDs'
            schemas alone exceed. Fix: add ServerSideApply=true to the
            Application's syncOptions (supported since Argo CD 2.5) —
            it drops that annotation entirely.
         - Using argocd app diff to preview changes
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — set KUBECTL_EXTERNAL_DIFF to point `app diff`
            at a real diff tool during an incident, e.g.
            `KUBECTL_EXTERNAL_DIFF=colordiff argocd app diff
            easypj-prod`, instead of squinting at plain diff output.
         - Checking controller logs: kubectl logs -n argocd statefulset/argocd-application-controller
         [TROUBLESHOOTING FLOWCHART: Sync failure → diagnosis → fix]
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — the bullet above (and the flowchart's Step 4) once
            read `kubectl logs -n argocd deploy/argocd-application-
            controller` — that's the single most common mistake
            operators make in the wild, and it was WRONG: since Argo CD
            1.8 (October 2020), argocd-application-controller has been a
            StatefulSet, not a Deployment — specifically because it
            shards cluster-watching work across replicas (see this
            chapter's [G] Under the Hood). Running the deploy/ form
            against a current install returns `Error from server
            (NotFound): deployments.apps
            "argocd-application-controller" not found`. Both the
            bullet and the flowchart above are now corrected to
            `statefulset/argocd-application-controller`; once
            horizontally scaled, target a shard directly:
            `kubectl logs -n argocd argocd-application-controller-0`.
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD SYNC FAILURE DEBUGGING FLOWCHART                  │
         │                                                            │
         │  APP SHOWING "OutOfSync" or "Unknown"?                     │
         │         │                                                  │
         │         ▼                                                  │
         │  ┌──────────────────┐                                      │
         │  │ 1. argocd app    │                                      │
         │  │    diff <name>   │  ← See EXACTLY what's different      │
         │  └────────┬─────────┘                                      │
         │           │                                                │
         │     ┌─────┴─────┐                                          │
         │     │ Error?    │                                          │
         │     └─────┬─────┘                                          │
         │    ┌──────┴──────┐                                         │
         │    │ YES         │  → Check error message:                 │
         │    │             │    • "repo not found" → verify repoURL  │
         │    │             │    • "path does not exist" → check path │
         │    │             │    • "Permission denied" → check creds  │
         │    │             │    • "YAML parse error" → fix YAML      │
         │    └─────────────┘                                         │
         │    ┌──────┴──────┐                                         │
         │    │ NO (diff    │  → Diff shows resources to be          │
         │    │ shows       │    created/updated/deleted              │
         │    │ changes)    │    → argocd app sync <name>            │
         │    └──────┬──────┘                                         │
         │           │                                                │
         │           ▼                                                │
         │  ┌──────────────────┐                                      │
         │  │ 2. Sync succeeds │  → ✅ Done!                          │
         │  │    but still      │                                      │
         │  │    "Degraded"?    │                                      │
         │  └────────┬─────────┘                                      │
         │           │                                                │
         │           ▼                                                │
         │  ┌──────────────────┐                                      │
         │  │ 3. Check health  │                                      │
         │  │    kubectl get   │  ← Pod crashing? OOM? ImagePullErr? │
         │  │    pods -n <ns>  │                                      │
         │  │    kubectl logs  │                                      │
         │  │    <pod-name>    │                                      │
         │  └────────┬─────────┘                                      │
         │           │                                                │
         │           ▼                                                │
         │  ┌──────────────────┐                                      │
         │  │ 4. Check         │                                      │
         │  │    controller    │  ← Deepest insights when sync       │
         │  │    logs:         │    itself is failing                │
         │  │    kubectl logs  │                                      │
         │  │    -n argocd     │                                      │
         │  │    statefulset/  │                                      │
         │  │    argocd-       │                                      │
         │  │    application-  │                                      │
         │  │    controller    │                                      │
         │  └──────────────────┘                                      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.4  Multi-Cluster Management
         - Adding external clusters to Argo CD
         📖 KEY TERM — --cluster-endpoint {kubeconfig|kube-public|
            internal} controls WHICH server URL is written into the
            cluster Secret, independent of which kubeconfig context ran
            the command. `internal` matters when Argo CD and the target
            cluster share a cloud VPC (e.g. two EKS/GKE clusters) — it
            points at the private endpoint instead of the public one,
            cutting egress cost and public exposure.
         - Deploying easypj to multiple clusters from one Argo CD
         🧠 MENTAL MODEL — hand-writing one Application per cluster
            (easypj-prod-use1, easypj-prod-euw1, ...) doesn't scale past
            2-3 clusters. The production answer is a Cluster generator
            (ApplicationSet, Ch 6): ONE template + the set of clusters
            `argocd cluster list` already knows about STAMPS OUT one
            Application per matching cluster automatically. `argocd
            cluster add` here is the prerequisite step that makes a
            cluster eligible to be picked up that way.
         - Cluster secrets and credentials
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — `argocd cluster add` grants the argocd-manager
            ServiceAccount CLUSTER-ADMIN on the target cluster BY
            DEFAULT when no --namespace is passed — not just generic
            "access." Argo CD's own security docs call this fine for a
            learning cluster and inappropriate for production. For
            easypj-prod, always scope with --namespace easypj-prod
            (Role/RoleBinding instead of ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding)
            or supply a pre-scoped --service-account.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — anyone with get/list on Secrets in the argocd
            namespace can read every registered cluster's bearer token
            in plain text and use it directly — completely bypassing
            Argo CD's own RBAC. Treat read access to that namespace's
            Secrets as equivalent to holding credentials to every
            cluster Argo CD manages.
         [ARCHITECTURE DIAGRAM: Argo CD managing multiple clusters]
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — CAPA scenario questions in this area tend
            to describe a symptom ("a second cluster was compromised
            through Argo CD") and expect the fix named as scoped
            --namespace RBAC or `cluster rotate-auth` — not "delete and
            re-add the cluster."

    8.5  Metrics and Observability
         - Prometheus endpoints Argo CD ships out of the box
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — Chapter 20.4 already told you WHERE these live
            (argocd-metrics:8082 controller, argocd-server-metrics:8083,
            argocd-repo-server:8084) — this section tells you WHAT to
            actually query once something scrapes them.
         📖 KEY TERM — argocd_app_info is the most useful gauge here:
            one time series per Application, labeled sync_status,
            health_status, autosync_enabled, repo, dest_namespace.
            `sum(argocd_app_info{sync_status!="Synced"})` is the PromQL
            one-liner for "how many easypj Applications are drifted
            right now" — the metric equivalent of scanning 8.2's
            Dashboard.
         [TABLE: Key Argo CD metric names]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO CD-SPECIFIC PROMETHEUS METRICS                        │
         │                                                            │
         │  argocd_app_info                  gauge, 1 per App          │
         │  argocd_app_sync_total             counter, syncs by phase  │
         │  argocd_app_reconcile              histogram, reconcile secs│
         │  argocd_app_k8s_request_total      counter, K8s API calls   │
         │                                    during reconciliation    │
         │  argocd_cluster_info                gauge, 1 per registered │
         │                                    cluster (8.4's Secrets)  │
         │  argocd_cluster_connection_status   gauge, 1/0 reachability │
         │                                    per registered cluster   │
         │  argocd_login_request_total         counter, on argocd-server│
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — argocd_cluster_connection_status is the
            fastest way to alert on "8.4's cluster Secret token expired
            or was revoked" WITHOUT waiting for a human to notice
            easypj-prod stuck OutOfSync — page on this metric for that
            specific failure mode, not on app health.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — none of these are exposed unless something scrapes
            them: the Services ship by default for controller/server/
            repo-server (8082-84, per 20.4's table), but a Prometheus
            Operator ServiceMonitor (or prometheus.io/scrape
            annotations) still has to target them — an open port isn't
            the same as something graphing it.

    8.6  UI Customization — Deep Links and Status Badges
         - Deep Links: one-click jumps from the UI to a third-party
           system (Splunk, Datadog, an internal runbook)
         📖 KEY TERM — three independent `<location>.links` keys in the
            argocd-cm ConfigMap: `application.links` (Application
            summary tab), `resource.links` (a SPECIFIC resource inside
            the Tree/Network/List/Pods views from 8.2), and
            `project.links` (the AppProject tab, 8.1's `argocd proj
            get`). Each entry: title + url (Go text/template) + optional
            description/icon.class/if (an expr-lang/expr condition).
         [YAML EXAMPLE: link a Degraded Pod straight to Grafana]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  resource.links: |                                          │
         │    - url: https://grafana.easypj.internal/d/pods?var-       │
         │        pod={{.resource.metadata.name}}                      │
         │      title: Grafana                                         │
         │      if: resource.kind == "Pod"                             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — deep links are STATIC config on the Argo CD side
            (argocd-cm), not a per-Application Git field — swapping an
            Application's AppProject doesn't change which links show; a
            ConfigMap edit does.
         - Status Badge: an embeddable Synced/Healthy image
         📖 KEY TERM — `${server}/api/badge?name=<app>` (add
            `&revision=true` to also show the tracked Git revision,
            `&showAppName=true` to print the name on the badge). Off by
            default via `statusbadge.enabled` in argocd-cm.
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — it's OFF by default because the badge endpoint is
            intentionally UNAUTHENTICATED even on a locked-down Argo CD
            instance — enabling it for easypj-prod means anyone who can
            reach the URL learns its sync/health status with zero
            login. Treat it as a public status page, not an internal
            one.

    8.7  Web-based Terminal
         - `kubectl exec` from the browser, no local kubeconfig needed
         📖 KEY TERM — added in Argo CD 2.4; OFF by default. Two
            separate switches, both required:
            1. `exec.enabled: "true"` in argocd-cm (+ restart
               argocd-server) — the FEATURE toggle.
            2. an Argo CD RBAC line granting the built-in `exec`
               resource, e.g. `p, role:easypj-oncall, exec, create,
               easypj/easypj-prod, allow` — the PER-USER toggle (same
               argocd-rbac-cm ConfigMap 8.1's `argocd proj` commands
               already touch).
         ⚠️ GOTCHA — on Kubernetes < 1.31, argocd-server's OWN
            ServiceAccount also needs a `pods/exec` create rule against
            the TARGET cluster (on top of whatever 8.4's `argocd
            cluster add --namespace` already granted) — without it,
            Argo CD's own RBAC says yes but the K8s API server still
            returns Forbidden. On 1.31+, the `get` permission on Pods
            (already required for the Tree/Pods views in 8.2) is enough
            by itself.
         🏢 DAILY-WORK — this replaces "ask on-call for kubeconfig
            access" with a scoped Argo CD RBAC grant — but it inherits
            whatever the target Pod's mounted ServiceAccount can do:
            exec into a jobpost Pod that mounts a token, and you
            effectively HAVE that ServiceAccount's permissions for the
            rest of the shell session.
         - Configuring which shells are tried
         🔧 UNDER THE HOOD — `exec.shells` in argocd-cm controls the
            fallback order tried inside the container (default: bash,
            sh, powershell, cmd) — first one present in the image wins;
            a distroless easypj image with none of those makes the
            terminal button fail even with correct RBAC.
         🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE — a CAPA scenario describing "give a
            developer temporary shell access to a Pod without handing
            out kubeconfig" is THIS feature — the fix lives in
            argocd-rbac-cm's `exec` resource, not a Kubernetes RBAC
            object.


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How Multi-Cluster Management Really Works
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    Argo CD has exactly ONE way to know about a cluster: a Kubernetes
    Secret, in its own namespace, labeled
    argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type: cluster, holding a name, server URL,
    and a bearer-token config block. `argocd cluster add <context>`
    generates it for you:

    1. Uses YOUR local kubeconfig context's admin credentials against
       the TARGET cluster, ONE TIME.
    2. On the TARGET cluster, creates a ServiceAccount argocd-manager
       (kube-system), plus a ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding granting it
       access (pass --namespace ns1,ns2 for a scoped Role/RoleBinding
       instead — least privilege).
    3. Reads that ServiceAccount's bearer token.
    4. Writes the cluster Secret back on the Argo CD cluster.
    5. argocd-application-controller — a StatefulSet, specifically
       because it SHARDS (each replica owns a hash subset of registered
       clusters) — picks up the new Secret and starts watching.

    The cluster Argo CD itself runs on needs none of this: it's
    registered implicitly as "in-cluster", server
    https://kubernetes.default.svc, no Secret required.

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  ARCHITECTURE: one Argo CD, two clusters                   │
    │                                                              │
    │   ARGO CD (management) CLUSTER                               │
    │   argocd-server | argocd-repo-server | redis                 │
    │   argocd-application-controller (StatefulSet, sharded)       │
    │        │ (in-cluster,           │ (Secret: easypj-prod-      │
    │        │  no Secret needed)     │  cluster)                  │
    │        v                        v                            │
    │   easypj-dev / easypj-staging   EXTERNAL: easypj-prod         │
    │   namespaces, THIS cluster      cluster. SA: argocd-manager   │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - app diff before app sync — always. It's your dry run.
    - Sync does NOT delete by default — you must pass --prune.
    - -p/--parameter is a Helm-value override ONLY — use --repo/--path/
      --revision for Application spec fields.
    - The application-controller is a StatefulSet — logs come from
      statefulset/argocd-application-controller, not deploy/...
    - A cluster = a labeled Secret + an argocd-manager ServiceAccount/
      ClusterRole on the TARGET cluster.
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: this chapter is the hands-on drill for
       "Synchronize Applications" and the primary teaching ground for
       "Identify Common Reconciliation Patterns."

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. app diff EXCLUDES Secrets from comparison by default — a
       drifted Secret can hide behind a "clean" diff.
    2. Forgetting --grpc-web when Argo CD sits behind an HTTP/1.1-only
       proxy — CLI calls hang or fail with generic transport errors.
    3. -p on app set is a Helm-parameter override, NOT a way to set
       arbitrary Application fields.
    4. Reading controller logs with deploy/argocd-application-
       controller — it's a StatefulSet; the command returns nothing.
    5. argocd cluster rm deletes the Secret but leaves the
       argocd-manager ServiceAccount/ClusterRole on the target cluster.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - alias acd=argocd — every keystroke counts under exam timing.
    - -o name on app list/app get for scriptable output.
    - app diff returns exit code 1 when there ARE differences, 0 when
      clean — usable directly in shell conditionals.
    - app wait --sync --health after a sync beats polling app get.
    - app sync --resource GROUP:KIND:NAME to fix ONE resource fast.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑 — Consolidated Cheat-Card
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  LOGIN / CONTEXT                                            │
    │  argocd login <server> [--sso|--username/--password] [--grpc-web] │
    │  argocd context [<name>] [--delete]                         │
    │  argocd account update-password                              │
    │                                                                │
    │  APPLICATIONS — the daily loop                               │
    │  argocd app list / app get <name>                            │
    │  argocd app diff <name>              <- ALWAYS before sync    │
    │  argocd app sync <name> [--prune] [--dry-run] [--resource…] │
    │  argocd app wait <name> --sync --health                      │
    │  argocd app history <name> / app rollback <name> [ID]       │
    │  argocd app logs <name> -f -c <container>                   │
    │  argocd app set <name> -p c=p=v   (Helm value only)         │
    │  argocd app set <name> --repo <url>  (repo URL only)        │
    │                                                                │
    │  CLUSTERS — fleet management                                 │
    │  argocd cluster add <context> [--namespace ns1,ns2]         │
    │  argocd cluster list / get <name>                            │
    │  argocd cluster rotate-auth <server|name>                    │
    │  argocd cluster rm <context>   (manual SA cleanup needed)    │
    │                                                                │
    │  TROUBLESHOOTING                                             │
    │  kubectl logs -n argocd statefulset/argocd-application-      │
    │    controller           <- NOT deploy/...                    │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪  (15 min, do it now)
    ─────────────────────────────────────────
      1. argocd login to your local cluster; note if --insecure or
         --grpc-web was needed.
      2. argocd app diff easypj-dev — read the output BEFORE syncing.
      3. Push a trivial Git change, then argocd app sync easypj-dev
         --resource apps:Deployment:easypj — confirm ONLY the
         Deployment synced.
      4. argocd app history easypj-dev, then rollback to an older ID.
      5. Register a second cluster with --namespace scoping; inspect
         the Secret it created.
      6. kubectl logs -n argocd statefulset/argocd-application-
         controller | tail -30.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. easypj-prod shows "OutOfSync." Show exactly what would change
        WITHOUT touching the cluster.
    A1. argocd app diff easypj-prod
    Explanation: pure comparison, never mutates anything. Won't show
    Secret drift (excluded by default).

    Q2. Sync easypj-staging so anything removed from Git is actually
        deleted, but limit blast radius to one Deployment.
    A2. argocd app sync easypj-staging --prune --resource apps:
        Deployment:jobpost
    Explanation: --prune is required to delete orphaned resources;
    --resource scopes the sync.

    Q3. Roll back easypj-prod to a known-good History ID 11.
    A3. argocd app rollback easypj-prod 11
    Explanation: re-applies a prior recorded deployment by History ID.

    Q4. CLI commands hang behind an HTTP/1.1-only corporate proxy, but
        the UI loads fine. Fix the login.
    A4. argocd login argocd.easypj.internal --grpc-web
    Explanation: CLI uses gRPC (needs HTTP/2) by default; --grpc-web
    switches to gRPC-Web, which works over HTTP/1.1 proxies.

    Q5. Temporarily bump easypj-prod's replicaCount to 5 without
        touching Git. What happens on the NEXT Git-driven sync?
    A5. argocd app set easypj-prod -p replicaCount=5. On the next
        sync, Argo CD reconciles back to what Git says — the override
        is live-only, not persisted.
    Explanation: to make it permanent, commit to Git instead.

    Q6. argocd app set easypj-prod -p repoURL=<url> had no effect. Why,
        and the correct command?
    A6. -p only accepts component=param=value Helm overrides; repoURL
        isn't a Helm value. Correct: argocd app set easypj-prod --repo
        <url>.
    Explanation: -p is silently ignored for non-Helm fields.

    Q7. Which Web UI view groups Pods by top-level owning resource for
        scanning replica health, and its CLI equivalent?
    A7. The Pods view. CLI equivalent: argocd app resources easypj-prod.

    Q8. argocd app diff easypj-dev errors with "repo not found." Steps
        and fix?
    A8. Check argocd repo list, verify spec.source.repoURL matches
        exactly, verify credentials with argocd repo get <url>; fix by
        correcting the URL or re-adding the repo.

    Q9. Register kind-easypj-prod but restrict access to only the
        easypj-prod namespace (shared cluster, least privilege).
    A9. argocd cluster add kind-easypj-prod --namespace easypj-prod
    Explanation: without --namespace, a cluster-wide ClusterRole is
    provisioned; --namespace scopes it to a Role/RoleBinding instead.

    Q10. A cluster's argocd-manager token may have leaked. Rotate it
         WITHOUT removing and re-registering the whole cluster.
    A10. argocd cluster rotate-auth <server-or-name>
    Explanation: rotates the bearer token in place — the direct,
    exam-correct command.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 8 IN ONE PICTURE                                   │
    │                                                                │
    │   TERMINAL              BROWSER            FLEET             │
    │   argocd app diff   <>  App Detail view  ->  N cluster Secrets│
    │   argocd app sync        Sync/History/       + argocd-manager│
    │   argocd app rollback    Diff buttons        SA per cluster  │
    │        │                     │                    │           │
    │        v                     v                    v           │
    │   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
    │   │      ONE Argo CD control plane (application-         │   │
    │   │      controller = sharded StatefulSet)                │   │
    │   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
    │        │                                          │           │

    │   easypj-dev/staging (in-cluster,           easypj-prod       │

    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: everything in this chapter is one idea wearing three
    outfits — the CLI verb, the UI button, and (at scale) the same
    verb replayed across every cluster registered as a Secret. Learn
    the verbs once (diff, sync, rollback, wait) and you've learned the
    UI and the fleet story for free.

    🎯 EASYPJ FOCUS: Build CI/CD pipelines for easypj (test → build
       → push), run DB migrations safely, and schedule nightly backups.

Chapter 9: Introduction to Argo Workflows
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟢🟡 BEGINNER/INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~2.5 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo Workflows 36% (foundational — the single highest-weighted domain on the exam) · 🏢 Daily-Work: high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ─────────────────────
    Part 2 gave easypj a working GitOps loop: push to Git, Argo CD syncs it
    to the cluster. But nobody's automated the steps that happen BEFORE
    that push lands — cloning, running the Django test suite, building the
    container image, pushing it to a registry, and safely running
    `manage.py migrate`. Today that's either a human typing commands from
    memory at 11pm, or a single brittle CI job that dies the moment two of
    those steps need to run in parallel or depend on each other's output.
    Argo Workflows is the engine that turns that checklist into a
    version-controlled, container-native graph — the SAME engine you'll
    later trigger automatically from a Git webhook (Part 5, Argo Events)
    and gate an Argo CD sync on (Ch 6 PreSync hooks).

    ┌─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE (manual/CI script)  │  AFTER (Argo Workflow)           │
    ├─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
    │  One long linear script:    │  A DAG: clone -> {test, lint}    │
    │  clone; test; lint; build;  │  (parallel) -> build -> push ->  │
    │  push; migrate — if step 2  │  migrate. Each step = its own    │
    │  fails, step 5 still runs   │  container; failure anywhere     │
    │  unless you wrote guards    │  halts downstream steps for free │
    │  Retries = re-run the whole │  Retry ONE failed step only, via │
    │  script from scratch         │  retryStrategy — not the whole   │
    │                              │  pipeline                        │
    └─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES — 🎯 What you'll be able to DO
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      - Explain what Argo Workflows is and correctly place it relative to
        Argo CD (deploys vs runs) and to a plain Kubernetes Job.
      - Given a pipeline scenario, decide Job vs Workflow and justify it
        with at least 3 concrete differentiators (structure, dependencies,
        artifact passing).
      - Name the Workflow Controller, Argo Server, and the (per-pod,
        not cluster-level) emissary Executor, and state what breaks if
        each is missing.
      - Install Argo Workflows quick-start into the argo namespace with
        --server-side, and verify exactly 2 pods reach Running.
      - Install the argo CLI, reach the Argo Server UI via port-forward +
        argo auth token, and submit/watch a real Workflow end to end.
      - Diagnose the two most common first-install failures: the
        client-side-apply annotation-size error, and assuming MinIO/
        Postgres are bundled by default.

    [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM — you are here

    │              CHAPTER 9: INTRODUCTION TO ARGO WORKFLOWS           │

    PROSE RECAP: this chapter is one straight line — define it (9.1),
    justify it against what you already know (9.2), name its moving
    parts (9.3), stand those parts up (9.4), then prove it all works
    with one real Workflow (9.5). Everything in Ch 10-13 assumes you
    can do all five without looking anything up.

    9.1  What is Argo Workflows?
         - Container-native workflow engine for Kubernetes
         - Each step runs in its own container
         - DAGs (Directed Acyclic Graph), Steps, and parallel execution
         - Use cases: CI/CD, data processing, ML pipelines, infrastructure automation
         [CONCEPT DIAGRAM: Workflow = DAG of containers]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  A WORKFLOW = A DAG, EACH NODE IS ITS OWN CONTAINER          │
         │                                                            │
         │           ┌──────────┐                                     │
         │           │  clone   │  container 1: alpine/git             │
         │           │  easypj  │                                     │
         │           └────┬─────┘                                     │
         │                │                                            │
         │       ┌────────┼────────┐                                  │
         │       ▼        ▼        ▼                                  │
         │  ┌────────┐┌────────┐┌────────┐                            │
         │  │ unit   ││ lint   ││ SAST   │  containers 2-4: run        │
         │  │ tests  ││        ││ scan   │  IN PARALLEL, own pod each  │
         │  └───┬────┘└───┬────┘└───┬────┘                            │
         │      └─────────┼─────────┘                                  │
         │                ▼                                            │
         │           ┌──────────┐                                     │
         │           │  build   │  container 5: kaniko                │
         │           │  image   │                                     │
         │           └──────────┘                                     │
         │                                                            │
         │  🎯 Compare to a plain shell script: one process, one       │
         │  filesystem, no isolation between steps. Here, EVERY box     │
         │  above is its own Pod — its own image, its own resource      │
         │  limits, its own failure/retry, and (Ch 10.5) its own way    │
         │  to hand a file to the next box via an artifact.             │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.2  Why Not Just Use Kubernetes Jobs?
         - Jobs are linear, Workflows are graphs
         - Workflows support parallelism, conditionals, loops
         - Workflows have artifact passing between steps
         - Workflows have richer output, retry, and timeout control
         [COMPARISON TABLE: Kubernetes Job vs Argo Workflow]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  KUBERNETES JOB vs ARGO WORKFLOW                           │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌─────────────────┬─────────────────┬───────────────────┐ │
         │  │ FEATURE         │ K8S JOB         │ ARGO WORKFLOW     │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Structure       │ Linear (1 task) │ DAG / Steps       │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Parallelism     │ Fixed completions│ Dynamic fan-out  │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Dependencies    │ ❌ No            │ ✅ depends: [...] │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Conditionals    │ ❌ No            │ ✅ when: <expr>  │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Loops           │ ❌ No            │ ✅ withItems/Seq  │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Artifact Passing│ ❌ No            │ ✅ S3/GCS/MinIO   │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Retry Strategy  │ Basic (backoff) │ ✅ Exponential +  │ │
         │  │                 │                 │ custom policies   │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Scheduling      │ CronJob         │ CronWorkflow      │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ UI              │ ❌ No native    │ ✅ Built-in web   │ │
         │  ├─────────────────┼─────────────────┼───────────────────┤ │
         │  │ Use Case        │ Simple run-to-  │ Complex pipelines │ │
         │  │                 │ completion      │ (CI/CD, ML, ETL)  │ │
         │  └─────────────────┴─────────────────┴───────────────────┘ │
         │                                                            │
         │  easypj EXAMPLE:                                           │
         │  K8s Job: Run "python manage.py migrate" once              │
         │  Argo Workflow: Clone → Test → Build → Push → Update Git   │
         │  → A full pipeline with parallel steps, not just one task  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.3  Argo Workflows Architecture
         - Workflow Controller — watches for Workflow CRDs
         - Executor — runs each step in its own pod
         - Argo Server — API and UI
         - Persistence: Postgres or MySQL for workflow state
         [ARCHITECTURE DIAGRAM: Argo Workflows components]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ARGO WORKFLOWS ARCHITECTURE — 2 real Deployments + 1        │
         │  per-pod helper (NOT a 3rd Deployment — see Ch 9's [I])      │
         │                                                            │
         │  ┌────────────────────┐        ┌────────────────────┐      │
         │  │ workflow-controller │        │   argo-server        │      │
         │  │ (Deployment)         │        │   (Deployment,        │      │
         │  │                       │        │    OPTIONAL)          │      │
         │  │ watches Workflow      │        │ REST API + Web UI +   │      │
         │  │ objects, creates      │        │ SSO + Archive access  │      │
         │  │ Pods per DAG step     │        └──────────┬────────────┘      │
         │  └──────────┬────────────┘                   │                   │
         │             │ creates                          │ reads/writes     │
         │             ▼                                  ▼                  │
         │  ┌────────────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────┐      │
         │  │  Pod (per DAG step)          │   │ Workflow Archive DB   │      │
         │  │  ┌────────────┐ ┌──────────┐│   │ (Postgres/MySQL/      │      │
         │  │  │   main      │ │ emissary ││   │  MariaDB — OPTIONAL,  │      │
         │  │  │ container   │ │ (init +  ││   │  Ch 9.3 [H])           │      │
         │  │  │ (your work) │ │ sidecar) ││   └──────────────────────┘      │
         │  │  └────────────┘ └──────────┘│                                  │
         │  └────────────────────────────┘                                  │
         │                                                            │
         │  `kubectl get deploy -n argo` shows ONLY workflow-controller │
         │  and argo-server — the emissary "Executor" is never a        │
         │  standalone Deployment, it rides inside every step's Pod.     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.4  Installing Argo Workflows
         - Quick start: kubectl apply install manifest
         - Configuring artifact repository (MinIO, S3, GCS)
         - Verifying: kubectl get pods -n argo
         [CODE BLOCK: Full install commands]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  ARGO WORKFLOWS — COMPLETE INSTALLATION                    │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Step 1: Create namespace                                │
          │  kubectl create namespace argo                             │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Step 2: Install Argo Workflows (quick-start)            │
          │  export ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION=v3.6.4  # pin client+server │
          │  kubectl apply --server-side -n argo -f \                 │
          │    "https://github.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/releases/   │
          │    download/${ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION}/                     │
          │    quick-start-minimal.yaml"                                │
          │  # --server-side is REQUIRED: client-side apply fails with │
          │  # "metadata.annotations: Too long" — CRDs exceed the      │
          │  # 262144-byte last-applied-configuration cap (same trap   │
          │  # as the Argo CD install in Ch 3)                         │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Step 3: Wait for pods                                   │
          │  kubectl wait --for=condition=Ready pods --all \           │
          │    -n argo --timeout=300s                                  │
          │  kubectl get pods -n argo                                  │
          │  # Expected:                                               │
          │  # NAME                                  READY  STATUS     │
          │  # argo-server-...                       1/1    Running    │
          │  # workflow-controller-...                1/1    Running    │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Step 4: Install Argo CLI (if not done in Ch 2)         │
          │  curl -sLO "https://github.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/    │
          │    releases/download/${ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION}/            │
          │    argo-linux-amd64.gz"     # SAME version as the server   │
          │  gunzip argo-linux-amd64.gz                                │
          │  chmod +x argo-linux-amd64                                 │
          │  sudo mv argo-linux-amd64 /usr/local/bin/argo             │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Step 5: Configure artifact repository                   │
          │  # quick-start-minimal.yaml bundles NEITHER MinIO nor a    │
          │  # database — for a working demo use quick-start-postgres  │
          │  # .yaml instead; for production, edit configmap/          │
          │  # workflow-controller-configmap -n argo and set           │
          │  # artifactRepository.s3.bucket, endpoint, etc.            │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Step 6: Access UI                                       │
          │  kubectl -n argo port-forward svc/argo-server 2746:2746   │
          │  # Open https://localhost:2746                             │
          │  # Get token: argo auth token                              │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         - UI: argo server (localhost:2746)

    9.5  Your First Workflow: Hello World
         - The canonical hello-world.yaml: one container template, entrypoint
         [YAML SKELETON, annotated]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  hello-world.yaml — every line explained                    │
         │                                                              │
         │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1  # the Workflow CRD's API  │
         │  kind: Workflow                     # this IS the CRD kind  │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    generateName: hello-world-       # K8s appends a suffix  │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    entrypoint: hello-world           # which template starts│
         │    templates:                                                │
         │    - name: hello-world               # template's own name  │
         │      container:                                              │
         │        image: busybox                                       │
         │        command: [echo]                                       │
         │        args: ["hello world"]                                 │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         [HANDS-ON]
         argo submit -n argo --watch \
           https://raw.githubusercontent.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/main/examples/hello-world.yaml
         argo list -n argo
         argo logs -n argo @latest

    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — What actually happens on `argo submit --watch`
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    1. `argo` CLI POSTs the Workflow manifest to the K8s API server
       (creates a `Workflow` object, phase starts empty/Pending).
    2. The Workflow Controller's watch/informer fires on the new object,
       reads `spec.entrypoint`, and begins evaluating that template.
    3. For a `container` template, the Controller creates ONE Pod, labeled
       `workflows.argoproj.io/workflow=<name>`, containing your `main`
       container PLUS the emissary executor as an init container + sidecar.
    4. The executor wraps `main`'s process so it can capture stdout/
       stderr, enforce `activeDeadlineSeconds`, and (if configured) save
       output artifacts/parameters when the container exits.
    5. The Controller polls/watches that Pod's status and writes it back
       onto the Workflow object's `status.nodes` map — this is what
       `--watch` is streaming to your terminal in real time.
    6. On a terminal phase (Succeeded/Failed/Error), if an archive
       database (Postgres/MySQL/MariaDB) is configured, Argo Server
       persists the completed Workflow for `argo list --archived`; the
       live Pod is later cleaned up per the `podGC` policy.
    PROSE RECAP: nothing here is magic — it's the standard Kubernetes
    controller pattern (watch -> reconcile -> create Pods -> update
    status) applied recursively down a DAG, with a small helper process
    (emissary) riding inside every Pod to do the bookkeeping a plain
    container can't do for itself.

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - Argo CD deploys; Argo Workflows RUNS things — separate projects,
      separate CLIs (argocd vs argo), separate CRDs, separate installs.
    - K8s Job = one linear task (optionally N identical parallel copies).
      Argo Workflow = a graph of DIFFERENT tasks with dependencies,
      conditionals, loops, artifact passing, and tunable retry.
    - Two cluster-level components: Workflow Controller (required,
      reconciles Workflow CRDs) + Argo Server (optional: API/UI/SSO/
      Archive). The Executor (emissary) is per-POD, not a Deployment.
    - Install = `kubectl create namespace argo` + `kubectl apply
      --server-side -n argo -f <pinned quick-start-minimal.yaml URL>`.
    - quick-start-minimal.yaml has NO bundled MinIO/database —
      quick-start-postgres.yaml does, if you want those features working.
    - `argo submit --watch` is the fastest verified path from YAML to a
      confirmed-working result.
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: this chapter drills "Understand Argo Workflow
       Fundamentals" — part of the single largest domain (36%) on CAPA.
       Every later Workflows chapter assumes this vocabulary cold.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Client-side `kubectl apply -f quick-start-minimal.yaml` (no
       --server-side) fails with "metadata.annotations: Too long" — the
       identical trap as the Argo CD install in Ch 3.
    2. quick-start-minimal.yaml bundles NEITHER MinIO nor a database —
       don't assume artifact-passing or the Workflow Archive "just works."
    3. `dependencies: [A, B]` (list of task names) and `depends: "A &&
       B.Succeeded"` (boolean expression string) are TWO DIFFERENT
       fields — mixing their syntax is a real type error, not style.
    4. Assuming Argo Server must be running before ANY Workflow can
       execute — false; the Workflow Controller alone reconciles the CRD.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - `export ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION=vX.Y.Z` once; reuse it for both the
      manifest URL and the CLI download so client/server never skew.
    - `argo submit --watch file.yaml` beats submit + get + logs as 3
      separate calls.
    - `argo submit -n argo --watch <raw-github-URL>` works directly
      against a URL — no need to curl the file down first.
    - `kubectl get pods -n argo` right after install: expect exactly 2
      Running pods (argo-server, workflow-controller) for quick-start-
      minimal — a 3rd or a CrashLoopBackOff means something's off.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ──────────────────────────────
    ┌─ INSTALL ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │ kubectl create namespace argo                                         │
    │ kubectl apply --server-side -n argo -f \                              │
    │   "https://github.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/releases/download/      │
    │   ${ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION}/quick-start-minimal.yaml"                 │
    │ kubectl get pods -n argo         # expect 2 Running                   │
    ├─ CLI ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ curl -sLO "https://github.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/releases/       │
    │   download/${ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION}/argo-linux-amd64.gz"             │
    │ gunzip argo-linux-amd64.gz && chmod +x argo-linux-amd64 && \          │
    │   sudo mv argo-linux-amd64 /usr/local/bin/argo                        │
    ├─ ACCESS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ kubectl -n argo port-forward svc/argo-server 2746:2746                │
    │ argo auth token                  # bearer token for UI login          │
    ├─ FIRST WORKFLOW ───────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    │ argo submit -n argo --watch \                                         │
    │   https://raw.githubusercontent.com/argoproj/argo-workflows/main/     │
    │   examples/hello-world.yaml                                           │
    │ argo list -n argo                                                      │
    │ argo logs -n argo @latest                                             │
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~15 min, do-it-now)
    ────────────────────────────────────────────
    1. Create namespace argo; install quick-start-minimal.yaml WITH
       --server-side; confirm exactly 2 pods reach Running.
    2. Port-forward argo-server 2746:2746; get a token with `argo auth
       token`; open the UI (accept the self-signed cert warning).
    3. Submit the official hello-world.yaml with --watch; note the Pod
       name it creates; read its logs with `argo logs`.
    4. Run `kubectl get pods -n argo` again — is the hello-world pod
       still there? Why or why not (hint: podGC)?
    5. In one sentence, explain the difference between the Workflow
       Controller and the emissary executor.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. `kubectl apply -n argo -f quick-start-minimal.yaml` (no extra
        flags) fails with "metadata.annotations: Too long: must have at
        most 262144 bytes." Fix it.
    A1. Re-run with --server-side.
    Explanation: client-side apply stores the whole prior config in a
    last-applied-configuration annotation; Argo's bundled CRDs exceed
    the 256KiB cap. --server-side apply tracks ownership via
    managedFields on the API server instead — same fix as Argo CD's
    install (Ch 3).

    Q2. Name the two Pods you expect Running in argo right after a
        quick-start-minimal install. What still works if only one is up?
    A2. argo-server, workflow-controller. If only workflow-controller is
        Running, you can still submit/run Workflows via kubectl/argo
        against the K8s API — you just can't reach the UI/REST API.
    Explanation: Argo Server is a separate, optional API/UI layer.

    Q3. True/False: the Executor is a Deployment alongside argo-server
        and workflow-controller in the argo namespace.
    A3. False.
    Explanation: since v3.4 the only executor is emissary, a process
    running as an init container + sidecar INSIDE each step's own Pod —
    created per-step by the Controller, not a static Deployment.

    Q4. Write the DAG field making task C wait for BOTH A and B, using
        the classic list-based syntax.
    A4.
        - name: C
          dependencies: [A, B]
          template: ...
    Explanation: dependencies: (plural) takes a LIST of task names.
    depends: is a separate expression-based field — not interchangeable
    syntax for the same thing.

    Q5. A pipeline must clone, then run tests+lint in PARALLEL, then
        (only if both pass) build+push. Which native object can't
        express this, and what feature can?
    A5. A Kubernetes Job (one linear pod template, optionally N
        identical parallel copies) cannot express fan-out/fan-in
        dependencies; a DAG template's dependencies/depends fields can.
    Explanation: this is the core Structure row of the 9.2 comparison.

    Q6. Give the exact command to submit hello-world.yaml straight from
        GitHub into the argo namespace, blocking until it finishes.
    A6. argo submit -n argo --watch https://raw.githubusercontent.com/
        argoproj/argo-workflows/main/examples/hello-world.yaml
    Explanation: argo submit accepts a URL or local file; --watch
    streams status until a terminal phase (Succeeded/Failed/Error).

    Q7. quick-start-minimal.yaml is installed. A Workflow using
        outputs.artifacts fails to save its artifact. Why, and which
        quick-start manifest avoids this?
    A7. quick-start-minimal.yaml bundles neither MinIO nor a database —
        no default artifact repository is configured. quick-start-
        postgres.yaml bundles both MinIO (artifacts) and Postgres
        (archive).
    Explanation: "minimal" really does mean minimal — configure
    artifactRepository in workflow-controller-configmap yourself, or
    use the postgres variant.

    Q8. What 3 database engines does the Workflow Archive support, and
        what breaks entirely without one?
    A8. Postgres (>=9.4), MySQL (>=5.7.8), or MariaDB (>=10.2). Without
        one, `argo list --archived` returns nothing — completed
        Workflows vanish once garbage-collected off the cluster.
    Explanation: the archive is opt-in persistence layered on top of
    ephemeral in-cluster Workflow objects.

    Q9. A colleague says "Argo Workflows is basically CronJob with
        extra steps." Correct them in one sentence.
    A9. It's a graph engine, not a scheduler wrapper — e.g. withItems/
        withParam dynamic fan-out and native artifact passing between
        steps have no Job/CronJob equivalent; CronWorkflow is Argo's
        scheduler layer ON TOP of that graph engine.
    Explanation: reinforces the 9.2 table plus the CronWorkflow mapping.

    Q10. You install the server pinned to an older $ARGO_WORKFLOWS_
         VERSION, but grab the CLI from releases/latest/download/. What
         class of problem should you expect, and how do you avoid it?
    A10. Client/server API version skew — deprecated/renamed flags or
         fields one side doesn't understand. Avoid it by downloading the
         CLI from the SAME releases/download/${ARGO_WORKFLOWS_VERSION}/
         path used for the manifest.
    Explanation: pin client and server together; "latest" silently
    drifts over time.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 9 IN ONE PICTURE                                        │
    │                                                                    │
    │   YOU               K8s API              WORKFLOW CONTROLLER      │
    │   argo submit  -->  Workflow object  -->  watches, reads          │
    │   --watch           (Pending)             entrypoint template     │
    │        │                                        │                 │
    │        │                                        v                 │
    │        │                              creates 1 Pod per step:     │
    │        │                              [main container] +          │
    │        │                              [emissary: init+sidecar]    │
    │        │                                        │                 │
    │        v                                        v                 │
    │   status stream  <──────────────────  status.nodes updated        │
    │   (Succeeded)                          on the Workflow object     │
    │                                                                    │
    │   OPTIONAL: ARGO SERVER (API/UI/SSO) ── reads/writes archive ──>  │
    │   Postgres / MySQL / MariaDB  (only if quick-start-postgres.yaml  │
    │   or your own DB config was used — NOT quick-start-minimal.yaml)  │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: submitting a Workflow is the same watch-reconcile
    loop you already know from Argo CD, just aimed at Pods instead of
    generic manifests — the Controller reads the DAG, spawns one pod
    per step with a small emissary helper riding along for logs/
    artifacts/timeouts, and (only if you opted into a database) Argo
    Server archives the result. You now have a running install, a
    verified hello-world, and every term Chapters 10-13 will build on.


Chapter 10: Workflow Templates and Composition
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~3.5 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo Workflows 36% (Templates + DAG core, Artifacts intro) · 🏢 Daily-Work: high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    Chapter 9 got you a single Workflow that runs once and dies. That's
    fine for a demo — useless for easypj's real CI/CD, where every PR
    needs the SAME clone->test->build->push->update-GitOps pipeline
    without you retyping YAML each time. Chapter 10 turns that
    one-off script into a reusable, composable, shareable asset: a
    WorkflowTemplate the whole team calls by name, a DAG that runs
    lint/unit-tests/SAST in parallel instead of one-at-a-time, and
    artifacts (test reports, built images) that actually flow from
    one step to the next instead of vanishing when each step's pod
    terminates.

    ┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE CHAPTER 10              │  AFTER CHAPTER 10               │
    ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
    │ "I copy-paste my Workflow YAML  │ "I define easypj-ci-template    │
    │  into every new pipeline, and   │  ONCE; every PR submits         │
    │  each step runs one-at-a-time   │  workflowTemplateRef against    │
    │  even when nothing depends on   │  it. Lint, unit-tests, and SAST │
    │  the one before it."            │  fan out in parallel via DAG,   │
    │                                  │  and the test report artifact   │
    │                                  │  flows straight into build."    │
    └────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES
    ────────────────────────
      - Choose correctly between Workflow, WorkflowTemplate, and
        ClusterWorkflowTemplate for a given easypj scenario — and
        reference the right one with templateRef/workflowTemplateRef
        (including the clusterScope: true trap).
      - Pick the correct template type (Container, Script, Resource,
        Suspend, DAG, Steps — and know HTTP/ContainerSet/Data/Plugin
        exist) for a given pipeline step, not just "run a container."
      - Write a DAG using the modern `depends` expression syntax
        (not just legacy `dependencies` lists) to fan out easypj's
        test suite in parallel and fan back in before building.
      - Build a Steps template using withItems (static matrix) and
        withParam (dynamic list from a prior step's output).
      - Pass an artifact (e.g. a JUnit report or built image digest)
        from one DAG task to the next, and configure an S3/MinIO
        artifact repository correctly (including the ConfigMap-based
        cluster default).

    └───────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┘

    is the SOLE teaching chapter for 2 of the 16 graded CAPA competencies

    │  CHAPTER 10 — Workflow Templates and Composition            │
