    10.1  WorkflowTemplate vs Workflow
          - Workflow: One-time execution
          - WorkflowTemplate: Reusable blueprint in the cluster
          - ClusterWorkflowTemplate: Available cluster-wide
          [COMPARISON TABLE: Workflow vs WorkflowTemplate vs ClusterWorkflowTemplate]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  WORKFLOW vs WORKFLOWTEMPLATE vs CLUSTERWORKFLOWTEMPLATE    │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌──────────────┬───────────────┬───────────┬────────────┐│
          │  │ FIELD        │ Workflow      │ WFTemplate│ ClusterWFT ││
          │  ├──────────────┼───────────────┼───────────┼────────────┤│
          │  │ kind:        │ Workflow      │ Workflow  │ ClusterWork││
          │  │              │               │ Template  │ flowTemplate│
          │  │ Scope        │ 1 execution   │ namespace │ whole      ││
          │  │              │ (a run)       │           │ cluster    ││
          │  │ Runs on its  │ ✅ yes         │ ❌ no      │ ❌ no      ││
          │  │ own?         │ (has status)  │ (a blueprint, no run) ││
          │  │ Reused by    │ n/a           │ templateRef/ workflow ││
          │  │ other specs? │               │ TemplateRef (see below)││
          │  │ easypj use   │ ad-hoc one-   │ "run the  │ "run the   ││
          │  │              │ off migration │ CI pipe"  │ same DB    ││
          │  │              │ job           │ reused by │ backup job ││
          │  │              │               │ every PR  │ from ANY   ││
          │  │              │               │           │ namespace" ││
          │  └──────────────┴───────────────┴───────────┴────────────┘│
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          🔧 TWO WAYS TO CONSUME A TEMPLATE — don't confuse them:

          (a) Run an ENTIRE WorkflowTemplate AS a Workflow (top-level):
              apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
              kind: Workflow
              metadata: { generateName: easypj-ci- }
              spec:
                workflowTemplateRef:
                  name: easypj-ci-template      # WorkflowTemplate name
                  clusterScope: true             # <- REQUIRED if it's a
                                                  #    ClusterWorkflowTemplate
              # (also: argo submit --from workflowtemplate/easypj-ci-template)

          (b) Reference ONE template BY NAME from inside a DAG/Steps task
              (composition — reuse a single reusable step, not the whole
              template's entrypoint):
                - name: build
                  templateRef:
                    name: shared-build-steps      # WFTemplate/ClusterWFT name
                    template: docker-build         # the specific template
                    clusterScope: true             # same rule applies

          ⚠️ GOTCHA: omit clusterScope: true against a ClusterWorkflowTemplate
          and Argo looks for a NAMESPACED WorkflowTemplate of the same name
          instead — it fails with "not found," not a clear "wrong scope"
          error. This is the #1 trap CAPA tests on this topic.

          ⚠️ A WorkflowTemplate and a ClusterWorkflowTemplate CAN share the
          same metadata.name — the resolver picks based on clusterScope,
          not name uniqueness. Creating ClusterWorkflowTemplate objects
          needs cluster-scoped RBAC (like ClusterRole) — a team with only
          namespace access can define WorkflowTemplates but not these.

    10.2  Template Types Deep Dive
          - Container template: Run a container
          - Script template: Run a script in a container
          - Resource template: Create/update K8s resources
          - DAG template: Define a directed acyclic graph
          - Steps template: Define sequential/parallel steps
          - Suspend template: Wait for manual approval
          [DIAGRAM: Template types and their relationships]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  WORKFLOW TEMPLATE TYPES — Decision Guide                  │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
          │  │ TEMPLATE TYPE     │ WHEN TO USE                      │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Container         │ Run a single container with args │ │
          │  │                   │ (docker run equivalent)          │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Script            │ Run inline script (Python, Bash, │ │
          │  │                   │ Node) — source in YAML           │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Resource          │ Create/update/patch K8s objects  │ │
          │  │                   │ (e.g., create a ConfigMap)       │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ DAG               │ Complex pipelines with parallel  │ │
          │  │                   │ tasks and dependencies           │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Steps             │ Sequential steps with optional   │ │
          │  │                   │ parallelism via withItems        │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Suspend           │ Pause workflow, wait for manual  │ │
          │  │                   │ approval (prod deployments!)     │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Container Set     │ Multiple containers in ONE pod,  │ │
          │  │ (v3.1+)           │ sharing emptyDir — faster start  │ │
          │  │                   │ than DAG (no per-step pod churn) │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ HTTP (v3.2+)      │ Call a REST endpoint; response   │ │
          │  │                   │ body -> {{...outputs.result}}    │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Data (v3.1+)      │ Source + transform a data list   │ │
          │  │                   │ (e.g. filter S3 artifact paths)  │ │
          │  ├───────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Plugin            │ Executor plugin extends the      │ │
          │  │                   │ engine w/ a custom template kind │ │
          │  └───────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘ │
          │                                                            │
          │  TEMPLATE COMPOSITION:                                     │
          │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
          │  │  Workflow                                         │ │
          │  │  └── DAG Template (top-level)                      │ │
          │  │       ├── Container Template: "clone-repo"         │ │
          │  │       ├── Steps Template: "run-tests" (parallel)   │ │
          │  │       │    ├── Container: "unit-tests"             │ │
          │  │       │    └── Container: "lint-checks"            │ │
          │  │       ├── Container Template: "build-image"        │ │
          │  │       └── Suspend Template: "approve-deploy"       │ │
          │  │            └── (manual approval required)          │ │
          │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
          │                                                            │
          │  EXAM TIP: DAG for CI/CD, Steps for sequential processing, │
          │  Suspend for production gates. Know the differences!       │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          ⚠️ GOTCHA: only a SCRIPT template auto-captures stdout into the
          special `result` output ({{steps.x.outputs.result}} /
          {{tasks.x.outputs.result}}). A plain CONTAINER template does
          NOT — you must add an explicit outputs.parameters[].valueFrom.
          path, or switch the step to `script:` instead of `container:`.
          Forgetting this is why "{{tasks.build.outputs.result}}" often
          comes back empty for a Container-typed build step.

          🔑 RESOURCE template `action:` is one of: get | create | apply |
          delete | replace | patch. `patch` additionally needs
          mergeStrategy: strategic | merge | json.

          🔑 SUSPEND template isn't only "wait forever for a human":
                - name: approve-deploy
                  suspend: {}                 # indefinite; argo resume <wf>
                - name: cooldown
                  suspend:
                    duration: "10m"            # auto-resumes, no human needed

    10.3  DAG (Directed Acyclic Graph) Templates
          - Defining dependencies between tasks
          - Parallel execution of independent tasks
          - DAG example: CI pipeline for easypj (test → build → deploy)
          [SYNTAX: dependencies vs depends — DO NOT MIX in one DAG]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  dependencies: [A, B]      <- legacy list; C waits for ALL │
          │                               of A,B to be Succeeded/      │
          │                               Skipped/Daemoned             │
          │                                                            │
          │  depends: "A && B"         <- Enhanced Depends (v3.1+),    │
          │                               a boolean EXPRESSION string  │
          │                                                            │
          │  Result keywords usable in depends:                       │
          │    .Succeeded  .Failed  .Errored  .Skipped  .Omitted       │
          │    .Daemoned   (withItems: .AnySucceeded / .AllFailed)     │
          │  Operators: && || ! ( )                                   │
          │                                                            │
          │  depends: "(lint.Succeeded || lint.Skipped) && !test.Failed"│
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚠️ Can't mix dependencies + depends in the SAME DAG.       │
          │  ⚠️ continueOn does NOT work with depends — reference      │
          │     .Failed directly in the expression instead.            │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          [DAG DIAGRAM: CI pipeline as DAG]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  DAG WORKFLOW — CI Pipeline for easypj                     │
          │                                                            │
          │                  ┌──────────┐                              │
          │                  │  Clone   │                              │
          │                  │  Repo    │                              │
          │                  └────┬─────┘                              │
          │                       │                                    │
          │          ┌────────────┼────────────┐                       │
          │          │            │            │                       │
          │          ▼            ▼            ▼                       │
          │    ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐                 │
          │    │ Run Unit │ │ Run Lint │ │ Run SAST │                 │
          │    │  Tests   │ │   Checks │ │   Scan   │                 │
          │    └────┬─────┘ └────┬─────┘ └────┬─────┘                 │
          │         │            │            │                        │
          │         └────────────┼────────────┘                        │
          │                      │                                     │
          │                      ▼                                     │
          │               ┌──────────┐                                 │
          │               │ Build    │                                 │
          │               │ Docker   │                                 │
          │               │  Image   │                                 │
          │               └────┬─────┘                                 │
          │                    │                                       │
          │                    ▼                                       │
          │               ┌──────────┐                                 │
          │               │ Push to  │                                 │
          │               │ Registry │                                 │
          │               └────┬─────┘                                 │
          │                    │                                       │
          │                    ▼                                       │
          │               ┌──────────┐                                 │
          │               │ Update   │                                 │
          │               │ GitOps   │                                 │
          │               │ Repo     │                                 │
          │               └──────────┘                                 │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          [YAML: the diagram above, as an actual DAG template]
          templates:
          - name: ci-pipeline
            dag:
              tasks:
              - name: clone-repo
                template: git-clone
              - name: unit-tests
                depends: "clone-repo"
                template: run-tests
                arguments: { parameters: [{name: suite, value: unit}] }
              - name: lint-checks
                depends: "clone-repo"
                template: run-tests
                arguments: { parameters: [{name: suite, value: lint}] }
              - name: sast-scan
                depends: "clone-repo"
                template: run-tests
                arguments: { parameters: [{name: suite, value: sast}] }
              - name: build-image
                depends: "unit-tests && lint-checks && sast-scan"
                template: docker-build
              - name: push-registry
                depends: "build-image"
                template: docker-push
              - name: update-gitops-repo
                depends: "push-registry"
                template: git-commit-tag
          # Fan-out (clone -> 3 parallel checks) then fan-in
          # (3 checks -> build) is expressed purely by which task names
          # appear in each `depends` string — no separate "join" node.

          ⚠️ GOTCHA: a DAG runs independent branches in parallel by
          DEFAULT — there's no limit unless you set `parallelism:` on
          the Workflow (global) or the DAG template itself (per-DAG cap
          on simultaneously running tasks). Forgetting this on a fan-out
          of, say, 50 test-matrix tasks can flood the cluster with pods.

    10.4  Steps Templates — Sequential and Parallel
          - Inline vs templateRef
          - withItems: Run the same step with different inputs
          - withParam: Dynamic parameter expansion from JSON
          [CODE EXAMPLES: Steps with parallel execution]
          [YAML: easypj test matrix with withItems]
          - name: test-matrix
            steps:
            - - name: test-python-version
                template: run-django-tests
                arguments:
                  parameters:
                  - {name: python-version, value: "{{item.py}}"}
                  - {name: django-version, value: "{{item.dj}}"}
                withItems:
                - {py: "3.11", dj: "4.2"}
                - {py: "3.12", dj: "4.2"}
                - {py: "3.12", dj: "5.0"}
          # The 3 inner-list items run IN PARALLEL (same "- -" step row);
          # a NEW outer "- name:" row would instead run sequentially AFTER.

          [YAML: withParam sourced dynamically from a prior step's output]
          - - name: discover-migrations
              template: list-pending-migrations   # a Script template;
                                                    # stdout -> result
          - - name: apply-migration
              template: run-migration
              arguments:
                parameters:
                - {name: app, value: "{{item}}"}
              withParam: "{{steps.discover-migrations.outputs.result}}"
          # withParam expects a JSON ARRAY string — e.g. ["jobpost",
          # "skills","locations"] — usually produced by a prior Script
          # template's stdout, not typed by hand.

          🏢 DAILY-WORK: a Steps template's individual step doesn't have
          to point at a `template:` defined in the SAME Workflow — swap
          it for the `templateRef:` form from 10.1(b) to call a shared,
          centrally-maintained step (e.g. a security-team-owned
          "sast-scan" template) from every team's pipeline at once.
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  withItems vs withParam — SEQUENTIAL vs PARALLEL, AT A GLANCE│
          │                                                            │
          │  SEQUENTIAL (separate "- name:" rows):                     │
          │    - - name: step-A        ← row 1, runs FIRST              │
          │    - - name: step-B        ← row 2, runs AFTER row 1         │
          │                                                            │
          │  PARALLEL (same "- -" row, list expansion):                │
          │    - - name: test-python-version                            │
          │        withItems: [...]    ← ALL 3 items fire AT ONCE        │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌───────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐ │
          │  │           │ withItems            │ withParam            │ │
          │  ├───────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤ │
          │  │ Data known│ At YAML-write time    │ Only at RUN time     │ │
          │  │ when?     │                       │ (from a prior step)  │ │
          │  │ Input type│ Literal list in YAML  │ JSON array STRING    │ │
          │  │ Source    │ You type it            │ {{steps.X.outputs.  │ │
          │  │           │                        │  result}}            │ │
          │  │ easypj use│ Fixed py/dj version    │ Discover which       │ │
          │  │           │ matrix (3.11/3.12)      │ Django apps need     │ │
          │  │           │                         │ migrating, THEN loop │ │
          │  └───────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘ │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    10.5  Artifacts — Passing Data Between Steps
          - Input artifacts, output artifacts
          - Artifact repositories: S3, GCS, MinIO, Azure Blob
          - Plain text, JSON, binary, Docker images
          [DIAGRAM: Artifact passing between workflow steps]
          ⚠️ GOTCHA: MinIO is NOT its own YAML backend key — it's
          S3-API-compatible, so it's configured under `s3:` with a
          custom endpoint (matches the Ch 9 install: 4.5):
              outputs:
                artifacts:
                - name: test-report
                  path: /tmp/report.xml
                  s3:
                    endpoint: minio.argo.svc:9000
                    bucket: easypj-artifacts
                    key: "{{workflow.name}}/report.xml"
                    insecure: true              # MinIO w/o TLS
                    accessKeySecret: {name: minio-cred, key: accesskey}
                    secretKeySecret: {name: minio-cred, key: secretkey}

          Full verified backend list (all live under outputs.artifacts /
          inputs.artifacts): s3 (AWS S3 AND MinIO/any S3-compatible),
          gcs, azure (Azure Blob), hdfs, artifactory, http, oss
          (Alibaba Cloud), raw (a literal string, no external store —
          for tiny fixed artifacts), git, plugin.

          🏢 DAILY-WORK: don't hardcode S3 creds in every template. A
          ConfigMap named artifact-repositories in the argo namespace,
          annotated as default, is picked up automatically:
              apiVersion: v1
              kind: ConfigMap
              metadata:
                name: artifact-repositories
                annotations:
                  workflows.argoproj.io/default-artifact-repository: easypj-s3
              data:
                easypj-s3: |
                  s3: {bucket: easypj-artifacts, endpoint: minio:9000,
                       insecure: true,
                       accessKeySecret: {name: minio-cred, key: accesskey},
                       secretKeySecret: {name: minio-cred, key: secretkey}}
          Every Workflow in that namespace then needs ZERO artifact
          config — or pins a non-default one via:
              spec:
                artifactRepositoryRef: {configMap: artifact-repositories,
                                         key: easypj-s3}

          [YAML: passing an artifact between two DAG tasks]
          - name: unit-tests
            outputs:
              artifacts:
              - {name: junit-xml, path: /tmp/report.xml}
          - name: publish-results
            depends: "unit-tests"
            inputs:
              artifacts:
              - name: junit-xml
                path: /tmp/incoming-report.xml
            arguments:
              artifacts:
              - name: junit-xml
                from: "{{tasks.unit-tests.outputs.artifacts.junit-xml}}"
          # Between DAG tasks, use {{tasks.NAME...}}; between Steps use
          # {{steps.NAME...}} — same shape, different scope prefix.


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How templateRef Resolution Actually Survives Edits
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    A WorkflowTemplate is just a blueprint — nothing prevents someone
    from editing it (or its ClusterWorkflowTemplate cousin) WHILE a
    Workflow built from it is still running, or after it's finished but
    someone wants to `argo resubmit` it. Argo Workflows solves this with
    a snapshot, not a live reference: when a Workflow starts, the
    controller resolves every templateRef/workflowTemplateRef it needs
    and writes the resolved template specs into the Workflow's OWN
    status object — status.storedWorkflowTemplateSpec ("stores the
    WorkflowTemplate spec for future execution") and status.
    storedTemplates ("a mapping between a template ref and the node's
    status"). From that point on, the running Workflow reads from its
    OWN stored copy, not from the live WorkflowTemplate object.

    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  EDIT-DURING-RUN: what actually happens                     │
    │                                                              │
    │   easypj-ci-template (WorkflowTemplate, live in cluster)     │
    │        │ resolved ONCE, at submit time                       │
    │        v                                                     │
    │   Workflow.status.storedWorkflowTemplateSpec  <- frozen copy │
    │   Workflow.status.storedTemplates             <- frozen copy │
    │        │                                                     │
    │        v                                                     │
    │   Running/finished Workflow keeps executing against the      │
    │   FROZEN copy — someone editing easypj-ci-template mid-run   │
    │   affects the NEXT submission, never this one.                │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: templateRef and workflowTemplateRef feel like a live
    pointer, but behave like a copy taken at submit time — this is WHY
    a bad edit to a shared WorkflowTemplate can't retroactively break
    an in-flight production pipeline, and why "resubmit vs restart" in
    Ch 13 matters (resubmit re-resolves; retry does not).

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - Workflow = one run. WorkflowTemplate = namespaced blueprint.
      ClusterWorkflowTemplate = cluster-wide blueprint (needs
      clusterScope: true wherever it's referenced).
    - workflowTemplateRef runs a template's WHOLE entrypoint as a new
      Workflow; templateRef (inside a step) reuses ONE named template.
    - Only Script templates auto-populate {{...outputs.result}} from
      stdout — Container templates need explicit output wiring.
    - DAG dependencies: prefer `depends: "A && B"` (Enhanced Depends)
      over legacy `dependencies: [A, B]` — richer, but the two can't
      be mixed in one DAG, and continueOn doesn't work with depends.
    - MinIO/S3-compatible stores are configured under `s3:` — there is
      no separate `minio:` artifact field.
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: this chapter is the PRIMARY (often sole) teaching
       ground for "Understand Argo Workflow Templates" and "Work with
       DAG," and starts "Generating and Consuming Artifacts."

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Forgetting clusterScope: true when referencing a
       ClusterWorkflowTemplate — it silently looks for a namespaced
       WorkflowTemplate instead and returns "not found."
    2. Assuming a Container template's stdout is captured as `result`
       like a Script template's is — it isn't, without explicit
       outputs.parameters wiring.
    3. Mixing `dependencies` and `depends` in the same DAG — invalid.
    4. Writing `s3.type: minio` or a `minio:` block — MinIO is always
       configured as `s3:` with a custom `endpoint`.
    5. Using `continueOn` together with `depends` — it's ignored; use
       an explicit `.Failed` branch in the depends expression instead.
    6. Forgetting that DAG fan-out has no built-in concurrency cap —
       set `parallelism:` if the branch count is large.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - argo submit --from workflowtemplate/<name> — skip writing a
      wrapper Workflow YAML entirely for a quick manual run.
    - argo submit --from clusterworkflowtemplate/<name> — same, cluster-
      scoped.
    - argo template list / argo cluster-template list — fast inventory
      check before assuming a template doesn't exist yet.
    - argo lint <file.yaml> — catch a depends/dependencies mix-up or a
      missing entrypoint before submitting, not after.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑 — Consolidated Cheat-Card
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  TEMPLATES                                                   │
    │  argo template create <file.yaml>       (WorkflowTemplate)  │
    │  argo template list / get <name>                             │
    │  argo cluster-template create <file.yaml>                    │
    │  argo cluster-template list / get <name>                     │
    │                                                                │
    │  RUNNING FROM A TEMPLATE                                      │
    │  argo submit --from workflowtemplate/<name>                  │
    │  argo submit --from clusterworkflowtemplate/<name>           │
    │                                                                │
    │  VALIDATION                                                   │
    │  argo lint <file.yaml>                                       │
    │                                                                │
    │  ARTIFACTS                                                    │
    │  kubectl get cm artifact-repositories -n argo -o yaml         │
    │  argo get <workflow> -o yaml   (inspect resolved artifacts)  │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~15 min, do-it-now)
    ─────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Convert your Chapter 9 one-off Workflow into a WorkflowTemplate
         (kind: WorkflowTemplate) named easypj-ci-template; argo
         template create it.
      2. Submit it via argo submit --from workflowtemplate/
         easypj-ci-template — confirm it runs identically.
      3. Rewrite its dependencies: lists as depends: expressions; add
         a task that only runs on failure via .Failed.
      4. Add an outputs.artifacts block to your test step and consume
         it via {{tasks.NAME.outputs.artifacts.X}} in the next task.
      5. Create a ClusterWorkflowTemplate version of the same template;
         reference it with and WITHOUT clusterScope: true and observe
         the failure.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. You need the SAME CI pipeline available to every namespace in
        the cluster, not just easypj-dev. Which resource kind, and
        what must every referencing Workflow set?
    A1. kind: ClusterWorkflowTemplate; referencing Workflows must set
        clusterScope: true on their templateRef or workflowTemplateRef.
    Explanation: ClusterWorkflowTemplate is cluster-scoped like
    ClusterRole; clusterScope: true tells the resolver to look there
    instead of the namespaced WorkflowTemplate list.

    Q2. Run easypj-ci-template directly from the CLI without writing a
        wrapper Workflow YAML.
    A2. argo submit --from workflowtemplate/easypj-ci-template
    Explanation: --from builds a Workflow on the fly using the
    template's own entrypoint and spec.

    Q3. A DAG task's `depends: "build.Succeeded"` field never seems to
        omit the task even when `build` is skipped by a `when:`
        condition. Why, and how do you also allow that case?
    A3. `.Succeeded` alone excludes Skipped. Use
        depends: "build.Succeeded || build.Skipped".
    Explanation: unlike the implicit default for unspecified operands
    (Succeeded || Skipped || Daemoned), explicitly naming `.Succeeded`
    means ONLY Succeeded satisfies it.

    Q4. Your test step is a Container template, and
        {{tasks.unit-tests.outputs.result}} is always empty downstream.
        What's wrong and the fix?
    A4. Container templates don't auto-capture stdout into `result` —
        only Script templates do. Fix: convert the step to a `script:`
        template, or add an explicit outputs.parameters[].valueFrom.path.
    Explanation: this is a template-type behavior difference, not a
    bug — Container has no "result" magic.

    Q5. You have `dependencies: [lint, test]` on a build task and want
        to add a conditional "only if test did NOT fail." What must you
        do first?
    A5. Convert the whole task's field to depends: "lint.Succeeded &&
        !test.Failed" — dependencies and depends cannot coexist on the
        same DAG.
    Explanation: Enhanced Depends (depends) is a full replacement, not
    an additive field, for a given DAG.

    Q6. Configure easypj's test-report artifact to land in a local
        MinIO instance at minio.argo.svc:9000, bucket easypj-artifacts,
        without TLS.
    A6.
        outputs:
          artifacts:
          - name: test-report
            path: /tmp/report.xml
            s3:
              endpoint: minio.argo.svc:9000
              bucket: easypj-artifacts
              key: "{{workflow.name}}/report.xml"
              insecure: true
              accessKeySecret: {name: minio-cred, key: accesskey}
              secretKeySecret: {name: minio-cred, key: secretkey}
    Explanation: MinIO is S3-compatible — there is no separate `minio:`
    field; `insecure: true` disables TLS verification for the endpoint.

    Q7. Reduce artifact-repo boilerplate across every easypj Workflow
        in the argo namespace so credentials live in exactly one place.
    A7. Create a ConfigMap named artifact-repositories with the
        workflows.argoproj.io/default-artifact-repository annotation
        pointing at a data key holding the s3 config block; Workflows
        in that namespace need no artifact config at all.
    Explanation: this is the cluster-wide default artifact repository
    mechanism (artifactRepositoryRef with no explicit ref = use default).

    Q8. Fan out unit-tests, lint, and SAST in parallel after clone-repo,
        then only proceed to build-image once ALL three succeed. Write
        the three relevant `depends` lines.
    A8.
        - name: unit-tests
          depends: "clone-repo"
        - name: lint
          depends: "clone-repo"
        - name: sast-scan
          depends: "clone-repo"
        - name: build-image
          depends: "unit-tests && lint && sast-scan"
    Explanation: fan-out is 3 tasks depending on the same predecessor;
    fan-in is 1 task whose depends ANDs all 3 together.

    Q9. Run a 40-way test matrix (withItems) but cap the cluster to 5
        concurrently running test pods.
    A9. Add `parallelism: 5` to the DAG (or Steps) template — or to the
        Workflow spec for a global cap across the whole run.
    Explanation: withItems/withParam fan-out has no default concurrency
    limit; parallelism: is the throttle.

    Q10. A Resource template needs to patch an existing Deployment's
         replica count using a JSON Patch document instead of a
         strategic merge.
    A10.
         resource:
           action: patch
           mergeStrategy: json
           manifest: |
             apiVersion: apps/v1
             kind: Deployment
             metadata: {name: jobpost}
             ...
    Explanation: action: patch requires mergeStrategy, one of
    strategic | merge | json — json selects RFC 6902 JSON Patch
    semantics instead of a strategic merge.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 10 IN ONE PICTURE                                   │
    │                                                                │
    │   BLUEPRINT LAYER          COMPOSITION LAYER      DATA LAYER  │
    │   Workflow (1 run)         DAG: depends: "A && B"  Artifacts  │
    │   WorkflowTemplate    <->  Steps: withItems/Param  s3/gcs/    │
    │   ClusterWorkflow-         (templateRef reuses     azure/... │
    │   Template (+clusterScope)  ONE named template)               │
    │        │                        │                    │        │
    │        v                        v                    v        │
    │   workflowTemplateRef:     Container/Script/    outputs.      │
    │   runs the WHOLE thing     Resource/Suspend/     artifacts -> │
    │   as a new Workflow        HTTP/ContainerSet/    arguments.   │
    │                             Data/Plugin nodes     artifacts    │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: Chapter 10 is three nested decisions. WHERE does the
    pipeline definition live (Workflow vs WorkflowTemplate vs
    ClusterWorkflowTemplate — and how you reach it: workflowTemplateRef
    for the whole thing, templateRef for one piece, clusterScope: true
    whenever it's cluster-wide)? WHAT does each node in it DO (one of
    up to 10 template types — know that HTTP/ContainerSet/Data/Plugin
    exist beyond the classic 6)? And HOW do nodes relate and hand data
    to each other (DAG's depends expressions for control flow,
    outputs.artifacts/arguments.artifacts for the actual files)? Get
    those three questions automatic and Chapters 11-13 are just adding
    more verbs on top of this same skeleton.


Chapter 11: Advanced Workflow Features
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~3 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo Workflows 36% (Understand the Argo Workflow Spec + Run Data Processing Jobs) · 🏢 Daily-Work: high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    Chapter 9 gave you the engine; Chapter 10 gave you reusable
    blueprints. Neither one, on its own, survives contact with a real
    job: a flaky Postgres connection during easypj's migration step, a
    scrape job whose input list of job-board URLs isn't known until
    runtime, a CI run that must self-destruct if it hangs past 30
    minutes, a nightly backup that must NEVER run twice at once. This
    chapter is the difference between "a workflow that ran once" and
    "a workflow you'd trust in production" — retries that survive
    transient failures without you babysitting them, loops that fan
    out over dynamic data, memoization that stops re-scraping sources
    you already hit today, and a CronWorkflow that runs itself every
    night without a human touching kubectl.

    ┌───────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
    │ BEFORE CHAPTER 11              │ AFTER CHAPTER 11               │
    ├───────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
    │ One flaky step fails the whole │ retryStrategy auto-recovers,   │
    │ pipeline, every time            │ backoff-and-retry, no rerun    │
    │ Loop targets hand-written into  │ withParam iterates over data   │
    │ YAML at write-time               │ that only exists at runtime   │
    │ Re-scrapes the same source     │ memoize skips unchanged work,  │
    │ every single run                │ cache-hit in seconds           │
    │ No schedule — someone must run │ CronWorkflow + concurrencyPolicy│
    │ `argo submit` by hand nightly   │ runs itself, safely, forever   │
    │ Test namespaces pile up forever│ resource templates create AND  │
    │                                  │ delete their own scratch space │
    └───────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES — 🎯 What you'll be able to DO
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Pass parameters workflow→template→step, capture a step's
         output, and memoize an expensive step by its input key.
      2. Write when: conditionals (incl. the quoted-value gotcha) and
         choose correctly between withItems, withSequence, and
         withParam for a given data shape.
      3. Configure retryStrategy (limit/retryPolicy/backoff/
         expression) and set activeDeadlineSeconds at the right scope.
      4. Attach an onExit handler that branches on {{workflow.status}}.
      5. Share data between steps via a static PVC or a
         volumeClaimTemplates-managed volume, and know when to use
         which.
      6. Build a CronWorkflow with the correct concurrencyPolicy for a
         given scenario, and use resource templates + ttlStrategy/
         podGC to create and clean up K8s objects from a workflow.

    [D] CAPA RELEVANCE
    ───────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┐
    │ CAPA Competency                     │ Covered in this chapter   │
    ├────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────┤
    │ Understand the Argo Workflow Spec   │ 11.1-11.3 (parameters,    │
    │                                      │ when/loops, retry/timeout │
    │                                      │ fields are core Spec      │
    │                                      │ syntax questions)         │
    │ Run Data Processing Jobs with Argo  │ 11.2 (withParam over      │
    │ Workflows                           │ dynamic data), 11.4       │
    │                                      │ (shared volumes for ETL), │
    │                                      │ 11.5 (scheduled jobs)     │
    └────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────┘
    This chapter is the deepest Workflow-Spec chapter in the guide —
    retryStrategy field names, when: operators, and concurrencyPolicy
    values are among the most literally-quoted-from-YAML CAPA
    questions in the Workflows domain.

    [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM — you are here
    ────────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  11.1 Parameters/Inputs ───> 11.2 Conditionals & Loops        │
    │  (+ memoization)              (when/withItems/withParam/       │
    │        │                       withSequence/recursion)         │
    │        v                             │                        │
    │  11.3 Retry/Timeout/Error <──────────┘                        │
    │  Handling (retryStrategy/                                      │

    │  11.4 Volumes/Storage ──> 11.5 CronWorkflows ──> 11.6 Resource │
    │  (PVC / volumeClaim         (scheduled, cron-      Templates    │
    │   Templates)                 syntax, concurrency)  (create/delete│
