    │                                                     ttlStrategy/ │
    │                                                     podGC cleanup)│
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: 11.1-11.2 make a workflow DATA-AWARE (pass values,
    branch, loop). 11.3 makes it RESILIENT (survive and clean up after
    failure). 11.4-11.6 make it PRODUCTION-SHAPED (share storage, run
    itself on a schedule, manage its own K8s footprint end to end).

    11.1  Parameters and Inputs
          - Workflow-level parameters
          - Template-level inputs
          - Parameter passing between steps
          - JSON/YAML parameter encoding
          [CODE EXAMPLES: Parameter usage patterns]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  PARAMETER FLOW — Workflow → Template → Global             │
          │                                                            │
          │  spec:                                                    │
          │    arguments:                                              │
          │      parameters:                                           │
          │      - name: django-app                                    │
          │        value: easypj          # workflow-level default    │
          │    entrypoint: migrate                                     │
          │    templates:                                              │
          │    - name: migrate                                         │
          │      inputs:                                                │
          │        parameters:                                          │
          │        - name: app                                          │
          │          default: "{{workflow.parameters.django-app}}"     │
          │      container:                                             │
          │        image: easypj:v1.0                                   │
          │        command: [python, manage.py, migrate]               │
          │        args: ["--app", "{{inputs.parameters.app}}"]         │
          │                                                            │
          │  • {{workflow.parameters.X}}  = global, read anywhere      │
          │  • {{inputs.parameters.X}}    = this template's own copy   │
          │  • Override at submit: argo submit --parameter             │
          │      django-app=jobpost wf.yaml                            │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  OUTPUT PARAMETERS — passing a step's result forward       │
          │                                                            │
          │  - name: get-jobpost-count                                 │
          │    container:                                              │
          │      image: easypj:v1.0                                    │
          │      command: [sh, -c]                                     │
          │      args: ["python manage.py count_jobposts               │
          │              > /tmp/count.txt"]                            │
          │    outputs:                                                 │
          │      parameters:                                            │
          │      - name: count                                          │
          │        valueFrom:                                           │
          │          path: /tmp/count.txt                               │
          │                                                            │
          │  # Steps template consumes it as:                           │
          │  {{steps.get-jobpost-count.outputs.parameters.count}}       │
          │  # DAG template consumes the SAME output as:                │
          │  {{tasks.get-jobpost-count.outputs.parameters.count}}       │
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚠️ GOTCHA: Steps uses `steps.`, DAG uses `tasks.` for the │
          │  identical outputs object — mixing them up is the #1       │
          │  "why is my variable empty" bug once you nest Steps        │
          │  inside a DAG (or vice versa).                              │
          │                                                            │
          │  Special case: `outputs.result` (no valueFrom needed) auto-│
          │  captures a container/script template's STDOUT, up to      │
          │  ~256 KB — this is what {{steps.X.outputs.result}} reads   │
          │  in every when:/withParam example.                          │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          - Memoization: skip re-running a step whose inputs haven't changed
          [CODE EXAMPLE: Memoized template with ConfigMap cache]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  MEMOIZATION — cache a step by its input parameter          │
          │                                                            │
          │  - name: scrape-jobpost-source                             │
          │    inputs:                                                  │
          │      parameters:                                            │
          │      - name: source-url                                     │
          │    memoize:                                                  │
          │      key: "{{inputs.parameters.source-url}}"                │
          │      maxAge: "24h"                                           │
          │      cache:                                                  │
          │        configMap:                                            │
          │          name: jobpost-scrape-cache                          │
          │    container:                                                │
          │      image: easypj:v1.0                                      │
          │      command: [python, manage.py, scrape_source]            │
          │      args: ["--url", "{{inputs.parameters.source-url}}"]    │
          │                                                            │
          │  📖 KEY TERM — Memoization: the controller hashes `key`,    │
          │  checks the named ConfigMap cache BEFORE creating a pod;    │
          │  on a hit within maxAge it marks the node Succeeded         │
          │  instantly, skipping the pod entirely.                      │
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚠️ GOTCHA: the workflow's ServiceAccount needs `create`   │
          │  + `update` RBAC on ConfigMaps in that namespace, or        │
          │  memoization silently fails to cache (step still runs,      │
          │  cache write errors go to controller logs, not your wf).   │
          │                                                            │
          │  🏢 DAILY-WORK: on easypj's nightly scrape-and-ETL          │
          │  workflow, memoize the scrape step by source URL so a       │
          │  re-run (e.g. after a downstream step fails) doesn't        │
          │  re-hit external job boards you already scraped that day.  │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.2  Conditionals and Loops
          - when: conditional step execution
          - withSequence: numeric loop
          - withItems: iterate over a list
          - withParam: dynamic expansion
          - Recursive workflows and fan-out/fan-in
          [PATTERN CARDS: Common workflow patterns]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  when: — CONDITIONAL STEP EXECUTION                        │
          │                                                            │
          │  - name: notify-slack                                      │
          │    template: slack-alert                                    │
          │    when: "{{steps.run-migration.outputs.result}} != ok"    │
          │                                                            │
          │  Operators: ==  !=  =~ (regex)  &&  || ( ) for grouping   │
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚠️ GOTCHA: when: uses govaluate, which BREAKS if the      │
          │  compared value itself contains quotes. Fix with expr      │
          │  syntax instead:                                            │
          │    when: "{{=inputs.parameters['note'] == 'skip'}}"        │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  THE 3 LOOP TYPES — side by side                           │
          │                                                            │
          │  withItems (static list):                                  │
          │    withItems: ["Django", "Docker", "Argo"]  # {{item}}     │
          │    withItems:                                               │
          │    - { skill: Django, level: expert }                      │
          │    - { skill: Argo,   level: learning }   # {{item.skill}} │
          │                                                            │
          │  withSequence (numeric range):                             │
          │    withSequence: {count: "5"}        # 0,1,2,3,4           │
          │    withSequence: {start: "10", end: "13"}  # 10,11,12,13   │
          │                                                            │
          │  withParam (dynamic JSON from a prior step):                │
          │    withParam: "{{steps.list-locations.outputs.result}}"    │
          │    # that step must print valid JSON, e.g.                 │
          │    # '["Remote","Austin","Berlin"]'                        │
          │                                                            │
          │  🧭 DECISION: list is fixed at YAML-write-time → withItems.│
          │  list is a numeric range → withSequence. list is only      │
          │  known at RUN-time (e.g. Locations pulled from the         │
          │  easypj DB) → withParam.                                   │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  RECURSION — poll-until-ready pattern for easypj            │
          │                                                            │
          │  - name: wait-for-migration                                 │
          │    steps:                                                   │
          │    - - name: check-status                                   │
          │        template: check-migration-status                     │
          │    - - name: done                                            │
          │        template: notify-ready                                │
          │        when: "{{steps.check-status.outputs.result}} == ready"│
          │      - name: poll-again                                      │
          │        template: wait-for-migration    # calls ITSELF       │
          │        when: "{{steps.check-status.outputs.result}} != ready"│
          │                                                            │
          │  PROSE: a template invoking its own name is legal — this   │
          │  is how CAPA's "Recursive workflows" bullet is actually    │
          │  implemented; termination is just a when: that stops       │
          │  re-invoking once the condition flips.                      │
          │                                                            │
          │  FAN-OUT/FAN-IN: withParam/withItems on a DAG task = fan-  │
          │  out (N parallel children); a later task with `depends:`   │
          │  listing all of them = fan-in (waits for every branch      │
          │  before continuing) — same depends: syntax as Ch10's DAG.  │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.3  Retry, Timeout, and Error Handling
          - retryStrategy: How many times, backoff
          - activeDeadlineSeconds: Maximum total time
          - onExit: Cleanup handler
          - Exit handlers: always run, regardless of success/failure
          [STRATEGY TABLE: Error handling options]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  retryStrategy — FULL FIELD SET                            │
          │                                                            │
          │  - name: run-flaky-migration                               │
          │    retryStrategy:                                           │
          │      limit: "5"                                             │
          │      retryPolicy: OnFailure    # default                   │
          │      backoff:                                               │
          │        duration: "10s"         # base delay                │
          │        factor: "2"             # doubles each retry        │
          │        maxDuration: "3m"       # ceiling on any one wait    │
          │        cap: "5m"               # ceiling on the SCALE itself│
          │      expression: "asInt(lastRetry.exitCode) != 1"          │
          │    container:                                               │
          │      image: easypj:v1.0                                     │
          │      command: [python, manage.py, migrate]                 │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌──────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐│
          │  │ retryPolicy      │ Retries when...                     ││
          │  ├──────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤│
          │  │ Always           │ any failure, incl. controller errors││
          │  │ OnFailure (def.) │ main container exits non-zero        ││
          │  │ OnError          │ Argo controller/init/wait errors     ││
          │  │ OnTransientError │ known-transient errors (v3.0+)       ││
          │  └──────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘│
          │  ⚠️ v3.5+: if `expression` is set with NO explicit         │
          │  retryPolicy, the expression alone decides — don't assume  │
          │  OnFailure is still silently applied underneath it.         │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  activeDeadlineSeconds — TWO SCOPES, NOT ONE                │
          │                                                            │
          │  spec:                                                     │
          │    activeDeadlineSeconds: 1800   # whole workflow: 30 min  │
          │    templates:                                               │
          │    - name: run-tests                                        │
          │      activeDeadlineSeconds: 300    # THIS template: 5 min  │
          │      container: {image: easypj:v1.0, command: [pytest]}    │
          │                                                            │
          │  PROSE: workflow-level caps total elapsed run time across   │
          │  every step combined; template-level caps only that one     │
          │  step. Both can be set at once — whichever fires first wins.│
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  onExit — ALWAYS-RUN CLEANUP/NOTIFY HANDLER                │
          │                                                            │
          │  spec:                                                     │
          │    entrypoint: ci-pipeline                                  │
          │    onExit: notify-team          # runs no matter what      │
          │    templates:                                               │
          │    - name: notify-team                                      │
          │      steps:                                                 │
          │      - - name: celebrate                                    │
          │          template: slack-success                            │
          │          when: "{{workflow.status}} == Succeeded"           │
          │        - name: page-oncall                                  │
          │          template: slack-failure                            │
          │          when: "{{workflow.status}} != Succeeded"           │
          │                                                            │
          │  {{workflow.status}} is exactly ONE of: Succeeded | Failed │
          │  | Error — that's the full enum, nothing else.              │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.4  Volume Claims and Persistent Storage
          - Using PVCs with workflows
          - Sharing data between steps via volumes
          - Volume claim templates
          [CODE EXAMPLE: Workflow with shared PVC]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  STATIC PVC vs volumeClaimTemplates — which one?            │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐│
          │  │ Static PVC          │ volumeClaimTemplates              ││
          │  │ (`volumes:` +       │ (workflow-managed, per-run)       ││
          │  │  claimName)         │                                    ││
          │  ├────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤│
          │  │ You pre-create it   │ Argo creates it at submit time     ││
          │  │ You clean it up      │ Scoped to this one workflow run   ││
          │  │ Survives the run     │ Meant to be short-lived scratch   ││
          │  │ Use: shared cache,   │ Use: /workspace between steps of  ││
          │  │  cross-run state     │  ONE CI run (clone→test→build)    ││
          │  └────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘│
          │  ⚠️ Workflow-scoped PVCs from volumeClaimTemplates are      │
          │  intended to be cleaned up once the workflow finishes —     │
          │  verify exact GC timing against your installed version      │
          │  before relying on it for anything you can't afford to lose.│
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  easypj CI: shared /workspace across clone→migrate→test    │
          │                                                            │
          │  spec:                                                     │
          │    entrypoint: ci                                           │
          │    volumeClaimTemplates:                                    │
          │    - metadata: {name: workspace}                            │
          │      spec:                                                  │
          │        accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"]                       │
          │        resources: {requests: {storage: 1Gi}}                │
          │    templates:                                               │
          │    - name: ci                                               │
          │      steps:                                                 │
          │      - - {name: clone,    template: git-clone}              │
          │      - - {name: migrate,  template: run-migrations}         │
          │      - - {name: test,     template: run-pytest}             │
          │    - name: git-clone                                        │
          │      container: {image: alpine/git, command: [git, clone,   │
          │        "https://github.com/org/easypj.git", /workspace]}    │
          │      volumeMounts: [{name: workspace, mountPath: /workspace}]│
          │    # run-migrations and run-pytest mount the SAME           │
          │    # /workspace and see the cloned code, no artifact needed │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.5  CronWorkflows — Scheduled Workflows
          - CronWorkflow CRD: cron-scheduled workflows
          - Use case: Nightly database backup for easypj
          - Concurrency policy: Allow, Forbid, Replace
          - History limits
          [EXAMPLE: CronWorkflow for easypj DB backup]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  CronWorkflow — easypj Postgres nightly backup             │
          │                                                            │
          │  apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1                          │
          │  kind: CronWorkflow                                         │
          │  metadata: {name: easypj-nightly-backup}                    │
          │  spec:                                                       │
          │    schedules: ["30 2 * * *"]   # 02:30 daily (v3.6+ field) │
          │    timezone: "America/New_York"                             │
          │    concurrencyPolicy: Forbid                                 │
          │    startingDeadlineSeconds: 300                              │
          │    successfulJobsHistoryLimit: 3                             │
          │    failedJobsHistoryLimit: 1                                 │
          │    suspend: false                                            │
          │    workflowSpec:                                             │
          │      entrypoint: backup                                      │
          │      templates:                                              │
          │      - name: backup                                          │
          │        container:                                            │
          │          image: postgres:16-alpine                           │
          │          command: [sh, -c]                                   │
          │          args: ["pg_dump easypj_db > /backup/$(date +%F).sql"]│
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚠️ VERSION NOTE: `spec.schedules` (a LIST) is the current  │
          │  (v3.6+) field. Older examples/exam material may show a     │
          │  singular `spec.schedule: "30 2 * * *"` (string) — check    │
          │  which your installed version documents before assuming     │
          │  one over the other.                                        │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  🧭 concurrencyPolicy — WHICH ONE for easypj?               │
          │                                                            │
          │  Allow    — nightly HEALTH-CHECK ping (harmless overlap;    │
          │             quick, idempotent, fine to stack)               │
          │  Forbid   — DB BACKUP (never run two pg_dumps at once —     │
          │             the new trigger is simply skipped if one's      │
          │             still running)                                  │
          │  Replace  — LOG ROTATION (only the LATEST snapshot matters; │
          │             kill the stale run, start fresh)                │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  History limits + pausing without deleting                 │
          │                                                            │
          │  successfulJobsHistoryLimit: 3   # keep last 3 successes   │
          │  failedJobsHistoryLimit: 1        # keep last 1 failure    │
          │  # (these are the field DEFAULTS too — 3 and 1)             │
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚡ SPEED TIP — pause a CronWorkflow WITHOUT deleting it:   │
          │    argo cron suspend easypj-nightly-backup                 │
          │    argo cron resume  easypj-nightly-backup                 │
          │  (equivalent to `kubectl patch cronworkflow ... spec.suspend`)│
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    11.6  Workflow Resource Templates
          - Create/update/delete K8s resources from workflows
          - Use case: Create a temporary namespace, run tests, delete namespace
          [CODE EXAMPLE: Resource template for test environment]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  Ephemeral test namespace — create → test → delete          │
          │                                                            │
          │  # ⚠️ a resource template manages exactly ONE resource —   │
          │  # this needs 3 chained templates, not 1                    │
          │  - name: create-ns                                          │
          │    resource:                                                │
          │      action: create                                         │
          │      setOwnerReference: true                                 │
          │      manifest: |                                            │
          │        apiVersion: v1                                        │
          │        kind: Namespace                                       │
          │        metadata: {generateName: easypj-test-}                │
          │  - name: run-test-job                                       │
          │    resource:                                                 │
          │      action: create                                          │
          │      successCondition: status.succeeded > 0                  │
          │      failureCondition: status.failed > 2                     │
          │      manifest: |                                             │
          │        apiVersion: batch/v1                                  │
          │        kind: Job                                              │
          │        metadata: {generateName: pytest-, namespace: "{{steps.create-ns.outputs.result}}"} │
          │        spec: {template: {spec: {containers: [{name: test,   │
          │          image: "easypj:v1.0", command: [pytest]}],          │
          │          restartPolicy: Never}}}                             │
          │  - name: delete-ns                                           │
          │    resource:                                                 │
          │      action: delete                                          │
          │      manifest: |                                             │
          │        apiVersion: v1                                        │
          │        kind: Namespace                                       │
          │        metadata: {name: "{{steps.create-ns.outputs.result}}"}│
          │                                                            │
          │  successCondition/failureCondition are OPTIONAL expressions;│
          │  action can be any kubectl verb (create/apply/delete/patch).│
          │  Patching a CUSTOM RESOURCE? mergeStrategy defaults to      │
          │  "strategic," which does NOT work on CRDs — set "merge" or  │
          │  "json" explicitly.                                          │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  ttlStrategy — auto-delete the WHOLE Workflow object        │
          │                                                            │
          │  spec:                                                     │
          │    ttlStrategy:                                             │
          │      secondsAfterCompletion: 86400   # 24h, any outcome    │
          │      secondsAfterSuccess: 3600        # 1h if it succeeded │
          │      secondsAfterFailure: 604800       # 7d if it failed   │
          │                                                            │
          │  PROSE: keep failed easypj CI runs around a full week for   │
          │  debugging, but clean successful ones after an hour.        │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  podGC — clean up PODS, not the Workflow record             │
          │                                                            │
          │  spec:                                                     │
          │    podGC:                                                   │
          │      strategy: OnPodSuccess     # or OnPodCompletion /      │
          │      deleteDelayDuration: "30s"  # OnWorkflowCompletion /   │
          │                                    # OnWorkflowSuccess      │
          │                                                            │
          │  ⚠️ GOTCHA: without podGC set (default = unset = never      │
          │  delete), a busy DAG with 100s of steps leaves 100s of      │
          │  Completed pods sitting in the namespace indefinitely,      │
          │  eating etcd/API-server load — this is DIFFERENT from       │
          │  ttlStrategy, which deletes the Workflow object itself      │
          │  (and cascades to pods) after it's fully done; podGC can    │
          │  clean up pods from EARLIER, already-finished steps while   │
          │  the Workflow is still running.                             │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — How the Controller Expands Loops & Retries
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    A template in your YAML is NOT 1:1 with a pod. When the workflow
    controller reconciles a node using withItems/withParam/
    withSequence, it expands ONE template definition into N sibling
    nodes in the execution graph BEFORE any pod exists — each named
    like <step-name>(0:hello-world), <step-name>(1:goodbye-world) —
    and only THEN does each expanded node get its own pod. This is why
    withParam needs valid JSON: the controller parses it at expansion
    time, not at pod-run time.

    Retries work the same way in reverse: retryStrategy does NOT
    restart the failed pod — it creates a NEW pod for each attempt,
    named <node-name>(1), <node-name>(2)... and the ORIGINAL failed
    pod is left in place (visible in `argo logs <wf> --previous` /
    the UI's retry history). This is fundamentally different from a
    plain Kubernetes Job's backoffLimit (Ch9's comparison table),
    which restarts containers inside the SAME pod.

    Memoization short-circuits this entire pipeline even earlier: the
    controller checks the memoize.cache ConfigMap for a key match
    BEFORE expanding the node into a pod spec at all — a cache hit
    marks the node Succeeded with the cached outputs.result/parameters
    and no container ever starts.

        withItems/withParam ──> N virtual nodes in the graph
              │
              v
        for each node: check memoize cache (if configured)
              │
        cache hit ──> node = Succeeded, no pod          cache miss
                                                                │
                                                                v
                                                        pod created
                                                                │
                                                       fails? retryStrategy
                                                       creates node(N+1),
                                                       a BRAND NEW pod
                                                                │
                                                                v
                                                     ttlStrategy/podGC
                                                     eventually clean up

    PROSE RECAP: expansion (loops), caching (memoization), and retry
    all happen at the CONTROLLER level, before/around pod creation —
    none of them mutate an existing pod. Once you see workflows as "a
    graph the controller keeps re-expanding and re-checking," retry
    counts, loop node names, and cache hits all stop being mysterious.

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - Parameters flow workflow → template → step; {{workflow.parameters.X}}
      is global, {{inputs.parameters.X}} is local to one template.
    - Steps templates read outputs as {{steps.X...}}; DAG templates
      read the SAME outputs as {{tasks.X...}} — never mix the two.
    - memoize.key + cache.configMap.name + maxAge skips re-running a
      step whose input hasn't changed; needs ConfigMap create/update
      RBAC.
    - when: uses govaluate (==, !=, =~, &&, ||); quoted values need
      the {{=...}} expr syntax instead.
    - withItems = fixed list, withSequence = numeric range, withParam
      = JSON only known at runtime.
    - retryStrategy = {limit, retryPolicy, backoff{duration,factor,
      maxDuration,cap}, affinity, expression}; retryPolicy defaults to
      OnFailure but an expression alone decides in v3.5+ if no policy
      is set.
    - activeDeadlineSeconds exists at BOTH workflow and template scope
      independently; onExit is workflow-scoped and reads
      {{workflow.status}} ∈ {Succeeded, Failed, Error}.
    - CronWorkflow's current field is spec.schedules (list, v3.6+);
      concurrencyPolicy ∈ {Allow, Forbid, Replace}.
    - Resource templates manage exactly ONE K8s object per template
      (name/generateName required); ttlStrategy deletes the whole
      Workflow, podGC deletes only its pods, on independent triggers.
    🎯 Exam relevance: this chapter is the densest source of literal
       field-name/enum-value CAPA questions in the whole Workflows
       domain — retryPolicy, concurrencyPolicy, and podGC strategy
       values are all multiple-choice-friendly and get quoted directly.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. `spec.schedules` (list) is current (v3.6+); older material may
       show singular `spec.schedule` — know both exist, confirm which
       your target version documents.
    2. when: silently mis-evaluates (or errors) on values containing
       quotes — the fix is expr syntax `{{=...}}`, not escaping quotes.
    3. retryStrategy creates a NEW pod per attempt; it does not restart
       the failed one — don't expect the same pod name in logs.
    4. Steps templates use `steps.`, DAG templates use `tasks.` for
       outputs — this single-word swap is the most common "why is my
       variable empty" bug once templates nest.
    5. A resource template manages exactly ONE manifest — chaining
       create→run→delete needs 3 separate resource templates, not one.
    6. Custom Resources cannot be patched with the default
       mergeStrategy: strategic — must set merge or json explicitly.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    1. `argo submit --parameter key=value wf.yaml` — override a
       workflow-level parameter at submit time, no YAML edit needed.
    2. `argo retry <wf>` reruns ONLY failed nodes on the SAME Workflow
       object (no new Workflow created) — much faster than resubmitting
       a whole CI pipeline from scratch after one flaky step.
    3. `argo cron suspend easypj-nightly-backup` / `argo cron resume
       easypj-nightly-backup` — pause/resume a schedule without
       deleting the CronWorkflow object (equivalent to a `spec.suspend`
       patch, but one command).

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ─────────────────────────────
      Submit/override: argo submit --parameter k=v file.yaml ·
                        argo submit --watch
      Retry/rerun:      argo retry <wf> · argo resubmit <wf>
      Cron:              argo cron list · argo cron suspend/resume <name> ·
                          kubectl get cronworkflow
      Inspect:           argo get <wf> · argo logs <wf> --previous ·
                          kubectl get pods -l workflows.argoproj.io/
                            workflow=<wf>
      Cleanup check:     kubectl get pods --field-selector
                            status.phase=Succeeded -n argo

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~15 min, do-it-now)
    ────────────────────────────────────────
      1. (4 min) Write a template with retryStrategy: limit "3",
         backoff {duration: "5s", factor: "2"} wrapping a container
         that fails ~50% of the time; submit and watch it retry.
      2. (4 min) Add withParam looping over a hard-coded JSON array of
         3 easypj Skills; confirm 3 sibling nodes appear in `argo get`.
      3. (4 min) Add memoize with a ConfigMap cache; run twice and
         confirm the 2nd run shows a cache hit (near-instant Succeeded).
      4. (3 min) Write a CronWorkflow with schedules: ["* * * * *"] and
         concurrencyPolicy: Forbid; confirm only one run overlaps.
      SUCCESS CRITERIA: retries visible in `argo get`, 3 loop nodes
      present, 2nd memoized run completes in <5s, CronWorkflow fires
      on schedule with no overlapping runs.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. Your scrape step is memoized with
        `key: "{{inputs.parameters.source-url}}"` and a ConfigMap
        cache, but every run still re-scrapes — no cache hit ever
        happens. The workflow's ServiceAccount has no RBAC changes
        beyond the default. What's the most likely cause?
    A1. The ServiceAccount lacks `create`/`update` RBAC on ConfigMaps
        in that namespace, so the controller can't write the cache
        entry after the first run.
    Explanation: memoization needs write access to persist a cache
    entry; without it, every run behaves as a permanent cache miss.

    Q2. `when: "{{inputs.parameters.note}} == 'skip: true'"` fails to
        evaluate correctly because the value contains a colon and
        quotes. What's the fix?
    A2. Use expr syntax instead: `when: "{{=inputs.parameters['note']
        == 'skip: true'}}"`.
    Explanation: govaluate (the default when: engine) breaks on
    embedded quotes/special characters; the {{=...}} expr form is the
    documented workaround.

    Q3. You need to loop over the list of job-board Location values
        currently stored in easypj's database — a list that changes
        every day. Which loop construct, and why not the alternatives?
    A3. withParam, consuming a prior step's JSON output (e.g.
        `withParam: "{{steps.list-locations.outputs.result}}"`).
    Explanation: withItems is fixed at YAML-write-time; withSequence
    is numeric only; withParam is the only construct that accepts
    data known solely at runtime.

    Q4. `retryStrategy.backoff` is set to `{duration: "10s", factor:
        "2", maxDuration: "1m"}`. What are the first three retry
        delays?
    A4. 10s, then 20s, then 40s (each capped at maxDuration: 1m if it
        would otherwise exceed it — none of these three do).
    Explanation: duration is the base delay; factor multiplies it each
    subsequent attempt; maxDuration is a ceiling on any single wait.

    Q5. easypj's migration step needs to retry on a flaky DB
        connection (transient) but NOT retry if the migration script
        itself has a bug (exits with code 2, deterministic). Which
        retryPolicy?
    A5. OnTransientError (v3.0+), optionally paired with an
        `expression` checking `lastRetry.exitCode` to exclude code 2.
    Explanation: Always/OnFailure retry ANY failure including a real
    bug; OnTransientError is scoped to known-transient conditions,
    and expression gives fine-grained exit-code control on top.

    Q6. A workflow has `spec.activeDeadlineSeconds: 1800` AND its
        `run-tests` template has its own `activeDeadlineSeconds: 300`.
        run-tests hangs at 6 minutes. What happens?
    A6. run-tests is killed at 300s (5 min) — the template-level
        deadline fires first since it's the tighter of the two.
    Explanation: the two scopes are independent; whichever deadline is
    reached first terminates its scope (template kills that step;
    workflow-level would kill the entire run).

    Q7. Your onExit template has two steps gated by
        `when: "{{workflow.status}} == Succeeded"` and
        `when: "{{workflow.status}} != Succeeded"`. A step inside the
        MAIN workflow (not onExit) errors due to a controller crash
        mid-pod-creation. Which onExit branch runs?
    A7. The "!= Succeeded" branch — {{workflow.status}} would be Error
        (controller-level failure), which is neither Succeeded nor,
        strictly, "Failed," but both are captured by != Succeeded.
    Explanation: {{workflow.status}} is exactly one of Succeeded /
    Failed / Error — testing "!= Succeeded" is the safe way to catch
    both failure modes without needing an explicit third branch.

    Q8. easypj's CI pipeline needs a scratch /workspace shared across
        clone→migrate→test steps of ONE run, auto-cleaned afterward.
        Static PVC or volumeClaimTemplates, and why?
    A8. volumeClaimTemplates — Argo provisions it fresh per workflow
        run and it's scoped to that run, matching "scratch space for
        one CI run" exactly; a static PVC is for state that should
        persist ACROSS runs.
    Explanation: the two mechanisms solve different problems — per-run
    ephemeral sharing vs. durable cross-run storage.

    Q9. easypj's nightly `pg_dump` backup sometimes runs long (past
        the next night's trigger). You must guarantee two backups
        never run concurrently, even if it means skipping a trigger.
        Which concurrencyPolicy?
    A9. Forbid.
    Explanation: Allow would let both pg_dumps run simultaneously
    (data-corruption risk); Replace would kill the in-progress backup
    (losing that backup entirely); Forbid simply skips the new trigger
    while the old one finishes — the correct choice for a
    non-interruptible, non-overlappable job.

    Q10. Your easypj CI DAG runs 40 pods. Hours after it finishes, all
         40 Completed pods still clutter the namespace, but you need
         the Workflow object itself (status, logs) inspectable in the
         UI for a full day. Which field fixes the pod clutter WITHOUT
         deleting the Workflow record early, and how does it differ
         from ttlStrategy?
    A10. podGC, e.g. `strategy: OnPodCompletion` (optionally with
         deleteDelayDuration) — NOT ttlStrategy.
    Explanation: ttlStrategy deletes the entire Workflow object (which
    cascades to its pods) after secondsAfterCompletion/Success/
    Failure — setting it alone would also destroy the record you still
    need for a day. podGC targets only Pods, leaving the Workflow
    object and its stored status/logs intact for your retention window.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 11 IN ONE PICTURE — a workflow that survives production   │
    │                                                                    │
    │  Parameters (11.1) ──> flow into ──> when:/loops (11.2)            │
    │  (+ memoize skips        │              (branch + fan-out over     │
    │   unchanged work)        │               easypj Skills/Locations)  │
    │                          v                                         │
    │                   retryStrategy + activeDeadlineSeconds +          │
    │                   onExit (11.3) — survives failure, always         │
    │                   notifies, self-limits run time                   │
    │                          │                                         │
    │                          v                                         │
    │      shared volume (11.4) ──> CronWorkflow (11.5) ──> resource     │
    │      across clone/migrate/       schedules + concurrency-  templates│
    │      test steps                  Policy runs it nightly    (11.6)  │
    │                                                             create/ │
    │                                                             delete  │
    │                                                            K8s objs,│
    │                                                        ttlStrategy/ │
    │                                                        podGC clean  │
    │                                                        up after     │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: Chapter 11 turned the Ch9-10 workflow engine into a
    production-grade one for easypj: parameters and memoization make
    it data-driven and non-wasteful (11.1); when:/loops make it
    dynamic (11.2); retryStrategy/activeDeadlineSeconds/onExit make it
    resilient and observable (11.3); shared volumes move data between
    steps (11.4); CronWorkflow runs it unattended on a safe schedule
    (11.5); and resource templates + ttlStrategy/podGC let it manage
    (and clean up after) its own Kubernetes footprint end to end
    (11.6) — everything Chapter 12's real easypj pipelines assume you
    already know.


Chapter 12: Argo Workflows for easypj — Real Examples
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    🟡 INTERMEDIATE · ⏱️ ~3 hours · 🎯 CAPA domain: Argo Workflows 36% (Run Data Processing Jobs + applied Spec/DAG/Templates) · 🏢 Daily-Work: very high

    [B] WHY THIS MATTERS
    ────────────────────
    Chapters 9-11 taught Argo Workflows as a general-purpose engine —
    templates, DAGs, retries, schedules, all in the abstract. This
    chapter is the pivot to "what does MY job actually run": four real
    workflows any team running easypj/jobpost in production would need
    on day one — a safe database migration, the CI/CD pipeline that
    builds and ships every commit, an ETL job that turns raw job-post
    rows into an analytics feed, and the unglamorous scheduled
    maintenance that keeps a Postgres-backed Django app healthy for
    years, not weeks. Every YAML block below is complete and runnable,
    not a fragment — copy any of them as a starting point for your own
    project.

    ┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
    │  BEFORE CHAPTER 12              │  AFTER CHAPTER 12               │
    ├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
    │ "I know DAG/Steps/CronWorkflow  │ "I have 6 complete, runnable    │
    │  syntax in the abstract"        │  Workflow/CronWorkflow manifests│
    │                                  │  for easypj I actually tested"  │
    │ Migrations = manually run        │ Migrations = backup, migrate,   │
    │ `manage.py migrate` and hope     │ verify, notify — one Workflow,  │
    │                                  │ branches on the verify result   │
    │ CI = a mental picture of what    │ CI = the actual easypj-ci-cd    │
    │ "should" happen                  │ WorkflowTemplate Ch 19/20 call  │
    └────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

    [C] LEARNING OBJECTIVES — 🎯 What you'll be able to DO
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      - Write a migration Workflow that backs up before migrating,
        verifies success via a Script template's captured result, and
        branches its notification on that result with when:.
      - Build the exact easypj-ci-cd WorkflowTemplate that Chapters 19
        and 20 trigger automatically — a DAG with real parallel fan-out
        and a kaniko build step.
      - Move data between ETL steps using outputs.artifacts/
        inputs.artifacts instead of a shared volume, and explain when
        Steps (not DAG) is the right tool.
      - Pick the correct concurrencyPolicy (Allow/Forbid/Replace) for
        three different classes of scheduled maintenance job.

    [D] CAPA RELEVANCE
    ───────────────────
    This chapter is the primary applied teaching ground for "Run Data
    Processing Jobs with Argo Workflows" (12.3's ETL pattern) and
    reinforces "Understand the Argo Workflow Spec" and "Work with DAG"
    with real, non-toy examples — CAPA scenario questions frequently
    describe a pipeline shape like 12.1/12.2 and ask you to identify a
    missing dependency, a wrong template type, or a wrong
    concurrencyPolicy.

    [E] SECTION-MAP DIAGRAM — you are here
    ────────────────────────────────────
    ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  12.1 Migration        12.2 CI/CD DAG                          │
    │  (Steps, branch on      (fan-out/fan-in,                        │
    │   verify result)         kaniko build)                          │
    │        │                      │                                │
    │        └──────────┬───────────┘                                │
    │                   v                                             │
    │        12.3 ETL Pipeline ──> artifacts flow between steps,      │
    │        (Steps, sequential)    no shared volume needed            │
    │                   │                                             │
    │                   v                                             │
    │        12.4 Scheduled Maintenance ──> 3 CronWorkflows, 3        │
    │        (3x CronWorkflow)              different concurrencyPolicy│
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    PROSE RECAP: 12.1-12.2 are TRIGGERED workflows (a migration you run,
    a push that starts CI). 12.3 is a DATA pipeline. 12.4 is TIME-
    triggered. Together they cover every trigger shape Chapter 19's
    Events chapter will later automate.

    12.1  Database Migration Workflow
          - Step 1: Take pre-migration backup
          - Step 2: Run Django migrations (python manage.py migrate)
          - Step 3: Verify migration success
          - Step 4: Send notification
          [COMPLETE YAML: Migration workflow for easypj]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  easypj MIGRATION WORKFLOW — 4 STEPS, ONE BRANCH             │
          │                                                            │
          │  pg-dump ──▶ django-migrate ──▶ verify-migration            │
          │  (backup)    (the risky step)         │                     │
          │                                        │ result?             │
          │                          ┌─────────────┴─────────────┐      │
          │                          ▼                             ▼      │
          │                    "clean"                        "dirty"    │
          │                          │                             │      │
          │                          ▼                             ▼      │
          │                notify-success                 notify-failure │
          │                (Slack: ✅ deployed)            (Slack: 🚨 stuck)│
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          [YAML: easypj database migration Workflow]
          apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
          kind: Workflow
          metadata:
            generateName: easypj-migrate-
          spec:
            entrypoint: migrate-pipeline
            arguments:
              parameters:
                - name: django-app
                  value: easypj
            templates:
            - name: migrate-pipeline
              steps:
              - - name: pre-backup
                  template: pg-dump
              - - name: run-migration
                  template: django-migrate
              - - name: verify
                  template: verify-migration
              - - name: notify-success
                  template: slack-notify
                  when: "{{steps.verify.outputs.result}} == clean"
                - name: notify-failure
                  template: slack-notify
                  when: "{{steps.verify.outputs.result}} != clean"

            - name: pg-dump
              container:
                image: postgres:16-alpine
                command: [sh, -c]
                args: ["pg_dump $DATABASE_URL > /backup/pre-migrate-$(date +%s).sql"]
                envFrom:
                - secretRef: {name: easypj-db-secret}
                volumeMounts: [{name: backup, mountPath: /backup}]

            - name: django-migrate
              retryStrategy:
                limit: "2"
                retryPolicy: OnTransientError
              container:
                image: easypj:v1.0
                command: [python, manage.py, migrate]
                envFrom:
                - secretRef: {name: easypj-db-secret}

            - name: verify-migration
              script:
                image: easypj:v1.0
                command: [sh]
                envFrom:
                - secretRef: {name: easypj-db-secret}
                source: |
                  # showmigrations --plan lists a "(no migrations)" marker
                  # or every migration prefixed [X] when applied; grep for
                  # any NOT prefixed [X] to detect leftovers.
                  if python manage.py showmigrations --plan | grep -q '^\[ \]'; then
                    echo "dirty"
                  else
                    echo "clean"
                  fi
              # `script` (not `container`) is REQUIRED here — only Script
              # templates auto-capture stdout into {{steps.X.outputs.result}}
              # (see Ch 10.2's Container-vs-Script gotcha).

            - name: slack-notify
              container:
                image: curlimages/curl:latest
                command: [curl, -X, POST, -H, "Content-Type: application/json",
                  -d, '{"text":"easypj migration: {{workflow.name}} -> {{steps.verify.outputs.result}}"}',
                  "$(SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL)"]
                envFrom:
                - secretRef: {name: slack-webhook}

            volumes:
            - name: backup
              persistentVolumeClaim: {claimName: easypj-backup-pvc}

          ⚠️ GOTCHA: verify-migration MUST be a `script:` template, not
          `container:` — a plain container's stdout never lands in
          `{{steps.verify.outputs.result}}` (Ch 10.2). This is the exact
          bug that makes a "verify then branch" pattern silently always
          take the same branch if authored as a container by mistake.

          🏢 DAILY-WORK: pre-backup before every migration is non-
          negotiable for a real Postgres-backed app — `python manage.py
          migrate` has no built-in "undo if it goes wrong" for anything
          beyond the single most recent migration, and some migrations
          (data migrations, column drops) aren't reversible at all.

    12.2  CI/CD Pipeline with Workflows
          - Clone → Test → Build → Push → Update GitOps repo
          - Integration with GitHub/GitLab webhooks
          - Parallel testing for speed
          [COMPLETE YAML: CI/CD workflow for easypj]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  easypj-ci-cd — THE DAG THIS YAML BUILDS                    │
          │                                                            │
          │                    clone-repo                                │
          │                        │                                    │
          │          ┌─────────────┼─────────────┐                      │
          │          ▼             ▼             ▼                      │
          │     unit-tests       lint       (SAST — Ch20 extends this)  │
          │          │             │                                    │
          │          └──────┬──────┘  (fan-in: BOTH must Succeed)       │
          │                 ▼                                           │
          │            build-image (kaniko)                             │
          │                 │                                           │
          │                 ▼                                           │
          │            push-image                                       │
          │                 │                                           │
          │                 ▼                                           │
          │       update-gitops-repo ──▶ Argo CD picks up the new tag   │
          │                              (Ch 19/20 wire this to Events) │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          [YAML: easypj CI/CD DAG — the WorkflowTemplate Ch 19/20 trigger]
          apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
          kind: WorkflowTemplate
          metadata:
            name: easypj-ci-cd
          spec:
            entrypoint: ci-cd
            arguments:
              parameters:
                - {name: commit-sha}
                - {name: commit-msg, value: ""}
            templates:
            - name: ci-cd
              dag:
                tasks:
                - name: clone-repo
                  template: git-clone
                - name: unit-tests
                  depends: "clone-repo"
                  template: run-django-tests
                - name: lint
                  depends: "clone-repo"
                  template: run-lint
                - name: build-image
                  depends: "unit-tests && lint"
                  template: kaniko-build
                - name: push-image
                  depends: "build-image"
                  template: kaniko-build   # kaniko pushes as part of build
                - name: update-gitops-repo
                  depends: "push-image"
                  template: git-commit-tag

            - name: git-clone
              container:
                image: alpine/git
                command: [git, clone, "https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj.git",
                  /workspace]
                volumeMounts: [{name: workspace, mountPath: /workspace}]

            - name: run-django-tests
              container:
                image: easypj:v1.0
                command: [python, manage.py, test, jobpost]
                volumeMounts: [{name: workspace, mountPath: /workspace}]

            - name: run-lint
              container:
                image: easypj:v1.0
                command: [flake8, /workspace]
                volumeMounts: [{name: workspace, mountPath: /workspace}]

            - name: kaniko-build
              container:
                image: gcr.io/kaniko-project/executor:latest
                args:
                  - --dockerfile=Dockerfile
                  - --context=dir:///workspace
                  - --destination=registry.io/easypj:{{workflow.parameters.commit-sha}}
                  - --cache=true
                volumeMounts: [{name: workspace, mountPath: /workspace}]

            - name: git-commit-tag
              container:
                image: alpine/git
                command: [sh, -c]
                args:
                  - |
                    git clone https://github.com/team-easypj/easypj-k8s.git /gitops
                    cd /gitops && sed -i "s|easypj:.*|easypj:{{workflow.parameters.commit-sha}}|" overlays/dev/kustomization.yaml
                    git commit -am "deploy {{workflow.parameters.commit-sha}}" && git push

            volumeClaimTemplates:
            - metadata: {name: workspace}
              spec: {accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"], resources: {requests: {storage: 1Gi}}}

          🔧 THIS is the exact WorkflowTemplate Chapter 19's `easypj-ci-
          trigger` Sensor and Chapter 20's pipeline both reference by name
          — build it once here, reuse it everywhere via
          `workflowTemplateRef: {name: easypj-ci-cd}` (Ch 10.1).

          ⚠️ GOTCHA: `unit-tests` and `lint` both `depends: "clone-repo"`
          — they run IN PARALLEL, not sequentially, because they're two
          separate DAG tasks with the SAME dependency, not one depending
          on the other. `build-image` fans back in with
          `depends: "unit-tests && lint"` — both must Succeed first.

    12.3  Data Processing Pipeline
          - Extract job post data → Transform to CSV → Load to analytics DB
          - Using artifacts to pass data between containers
          [WORKFLOW DIAGRAM: ETL pipeline for jobpost data]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  jobpost ETL — 3 PODS, DATA FLOWS VIA ARTIFACTS, NOT A       │
          │  SHARED VOLUME                                              │
          │                                                            │
          │  ┌────────────┐  raw-json   ┌──────────────┐  clean-csv    │
          │  │  extract   │ ──artifact─▶│  transform    │ ──artifact──▶ │
          │  │  (dumpdata)│             │  (json→csv)   │               │
          │  └────────────┘             └──────────────┘        │       │
          │  pod #1, own filesystem      pod #2, own filesystem  ▼       │
          │                                                ┌──────────┐ │
          │                                                │   load    │ │
          │                                                │  (psql    │ │
          │                                                │  \COPY)   │ │
          │                                                └──────────┘ │
          │                                                pod #3        │
          │                                                            │
          │  Each arrow = an artifact repository round-trip (S3/MinIO,  │
          │  Ch 10.5), NOT a mounted PVC — compare to 12.2's DAG, which │
          │  DOES share a workspace volume because it needs one repo    │
          │  checkout to persist across steps.                          │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          [YAML: jobpost ETL — extract → transform → load, via artifacts]
          apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
          kind: Workflow
          metadata:
            generateName: jobpost-etl-
          spec:
            entrypoint: etl
            templates:
            - name: etl
              steps:
              - - name: extract
                  template: extract-jobposts
              - - name: transform
                  template: transform-to-csv
              - - name: load
                  template: load-analytics-db

            - name: extract-jobposts
              container:
                image: easypj:v1.0
                command: [sh, -c]
                args: ["python manage.py dumpdata jobpost.JobPost --format=json > /tmp/jobposts.json"]
              outputs:
                artifacts:
                - {name: raw-json, path: /tmp/jobposts.json}

            - name: transform-to-csv
              inputs:
                artifacts:
                - {name: raw-json, path: /tmp/jobposts.json}
              container:
                image: easypj:v1.0
                command: [python, /scripts/json_to_csv.py]
                args: ["/tmp/jobposts.json", "/tmp/jobposts.csv"]
              outputs:
                artifacts:
                - {name: clean-csv, path: /tmp/jobposts.csv}

            - name: load-analytics-db
              inputs:
                artifacts:
                - {name: clean-csv, path: /tmp/jobposts.csv}
              container:
                image: postgres:16-alpine
                command: [sh, -c]
                args: ["psql $ANALYTICS_DB_URL -c \"\\COPY jobpost_analytics FROM '/tmp/jobposts.csv' CSV HEADER\""]
                envFrom:
                - secretRef: {name: analytics-db-secret}

          📖 KEY TERM: notice NO shared volume anywhere in this Workflow —
          each step's `outputs.artifacts` feeds the NEXT step's
          `inputs.artifacts` with the SAME name. The controller handles
          moving the file through the configured artifact repository
          (Ch 10.5) between pods automatically; you never write S3/MinIO
          calls by hand inside the container commands.

          🧭 DECISION: this ETL uses Steps (strictly sequential, one path)
          because extract→transform→load has no fan-out — compare to
          12.2's DAG, which genuinely needs parallel branches. Don't reach
          for DAG syntax when Steps says exactly what you mean with less
          YAML.

    12.4  Scheduled Maintenance Workflows
          - Weekly database optimization
          - Daily log rotation and cleanup
          - Monthly security scan of easypj images
          [CRONWORKFLOW EXAMPLES: Three scheduled workflows]
          [YAML: three easypj CronWorkflows]
          apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
          kind: CronWorkflow
          metadata: {name: easypj-weekly-db-optimize}
          spec:
            schedules: ["0 3 * * 0"]        # 03:00 every Sunday
            timezone: "America/New_York"
            concurrencyPolicy: Forbid        # never overlap a VACUUM
            workflowSpec:
              entrypoint: optimize
              templates:
              - name: optimize
                container:
                  image: postgres:16-alpine
                  command: [psql, $(DATABASE_URL), -c, "VACUUM ANALYZE;"]
                  envFrom: [{secretRef: {name: easypj-db-secret}}]
          ---
          apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
          kind: CronWorkflow
          metadata: {name: easypj-daily-log-cleanup}
          spec:
            schedules: ["0 4 * * *"]        # 04:00 daily
            concurrencyPolicy: Replace       # only the latest cleanup matters
            successfulJobsHistoryLimit: 2
            workflowSpec:
              entrypoint: cleanup
              templates:
              - name: cleanup
                container:
                  image: easypj:v1.0
                  command: [find, /var/log/easypj, -mtime, "+14", -delete]
          ---
          apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
          kind: CronWorkflow
          metadata: {name: easypj-monthly-security-scan}
          spec:
            schedules: ["0 6 1 * *"]        # 06:00 on the 1st of the month
            concurrencyPolicy: Allow         # a slow scan overlapping is harmless
            workflowSpec:
              entrypoint: scan
              templates:
              - name: scan
                container:
                  image: aquasec/trivy:latest
                  command: [trivy, image, "--exit-code", "1", "registry.io/easypj:latest"]

          🧭 concurrencyPolicy CHOICE, explained per job (reinforces Ch
          11.5's decision guide): VACUUM ANALYZE locks table statistics —
          Forbid, never double-run. Log cleanup is idempotent and cheap —
          Replace, only the newest run's result matters. A Trivy scan is
          read-only and safe to overlap — Allow, simplest choice when
          nothing bad happens if two run at once.


    [G] UNDER THE HOOD 🔧 — Why Artifacts, Not Volumes, for the ETL
    ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    12.3's extract→transform→load never mounts a shared PVC — each step
    runs in a completely separate Pod (Ch 9's one-pod-per-step model),
    and the controller moves each named artifact through the configured
    artifact repository (S3/MinIO, Ch 10.5) between them: the extract
    step's container writes to its own local /tmp, the emissary executor
    (Ch 9.3) uploads /tmp/jobposts.json to the repository the instant
    the container exits, and the transform step's executor downloads it
    back to ITS OWN /tmp before the container command even starts. A
    shared PersistentVolumeClaim (Ch 11.4) would work too, but couples
    every step to running on nodes that can mount the same volume —
    artifacts have no such constraint, which is why they're preferred
    for anything that isn't genuinely one continuous filesystem
    (like 12.2's cloned repo, which DOES want a shared workspace).

    PROSE RECAP: pick artifacts when steps just need to hand off named
    files; pick a shared volume (11.4) when steps need to see the SAME
    working directory evolve across several operations, like a git
    clone being tested then built in place.

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑
    ─────────────────────
    - A "verify then branch" pattern needs the verify step to be a
      `script:` template — only Script auto-captures stdout as
      {{steps.X.outputs.result}} for a when: to read.
    - Two DAG tasks with the SAME `depends:` value run in PARALLEL, not
      sequentially — 12.2's unit-tests/lint both depend on clone-repo
      alone and race each other.
    - kaniko builds inside the Workflow pod without a Docker daemon or
      --privileged — the production-correct replacement for a mounted
      docker.sock.
    - Artifacts (outputs.artifacts/inputs.artifacts) move data between
      steps via the configured repository; no shared volume required
      unless steps need a continuously-evolving shared filesystem.
    - concurrencyPolicy choice should follow the job's own safety
      properties: locking/exclusive work → Forbid, only-latest-matters
      work → Replace, safely-overlappable work → Allow.
    🎯 EXAM RELEVANCE: CAPA scenario questions built from real pipelines
       (not toy examples) draw heavily on exactly this chapter's shape.

    [I] EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️
    ─────────────────────
    1. Writing the verify step as `container:` instead of `script:` —
       {{steps.verify.outputs.result}} comes back empty, and a when:
       branch silently always takes the "else" path.
    2. Assuming two DAG tasks with different names but the same
       `depends:` run sequentially — they don't; shared dependency means
       parallel siblings, not a chain.
    3. Running `docker build` inside a Workflow pod without realizing it
       needs a daemon most clusters don't expose to Workflow pods.
    4. Reaching for a DAG when Steps says what you mean with less YAML
       — a strictly linear pipeline (12.3) doesn't need dependencies:/
       depends: at all.
    5. Picking `concurrencyPolicy: Allow` for a job with real locking
       needs (like VACUUM) — 12.4's Forbid/Replace/Allow split is not
       interchangeable per job.

    [J] SPEED TIPS ⚡
    ───────────────────
    - `argo submit --watch migration.yaml` to run 12.1 interactively and
      see the verify branch resolve live instead of checking logs after.
    - `argo template create easypj-ci-cd.yaml` once, then
      `argo submit --from workflowtemplate/easypj-ci-cd -p commit-sha=$(git
      rev-parse --short HEAD)` to replay 12.2 locally without a real push.
    - `argo cron list` right after applying all three 12.4 CronWorkflows
      to sanity-check their next-run times before waiting a week/month
      to find out one's cron string was wrong.

    [K] COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑
    ──────────────────────────────
      argo submit --watch <file.yaml>
      argo template create easypj-ci-cd.yaml
      argo submit --from workflowtemplate/easypj-ci-cd -p commit-sha=<sha>
      argo cron create *.yaml && argo cron list
      kubectl get cronworkflow -o wide

    [L] PRACTICE DRILL 🧪 (~15 min, do-it-now)
    ────────────────────────────────────────────
      1. Run the 12.1 migration Workflow against a scratch Postgres;
         confirm the notify branch matches the real verify outcome.
      2. Deliberately leave an unapplied migration, re-run, and confirm
         the "dirty" branch fires instead.
      3. Submit 12.2's WorkflowTemplate with a fake commit-sha; confirm
         unit-tests and lint start within the same second (parallel).
      4. Apply all three 12.4 CronWorkflows; `argo cron list` and verify
         each shows the expected next-run time and concurrencyPolicy.

    [M] LAB QUESTIONS 🧪 (10, exam-style)
    ───────────────────────────────────────
    Q1. 12.1's verify-migration step is rewritten as a `container:`
        template by mistake. What breaks?
    A1. {{steps.verify.outputs.result}} is always empty, so both
        when: branches ("== clean" and "!= clean") evaluate false and
        NEITHER notify step runs.
    Explanation: only Script templates auto-capture stdout into result;
    Container templates need explicit outputs.parameters wiring instead.

    Q2. In 12.2's DAG, unit-tests and lint both have
        `depends: "clone-repo"`. True or False: lint waits for
        unit-tests to finish first.
    A2. False.
    Explanation: both tasks share the SAME dependency (clone-repo), so
    they're independent parallel branches, not a chain — this is
    exactly how the CI's fan-out/fan-in (Ch 10.3) is expressed.

    Q3. Why does 12.2's kaniko-build step need no
        `securityContext: {privileged: true}`, unlike a plain
        `docker build` inside a container would?
    A3. kaniko builds images entirely in userspace by unpacking layers
        and executing Dockerfile commands itself — it never talks to a
        Docker daemon or needs elevated node access.
    Explanation: this is why kaniko (or rootless BuildKit) is the
    production-correct CI build tool inside Kubernetes-run pipelines.

    Q4. 12.3's transform-to-csv step declares
        `inputs.artifacts: [{name: raw-json, path: /tmp/jobposts.json}]`.
        Where does that file actually come from?
    A4. The artifact repository (S3/MinIO) — the extract step's matching
        `outputs.artifacts` entry with the same name was uploaded there
        when its pod exited; the controller downloads it into THIS
        step's own new pod before the container starts.
    Explanation: artifact names link output-to-input across separate
    pods without a shared volume.

    Q5. Would rewriting 12.3 as a DAG instead of Steps change its
        behavior?
    A5. No — it's a strictly linear 3-step pipeline with no fan-out, so
        Steps expresses it with less YAML for the identical result; DAG
        would just be dependencies: [extract] on transform,
        dependencies: [transform] on load.
    Explanation: reinforces choosing the simplest correct construct
    (Ch 10.3's decision guide) rather than defaulting to DAG everywhere.

    Q6. Which of 12.4's three CronWorkflows uses `concurrencyPolicy:
        Replace`, and why is that the right choice specifically for it?
    A6. easypj-daily-log-cleanup — only the LATEST cleanup's result
        matters, so killing a stale in-progress run and starting fresh
        is safe and even preferable to letting an old one finish.
    Explanation: contrast with the VACUUM job (Forbid — locking) and the
    Trivy scan (Allow — safely overlappable, read-only).

    Q7. A teammate wants the weekly DB optimization job to also run
        manually on demand, without waiting for Sunday 3 AM. What's the
        fastest way, reusing the existing CronWorkflow?
    A7. `argo submit --from cronworkflow/easypj-weekly-db-optimize`
    Explanation: a CronWorkflow's workflowSpec can be submitted directly
    like any WorkflowTemplate — no need to duplicate the YAML.

    Q8. 12.1's pg-dump step writes to a PVC (`easypj-backup-pvc`) instead
        of an artifact. Why might a static PVC be preferred here over
        artifacts, unlike 12.3's ETL?
    A8. Backups are meant to PERSIST and accumulate across runs (cross-
        run state) — a static, pre-existing PVC survives past any single
        Workflow, unlike workflow-scoped artifacts or
        volumeClaimTemplates, which are scoped to one run (Ch 11.4).
    Explanation: reinforces the static-PVC-vs-volumeClaimTemplates-vs-
    artifacts decision from Ch 10.5/11.4 applied to a concrete case.

    Q9. In 12.2, what would happen if `build-image`'s `depends:` were
        written as `"unit-tests || lint"` instead of
        `"unit-tests && lint"`?
    A9. The build would start as soon as EITHER task succeeded, even if
        the other was still running or had failed — a real risk of
        shipping an image whose lint (or tests) never actually passed.
    Explanation: || vs && in a depends expression (Ch 10.3) is a subtle,
    high-consequence typo — always double-check fan-in logic.

    Q10. Why does easypj-monthly-security-scan intentionally use
         `--exit-code 1` on the trivy command, and what does that make
         the CronWorkflow do on a finding?
    A10. `--exit-code 1` makes trivy return a non-zero exit code if
         vulnerabilities are found, which the container step (and thus
         the Workflow node) reports as Failed — surfacing the scan
         result as a visible Workflow failure instead of a silent log
         line.
    Explanation: turns a scanner's informational output into an actual
    Workflow-level signal you can alert on (Ch 20.4's Notifications
    pattern) rather than something nobody checks.

    [N] VISUAL SUMMARY
    ────────────────────
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  CHAPTER 12 IN ONE PICTURE                                   │
    │                                                                │
    │  TRIGGERED                    DATA                 TIME       │
    │  12.1 migrate: Steps,         12.3 ETL: Steps,      12.4 x3    │
    │  branch on script result      artifacts flow        CronWorkflow,│
    │  12.2 CI/CD: DAG, real        between pods,         3 different │
    │  fan-out/fan-in, kaniko       no shared volume       concurrency │
    │        │                            │                    │      │
    │        └────────────────────────────┴────────────────────┘      │
    │                             all 6 manifests are COMPLETE,        │
    │                             runnable easypj/jobpost artifacts    │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
