    25.1  Lab 1: Install and Configure Argo CD
          - Install Argo CD on a fresh cluster
          - Create an Application for easypj
          - Configure auto-sync with pruning
          [LAB: Step-by-step with verification points]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  LAB 1 AT A GLANCE — what you'll have at the end            │
          │                                                            │
          │  fresh cluster ──▶ argocd namespace (6 pods Running)        │
          │                          │                                  │
          │                          ▼                                  │
          │           Application: easypj-dev (automated+prune+selfHeal)│
          │                          │                                  │
          │                          ▼                                  │
          │           jobpost Pod Running in easypj-dev namespace       │
          │           (manual drift you introduce gets auto-reverted)   │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          1. (2 min) Confirm a clean target: `kubectl cluster-info &&
             kubectl get nodes` (STATUS=Ready). Reusing an earlier
             cluster? `kubectl delete namespace argocd --ignore-not-found`
             first for a clean install.
          2. (5 min) Install: `kubectl create namespace argocd` then
             `kubectl apply -n argocd -f <Argo CD stable install manifest
             — see Appendix A>`.
             ✅ VERIFY: `kubectl get pods -n argocd` — all Running
             (argocd-server, -repo-server, -application-controller,
             -redis, -dex-server, -applicationset-controller).
          3. (2 min) Access: `kubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n
             argocd 8080:443` (background), then
             `argocd admin initial-password -n argocd` to read the
             bootstrap admin password.
          4. (2 min) CLI login: `argocd login localhost:8080 --username
             admin --password <printed-password> --insecure`.
             ✅ VERIFY: `argocd version` prints both client + server.
          5. (5 min) Create the easypj Application (either declaratively —
             reuse Chapter 3's easypj-dev-app.yaml shape — or via the CLI,
             the more exam-realistic path):
                argocd app create easypj-dev \
                  --repo <your-gitops-repo-url> --path overlays/dev \
                  --dest-server https://kubernetes.default.svc \
                  --dest-namespace easypj-dev \
                  --sync-policy automated --auto-prune --self-heal
          6. (3 min) Sync + verify:
             ✅ VERIFY: `argocd app get easypj-dev` → HEALTH=Healthy,
             SYNC=Synced; `kubectl get pods -n easypj-dev` → easypj Pod
             Running; `kubectl port-forward -n easypj-dev svc/easypj
             8000:80` then load the jobpost home page.
          7. (3 min) Prove self-heal, not just sync: manually
             `kubectl scale deployment easypj -n easypj-dev --replicas=5`,
             then watch Argo CD revert it to Git's declared count (poll
             window, or force with `argocd app sync easypj-dev`).
          ✅ SUCCESS CRITERIA: Synced+Healthy; manual drift auto-reverted;
          jobpost reachable.
          ⚠️ IF STUCK: "ComparisonError" → wrong repo URL/branch. App
          invisible to `argocd app list` → it's not in the `argocd`
          namespace (the exact trap from Chapter 2, Lab Question 3).

    25.2  Lab 2: Argo Workflows Pipeline
          - Create a CI pipeline as a WorkflowTemplate
          - Use DAG with parallel test steps
          - Pass artifacts between steps
          [LAB: Step-by-step with verification points]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  LAB 2 AT A GLANCE                                          │
          │                                                            │
          │  easypj-ci-pipeline (WorkflowTemplate)                      │
          │        │                                                    │
          │        ▼                                                    │
          │  clone-repo ──▶ [test-unit ‖ test-lint] ──▶ build/push       │
          │                  (run in PARALLEL — same DAG                │
          │                   depends: "clone-repo")                    │
          │        │                                                    │
          │        ▼                                                    │
          │  Workflow Phase=Succeeded; DAG view shows overlapping        │
          │  start times for test-unit/test-lint (proves parallel)      │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          1. (3 min) Install (if not already from Ch9): `kubectl create
             namespace argo` then apply the Argo Workflows install
             manifest (Appendix B). ✅ VERIFY: `kubectl get pods -n argo`
             all Running; `argo version` succeeds.
          2. (8 min) Write WorkflowTemplate `easypj-ci-pipeline`
             (entrypoint `ci-dag`) with a DAG:
                clone-repo --> [test-unit, test-lint]  (parallel fan-out)
                                        │
                              (both must succeed)
                                        v
                build-image --> push-image --> update-gitops-repo
             Give `clone-repo` a `retryStrategy: {limit: 2}` (transient
             git-clone failures are common and this is a real exam trap).
          3. (4 min) Wire an artifact: `clone-repo` outputs the checked-out
             source directory as an output artifact; `build-image`
             declares it as an input artifact — satisfies "pass artifacts
             between steps" for real, not just via a shared volume.
          4. (5 min) Register + run:
                argo template create easypj-ci-pipeline.yaml -n argo
                argo submit --from workflowtemplate/easypj-ci-pipeline \
                  -n argo --watch
          5. (5 min) Inspect: `argo list -n argo` (Succeeded); `argo logs
             @latest -n argo` (every step's stdout); open the DAG view
             (`kubectl port-forward svc/argo-server -n argo 2746:2746`)
             and visually confirm the fan-out/fan-in shape.
          ✅ SUCCESS CRITERIA: Workflow Phase=Succeeded; both test steps
          show overlapping start times (ran in parallel, not sequentially);
          build/push/update-gitops-repo only ran after BOTH tests passed.
          ⚠️ IF STUCK: Stuck Pending → image pull error, `kubectl describe
          pod -n argo <pod>`. Artifact step fails → no artifact repository
          configured (MinIO or an `emptyDir`-backed default).

    25.3  Lab 3: Canary Deployment with Rollouts
          - Convert easypj Deployment to Rollout
          - Configure canary strategy with steps
          - Set up AnalysisTemplate with Prometheus
          [LAB: Step-by-step with verification points]
          1. (3 min) Install (if not already from Ch15): `kubectl create
             namespace argo-rollouts` + install manifest (Appendix C) +
             the `kubectl argo rollouts` plugin. ✅ VERIFY: `kubectl argo
             rollouts version`.
          2. (5 min) Convert: change `kind: Deployment` → `kind: Rollout`,
             `apiVersion: apps/v1` → `argoproj.io/v1alpha1`, name it
             `easypj-rollout`, add:
                strategy.canary.steps:
                  - setWeight: 20
                  - pause: {duration: 60s}
                  - setWeight: 50
                  - pause: {duration: 60s}
                  - setWeight: 100
          3. (4 min) Add AnalysisTemplate `easypj-success-rate`
             (Prometheus provider) gating on an HTTP 2xx-ratio query
             against easypj's ingress metrics (reuse Chapter 16's
             Prometheus setup); reference it via an `analysis` step
             inserted between two `setWeight` steps.
          4. (4 min) Trigger a new revision: bump easypj's image tag in
             the GitOps repo (new git-sha); let Argo CD sync it in — this
             starts the Rollout automatically.
          5. (2 min) Watch live: `kubectl argo rollouts get rollout
             easypj-rollout --watch` — weight progression + AnalysisRun
             status stream in the terminal.
          6. (2 min) Prove BOTH outcomes once each: let one canary
             progress cleanly to 100%; then push a deliberately broken
             image tag and confirm the AnalysisRun fails and the Rollout
             auto-aborts (or `kubectl argo rollouts undo easypj-rollout`
             as the manual fallback).
          ✅ SUCCESS CRITERIA: `kubectl argo rollouts status
          easypj-rollout` → "Healthy" after the good revision; "Degraded"
          with the last-good ReplicaSet still stable after the bad one.
          ⚠️ IF STUCK: stuck at a `pause` step with no timer running →
          that's a MANUAL pause (no `duration`) — needs `kubectl argo
          rollouts promote easypj-rollout`. AnalysisRun stuck Pending →
          Prometheus not reachable from inside the cluster.

    25.4  Lab 4: Event-Driven Automation
          - Create webhook EventSource
          - Create Sensor that triggers Workflow
          - Test end-to-end: webhook → workflow → CD sync
          [LAB: Step-by-step with verification points]
          ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
          │  LAB 4 AT A GLANCE                                          │
          │                                                            │
          │  curl (simulated GitHub push) ──▶ easypj-webhook            │
          │                                    (EventSource)             │
          │                                         │                    │
          │                                         ▼                    │
          │                              easypj-deploy-sensor            │
          │                                         │ submits             │
          │                                         ▼                    │
          │                              a real Workflow appears in       │
          │                              `argo list` — nobody typed       │
          │                              `argo submit` by hand            │
          └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          1. (3 min) Install (if not already from Ch17): `kubectl create
             namespace argo-events` + install manifest + EventBus (NATS).
          2. (4 min) Create EventSource `easypj-webhook` (type: webhook,
             port 12000, endpoint `/easypj-deploy`).
          3. (5 min) Create Sensor `easypj-deploy-sensor`: dependency on
             `easypj-webhook`; trigger = submit a Workflow from
             WorkflowTemplate `easypj-ci-pipeline` (built in 25.2),
             parameterized with the webhook payload's commit SHA.
          4. (2 min) Expose for testing: `kubectl port-forward
             svc/easypj-webhook-eventsource-svc -n argo-events
             12000:12000`.
          5. (2 min) Fire it — no `argo submit`, no kubectl apply, just:
                curl -X POST http://localhost:12000/easypj-deploy \
                  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
                  -d '{"ref":"refs/heads/main","after":"<fake-sha>"}'
          6. (4 min) Verify the FULL chain, not just one link:
             `argo list -n argo` → a NEW Workflow appears that nobody
             submitted by hand; once it completes and updates the GitOps
             repo, `argocd app get easypj-dev` shows a fresh
             OutOfSync→Synced transition with no human touching Argo CD.
          ✅ SUCCESS CRITERIA: one `curl` results, unattended, in a
          running Workflow AND (minutes later) a new Argo CD sync.
          ⚠️ IF STUCK: `curl` hangs → wrong service name, check `kubectl
          get svc -n argo-events`. Sensor never fires → `dependencies[]`
          `eventName`/`eventSourceName` don't exactly (case-sensitively)
          match the EventSource.

    25.5  Lab 5: Full CAPA Simulation
          - 10 tasks across all 4 domains
          - 60-minute timed simulation
          - Scoring rubric and self-assessment
          [EXAM SIMULATION: Full practice exam]
          RULES: closed-terminal-help, open-book (this guide + official
          argo-project/kubernetes.io docs only — mirrors Ch23.1's real
          exam rule), one 60-minute clock for all 10 tasks, no peeking at
          "IF STUCK" hints from 25.1-25.4 until time is up. Task mix is
          weighted toward the two highest-value domains, matching the
          real blueprint (Workflows 36% + Argo CD 34% = 70% of the exam):

          ┌────┬─────────────┬──────┬──────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
          │ #  │ Domain      │ Time │ Task                                   │ ✅ Self-check command               │
          ├────┼─────────────┼──────┼──────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
          │ 1  │ Argo CD     │ 6 min│ Create Application easypj-staging     │ argocd app get easypj-staging      │
          │    │             │      │ (path overlays/staging), MANUAL sync  │ → SYNC=OutOfSync until you         │
          │    │             │      │ policy (not automated).               │ manually `argocd app sync`.        │
          │ 2  │ Argo CD     │ 6 min│ Restrict it via an AppProject to ONE   │ argocd proj get <project> → 1      │
          │    │             │      │ sourceRepo + 1 destination namespace. │ sourceRepo, 1 destination.         │
          │ 3  │ Argo CD     │ 5 min│ Diagnose: an app shows OutOfSync right │ argocd app diff <name> → no diff.  │
          │    │             │      │ after a successful sync — find + fix. │                                     │
          │ 4  │ Workflows   │ 7 min│ 2-step DAG (A→B); B only if A          │ argo get <wf> → task A node shows  │
          │    │             │      │ succeeds; A has retryStrategy limit 2.│ retries metadata.                  │
          │ 5  │ Workflows   │ 7 min│ Convert that template into a          │ argo cron list -n argo → schedule  │
          │    │             │      │ CronWorkflow, nightly at 02:00.       │ + next-run time shown.             │
          │ 6  │ Workflows   │ 6 min│ Pass task A's output as task B's      │ argo logs <wf> -c task-b → shows   │
          │    │             │      │ input parameter.                      │ A's value consumed correctly.      │
          │ 7  │ Workflows   │ 6 min│ Debug a Workflow stuck Pending; find  │ argo get <wf> → transitions to     │
          │    │             │      │ + fix the real cause.                 │ Running/Succeeded.                 │
          │ 8  │ Rollouts    │ 6 min│ Deployment→Rollout, 3-step canary     │ kubectl argo rollouts status <n>   │
          │    │             │      │ (20→50→100%), NO AnalysisTemplate —   │ → pauses at each step until        │
          │    │             │      │ manual promotion only.                │ `promote`.                          │
          │ 9  │ Rollouts    │ 6 min│ Add an AnalysisTemplate that WILL     │ kubectl argo rollouts get rollout  │
          │    │             │      │ fail on a bad metric; prove auto-     │ <n> → Degraded, stable RS intact.  │
          │    │             │      │ abort by intentionally triggering it. │                                     │
          │ 10 │ Events      │ 5 min│ Calendar EventSource (not Webhook)    │ argo list -n argo → a new Workflow │
          │    │             │      │ every 5 min → Sensor → trivial        │ appears ~every 5 min, unprompted.  │
          │    │             │      │ Workflow.                             │                                     │
          └────┴─────────────┴──────┴──────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘
          (6+6+5 + 7+7+6+6 + 6+6 + 5 = 60 minutes exactly.)

          SCORING RUBRIC — self-graded, be honest, 1 point per task whose
          ✅ self-check command actually returns the expected state:
            9-10 / 10 (90-100%) → Exam-ready with margin. Book it.
            8 / 10 (80%)        → At/above CAPA's real ~75% pass bar.
                                   Ready — review your 2 misses first.
            6-7 / 10 (60-70%)   → Close. Redo the specific 25.1-25.4 lab
                                   matching each miss, then re-run 25.5
                                   once, fresh, before booking.
            ≤5 / 10 (≤50%)      → Not yet. Return to the matching PART
                                   (2/3/4/5), not just the lab — a
                                   knowledge gap, not a lab-mechanics gap,
                                   is almost always the real cause.


    ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     CLOSING THE GUIDE
    ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

    [H] KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑 — THE WHOLE GUIDE, PART BY PART
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    - Part 1 · Foundation (Ch1-3): GitOps = Git as the single source of
      truth + a controller that continuously reconciles cluster state to
      match it. You built a real cluster and a real easypj image before
      installing a single Argo tool.
    - Part 2 · Argo CD (Ch4-8, 34% of the exam): the Application +
      AppProject + ApplicationSet trio IS the GitOps engine — a controller
      diffing Git against the cluster and syncing (or refusing to, per
      AppProject RBAC) on a poll or a webhook.
    - Part 3 · Argo Workflows (Ch9-13, 36% — the single highest-weighted
      domain): Pods orchestrated as DAG/Steps, reusable via
      WorkflowTemplate, scheduled via CronWorkflow. This is where easypj's
      CI (test → build → push → update GitOps repo) actually lives.
    - Part 4 · Argo Rollouts (Ch14-16, 18%): progressive delivery replaces
      "replace every Pod at once" with canary/blue-green promotion gated
      by an AnalysisTemplate reading real metrics, with automatic
      rollback built in, not bolted on.
    - Part 5 · Argo Events (Ch17-19, 12%): EventSource → EventBus (NATS)
      → Sensor → Trigger turns "someone has to kick off the pipeline"
      into "the pipeline kicks itself off."
    - Part 6 · easypj in Production (Ch20-22, Beyond CAPA): all four
      tools chained into ONE pipeline (push → Events → Workflows → CD →
      Rollouts), plus the hardening/security/observability layer real
      production needs and the exam doesn't test.
    - Part 7 · CAPA Exam Preparation (Ch23-25, this Part): turned all of
      the above into exam reflexes — strategy (Ch23), 35 cross-domain
      drill questions (Ch24), and this chapter's labs + simulation.
    🎯 Exam relevance: 100% — Workflows 36 + Argo CD 34 + Rollouts 18 +
       Events 12 = the entire graded blueprint, plus the Beyond-CAPA layer
       that makes you useful on day one of a real GitOps job.

    [I] FINAL EXAM-DAY GOTCHAS ⚠️ — one per domain, highest-value first
    ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    1. Argo CD: apiVersion must be argoproj.io/v1alpha1; Applications
       must live in the argocd namespace or they're invisible, not erred.
    2. Workflows: DAG dependency failures do NOT retry by default —
       retryStrategy must be set explicitly per-step.
    3. Rollouts: forgetting to point BOTH the stable and canary Service
       selectors correctly breaks traffic-split silently.
    4. Events: EventSource/Sensor dependency names are exact-string,
       case-sensitive matches — the #1 "Sensor never fires" cause.
    5. General: the real CAPA exam is closed-terminal, open-book,
       multiple-choice only. Practice READING a YAML/CLI-output snippet
       and picking the right answer fast — not just running commands.

    [J] WHERE TO GO FROM HERE 🚀
    ──────────────────────────────
    BOOKING THE REAL EXAM (verified against the official Linux Foundation
    certification page): CAPA is purchased directly at
    training.linuxfoundation.org — $250 exam-only (or $299 bundled with
    the LFS256 course, or $495 with a THRIVE-ONE annual subscription); no
    prerequisites. Once purchased, you have a 12-month window to schedule
    your remote-proctored session. The exam itself: 90 minutes, 60
    multiple-choice questions, Workflows 36% / Argo CD 34% / Rollouts 18%
    / Events 12% — exactly the split this guide has used throughout. One
    free retake is included. Once passed, the certification is valid for
    2 years.

    THE MOMENT YOU PASS: your two-year clock starts. Calendar-remind
    yourself ~3 months before expiry to recertify or move up.

    NATURAL NEXT CERTIFICATIONS: Certified GitOps Associate (CGOA)
    generalizes what Part 2 taught beyond just Argo CD to GitOps
    principles broadly. Certified Kubernetes Administrator (CKA) turns
    Chapter 2's refresher into full cluster administration depth. If this
    guide lives alongside sibling guides in the same series (CKA, CKAD,
    CKS, Istio, Helm, Backstage, Docker, K8s_cluster) — that's not a
    coincidence; pick up whichever one matches your next role's stack.

    CAREER PATHS THIS UNLOCKS: Platform Engineer, DevOps Engineer, GitOps
    Engineer, and SRE roles that name-check "Argo CD" or "GitOps" as a
    requirement. Day-to-day, you're now the engineer who can explain WHY
    a Deployment should become a Rollout, or why a hand-run Jenkins job
    should become a Workflow — not just how to click the buttons.

    KEEP EASYPJ ALIVE: the single best way to not lose this is to keep
    operating the SAME easypj cluster for a few more weeks past the exam
    — add a 5th EventSource type, try Blue-Green instead of Canary, wire
    in real alerting on top of Chapter 16's Prometheus setup. A guide
    ends; a real GitOps practice doesn't.

    PROSE RECAP: Chapter 1 opened this guide by asking "what is GitOps,
    and why does Argo exist?" Twenty-five chapters and one Django job
    board later, that question has a hands-on answer: easypj/jobpost now
    lives entirely as Git state, watched by four cooperating controllers
    (Argo CD, Workflows, Rollouts, Events) that build it, deploy it,
    promote it safely, and react to it — with no human running a manual
    kubectl apply in production. Whatever you do next — book the exam,
    open a sibling guide, or just keep pushing commits to easypj — you're
    not starting from zero anymore. That's the whole point.

Appendix F: easypj/jobpost — Complete Manifests (Your CAPA Reference)
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    ALL YAML files needed to deploy easypj/jobpost Django app
    using every Argo tool. Use as CAPA exam reference!
    ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    F.1  Dockerfile — Multi-stage build for easypj
         python:3.12-slim → pip install -r requirements.txt →
         copy code → collectstatic → gunicorn on port 8000
         [Full annotated Dockerfile in Chapter 2.6]

    F.2  K8s Base Manifests
         deployment.yaml: image easypj:v1.0, port 8000, liveness
         probe /health/, readiness probe /ready/, resources limits
         service.yaml: ClusterIP port 80→8000, selector app=easypj
         configmap.yaml: DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE, DB_HOST, DEBUG
         secret.yaml: DB_PASSWORD (base64), SECRET_KEY (base64)
         namespace.yaml: easypj-dev, easypj-staging, easypj-prod

         ⚠️ THE MISSING PIECE — DATABASE_URL points at SOMETHING; here's
         what, for real, in-cluster (dev/staging labs — prod should use
         a managed RDS-equivalent instead, per Ch 22.5's DR reasoning):
           postgres-secret.yaml: kind: Secret, data: {POSTGRES_PASSWORD:
             <base64>, DATABASE_URL: <base64 of "postgresql://easypj:
             <pw>@easypj-postgres:5432/easypj_db">}
           postgres-statefulset.yaml: kind: StatefulSet (NOT Deployment
             — a database needs a stable identity + stable storage,
             Ch 11.4's static-PVC reasoning applies to the DB itself);
             image postgres:16-alpine; envFrom the Secret above;
             volumeClaimTemplates: [{name: data, accessModes:
             [ReadWriteOnce], resources: {requests: {storage: 5Gi}}}];
             volumeMounts: [{name: data, mountPath: /var/lib/postgresql/
             data}]
           postgres-service.yaml: kind: Service, ClusterIP: None
             (headless — required for a StatefulSet's stable per-pod
             DNS, easypj-postgres-0.easypj-postgres.<ns>.svc), port 5432
         easypj's own deployment.yaml then references DATABASE_URL via
         `envFrom: [{secretRef: {name: postgres-secret}}]` — the SAME
         Secret, not a duplicated copy of the connection string.

    F.3  Kustomize Layout (base + overlays)
         base/ → common resources; overlays/dev/ → 1 replica;
         overlays/staging/ → 2 replicas; overlays/prod/ → 5 replicas
         + resource limits (CPU: 500m-2000m, Mem: 512Mi-2Gi)
         + configmap-patch.yaml per environment

    F.4  Argo CD Application YAMLs (3 files)
         easypj-dev.yaml → source path overlays/dev, dest ns easypj-dev
         easypj-staging.yaml → source path overlays/staging
         easypj-prod.yaml → source path overlays/prod
         All with: syncPolicy.automated.prune=true, selfHeal=true

    F.5  Argo CD ApplicationSet (1 file → generates 3 Applications)
         Generator: List [dev, staging, prod]
         Template: source path overlays/{{env}}, dest ns easypj-{{env}}
         Matrix option: env × region = 6+ Applications

    F.6  Argo Workflows — CI Pipeline WorkflowTemplate
         DAG: clone easypj → parallel(unit tests, lint, SAST) →
         docker build -t registry.io/easypj:{{version}} →
         docker push → update GitOps repo (commit new image tag)
         Artifacts: test-results.json, lint-report.txt

    F.7  Argo Workflows — DB Migration CronWorkflow
         schedule: "0 2 * * *" (2 AM nightly)
         Steps: pg_dump backup → python manage.py migrate →
         python manage.py check --deploy → Slack notification
         concurrencyPolicy: Forbid, historyLimit: 7

    F.8  Argo Rollouts — Canary Rollout + AnalysisTemplate
         Rollout: replicas 5, canary steps: 10%(30s)→25%(2m)→
         50%(5m)→100%. AnalysisTemplate: query Prometheus for
         sum(rate(http_errors{app="easypj"}[1m])) < 0.01 (write as
         result[0] < 0.01 per Ch 16.3's vector gotcha)
         canaryService: easypj-canary, stableService: easypj-stable
         ⚠️ NOTE: activeService/previewService are strategy.blueGreen
         fields ONLY (Ch 14.3/16.1) — a canary Rollout uses
         canaryService/stableService instead (Ch 22.4's corrected
         troubleshooting entry). Don't mix the two strategies' fields.

    F.9  Argo Events — Webhook EventSource + Sensor
         EventSource: GitHub webhook, port 12000, POST /push
         Sensor: filter on push to main, extract commit SHA,
         trigger CI WorkflowTemplate with parameters:
         repo_url=github.com/team-easypj/easypj, commit_sha={{sha}}

    F.10 App-of-Apps Bootstrap YAML
         Single Application: source path argo-apps/, syncs
         all F.4-F.9 files. ONE kubectl apply → entire easypj
         infrastructure running on Argo CD + Workflows + Events!

Appendix G: Common Errors and Solutions
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  TOP 15 CAPA ERRORS & FIXES                                │
    │                                                            │
    │  1. "repo not found" → Check repoURL is correct + accessible│
    │  2. "namespace not found" → syncOptions: CreateNamespace=true│
    │  3. "permission denied" → Check AppProject destinations    │
    │  4. "invalid spec" → Run: kubectl explain application.spec│
    │  5. "out of sync" stuck → argocd app sync --force <name>  │
    │  6. Workflow pod OOMKilled → Set memory limits in template│
    │  7. DAG task never starts → Check depends: field spelling  │
    │  8. Artifact "no such file" → Verify artifact repo config │
    │  9. Rollout not progressing → activeService selector wrong │
    │  10. AnalysisRun "unknown" → Prometheus URL incorrect     │
    │  11. Sensor "not ready" → EventBus pod not running        │
    │  12. Webhook returns 404 → EventSource port-forward wrong │
    │  13. "context deadline exceeded" → Check repo-server logs │
    │  14. ApplicationSet not generating → Check generator spec │
    │  15. Sync fails on CRDs → May need ServerSideApply=true   │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Appendix I: Argo Glossary — All Terminology Defined
    [GLOSSARY TABLE]
    ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  ARGO GLOSSARY                                             │
    │                                                            │
    │  Application — Argo CD CRD linking Git repo to K8s cluster │
    │  AppProject — Groups Applications + enforces restrictions  │
    │  ApplicationSet — Template that generates Applications     │
    │  Sync — Process of making cluster state match Git state    │
    │  Drift — When actual cluster state ≠ desired Git state     │
    │  Prune — Delete cluster resources not in Git               │
    │  Self-Heal — Automatically correct drift                   │
    │  Workflow — DAG or Steps-based container execution         │
    │  WorkflowTemplate — Reusable workflow blueprint            │
    │  DAG — Directed Acyclic Graph (tasks + dependencies)       │
    │  Artifact — File passed between workflow steps             │
    │  CronWorkflow — Scheduled workflow (cron expression)       │
    │  Rollout — Argo's replacement for K8s Deployment           │
    │  Blue-Green — Instant cutover deployment strategy          │
    │  Canary — Gradual traffic shift deployment strategy        │
    │  AnalysisTemplate — Metric-based promotion criteria        │
    │  EventSource — Listener for external events                │
    │  Sensor — Filters + transforms + triggers on events        │
    │  EventBus — Internal NATS streaming transport              │
    │  GitOps — Git as single source of truth for operations     │
    │  Kustomize — K8s-native YAML patching (overlay model)      │
    │  Helm — Package manager for K8s (Go template charts)       │
    │  CRD — Custom Resource Definition (extends K8s API)        │
    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
